# Cyberpunk Convai: TODO and captain's log

The single working document for development: open items first, then a dated log, then the standing facts every session needs. It replaces `HANDOFF_README.md` (removed October 5; still in git history). Earlier code and docs were written by a weaker model, so verify claims against code and data before relying on them.

## Open items

Last consolidated 2026-10-11 (closed items are listed in the Captain's log entry "2026-10-11: open items consolidated"). Install state: the game folder was synced from HEAD at the end of 2026-10-10 (see the log entry "end of day"): all `mod/` Lua except `diagnostics.lua` (modified, uncommitted), the bridge, `convai_gestures.archive` `a71aa51e` and `convai_facial.archive` `5d8750c5`. So every live check below runs on what is installed unless it says otherwise. Test sessions: install by copying only the intended files; Lua needs CET reload, `bridge/` needs a bridge restart, redscript, plugins and archives need a game restart.

### Release schedule (decided 2026-10-10)
- **Main mod on Nexus: Sun 18 Oct 2026, about 11:00 UTC (16:30 IST).** That's two days before *Cyberpunk: Edgerunners 2* drops all 10 episodes on Netflix (Tue 20 Oct, likely about 07:00 UTC). This gives YouTubers time to include it in post-show mod videos and lets it build trending endorsements before the returning-player wave. Release-blocking bugs must be fixed by then.
- **Add-on mod 1: Tue 20 Oct (show day).** A separate Nexus page that lists the main mod as a requirement, for a fresh spot on the New list.
- **Add-on mod 2: Sun 25 Oct, about 11:00 UTC.** The first weekend after the binge, when returning players peak.
- Add-on candidates (both TODO; order not chosen):
  - **Talking gun, Skippy:** talks while V carries it; two personalities.
  - **Talking car, Delamain-style:** talks while V drives; decide which cars count.
  - Both are voice-only (no face, lip sync, gestures or seating) and reuse the bridge, voices and memory.
- Players' conversations run on the project's Convai account, limited to 100 turns per player per local day and 12 a minute (2026-10-11; `DAILY_LIMIT` in `bridge/shared-key-guard.mjs`, `docs/OWN_CONVAI_KEY.md`, "Daily limit on the shared plan"). Raise it there if usage allows.
- Timing facts:
  - Cyberpunk on Nexus gets about 35 new mods a day, so a new mod stays on page 1 of New for about 12 to 14 hours.
  - Nexus trending ranks mods first published within the game's trending window by endorsements.
  - Steam peaks run about 13:00 to 14:00 UTC, and Sundays run about 10 to 20% above weekday peaks.

### Release blockers (before Sun 18 Oct 11:00 UTC)
- **Package and test a release build in the real game.** The pipeline (`docs/PACKAGING.md`, `.github/workflows/release.yml`, `packaging/build-release.mjs`) passes its dry run and clean-install test locally but has never been seen in the game from a package. Remove the development launcher first, or two services start. Check: the auto-start supervisor on first launch (launcher log in the plugin folder; talk without starting the bridge by hand; the bridge stops with the game), pasting a free Convai key on a clean install, Help > Generate report. Releases publish directly in `alystria-ai/cyberpunk-convai` with `GITHUB_TOKEN` (private until the user makes the repo public, which players need). Version scheme 0.N from 2026-10-11 (0.2, tag `v0.2`; hotfix 0.N.M; the first build was 2077.1 and stays). Secrets `CONVAI_API_KEY` and `HF_TOKEN` are set. Licence: `LICENSE` (Cyberpunk Convai Use License 1.1) and `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. Status 2026-10-13: the five-package dry run (from commit `268493c`) and the clean-install test pass locally (`--local-ai`, 26 of 26 checks; the OmniVoice pack's install check was not run because it loads the GPU, and the native plugin inspector `convai_plugin_host.exe` is only built in CI). Sizes: Scripts 1.95 MB, Runtime 1.14 GB, OmniVoice pack 6.37 GB in 4 parts, Local AI standard 5.31 GB in 5 parts, Local AI lite 1.43 GB. In-game checks needed from a package: the native plugin loads from `red4ext/plugins/CyberpunkConvaiNative/` (RED4ext log) with `runtime/native-off.txt` keeping actuator, vehicles, chauffeur, passage and faces off, and the mod still runs with the DLL deleted; deleting a line of `native-off.txt` turns that system on within a second; the Local AI row shows Warming up then Ready after the standard pack's first-start join (about a minute); the Mistral voice starts quickly on a machine without ffmpeg (the bridge log shows the prepared MP3 as cached).
- **Refresh the release data.** Run `node packaging/Refresh-ReleaseData.mjs` before each release so `packaging/release-data` carries the 2026-10-10 archives (gesture `a71aa51e`, facial `5d8750c5`) and Alt Cunningham's rows; the working copy shows `packaging/release-data/archives/convai_gestures.archive` modified and uncommitted. Done 2026-10-13: nothing needed refreshing (the mappings and archives in `packaging/release-data` are current). Do not run it blindly: its run copied the untested gestures archive `36f54d4f` over the tested `6966d508`; that run was reverted.
- **Ship the trained models in the package** (user decision 2026-10-07: players never train anything; CD Projekt allows game material in free fan content). The build takes models from the HF bucket `final/` (`packaging/fetch-models.py`, every file checked against `final/MANIFEST.json`). Confirm the package holds the lip-sync model and text stage (`face-model.{json,bin}`, `face-text.{json,bin}`, `face-lexicon.txt.gz`, `face-lexicon-NOTICE.txt`), the Pocket student v2 + decoder v2 export (`runtime/pocket-student-v2-s2000-ema-decv2`), the searched reference clips and the saved configs; Omni weights go in the Omni pack. `final/` was last refreshed 2026-10-08 (123 profiles, 496 clips, plus the 402 archetype clips); confirm Alt Cunningham's voice references and config entries (added 2026-10-10) and the output of the remaining-voice search, if it lands, are in it. Confirmed 2026-10-13: the Runtime zip holds the lip-sync model and text stage, the Pocket export with both receipts, the Moonshine model, the Pocket reference clips and config for all 256 voices (`8ug8ear` and `jake-estevez` included: their clips and configs are in the bucket and `final/MANIFEST.json` since 2026-10-13) and the pre-built Mistral references; the OmniVoice pack holds the long and short clips and configs. The build's SHA-256 checks pass.
- **Build the optional Omni pack** (`bridge/omni-pack.mjs` detects it; contents in `docs/PACKAGING.md`; the workflow's second job builds it and it arrives a while after the Runtime package). Without it players get Pocket and the cloud voice. Dry-run build 2026-10-13: 4 parts, 6.37 GB, with the 215 Mistral reference slots that differ from the Runtime's.
- **Native plugin, Local AI packs and Mistral references in the package (packaging done 2026-10-13, live checks above still to run).** `CyberpunkConvaiNative.dll` ships in the Runtime package (built from `native/core` locally and in CI; `--no-native` leaves it out) with `runtime/native-off.txt` (`actuator`, `vehicles`, `chauffeur`, `passage`, `faces`; a missing file means all on). Two separate downloads, Local AI standard (Gemma 4 E4B) and lite (Qwen3.5-2B), each with the llama.cpp b11560 Vulkan server (MIT) and the granite embedding model; the models are Apache-2.0; licences in `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`. The Mistral voice's reference clips are pre-encoded as 24 kHz 40 kbit/s MP3 (reference version 3, commit `4931270`) in `voices/prepared/`: 256 voices, 10.64 MB in the Runtime package and 215 voices, 8.99 MB in the OmniVoice pack (19.6 MB in all). Needs ffmpeg at build time (the workflow installs it). The Nexus requirements list RED4ext. `docs/PACKAGING.md` has the details.
- **Local AI: a 6 GB card never starts the standard model.** The start rule needs free video memory of the model file plus 1200 MiB (about 6 GB) although the model measures about 3.1 GB in use, so a 6 GB card always gets lite or the cloud text. Left as designed and stated in the README; lower the margin in `bridge/local-llm-server.mjs` only after a measured test on a 6 or 8 GB card.
- **Release-blocking bugs** found by the live checks below must be fixed by then.

### Requested 2026-10-12 (user's list for this week)
- **Performance first: move hot Lua work to C++** (started 2026-10-12; FPS is the top priority). Profile what the CET Lua does per tick or per event (the 10 Hz companion tick, look/aim queries, movement and lift watchers, polling of bridge files, notices, gesture and face drivers). Move the measured hot paths into a native plugin. Also improve code organisation and anything else that helps performance. Don't add per-frame main-thread work.
  - **Full C++ port, approved by the user 2026-10-12** ("any performance gain is welcome"; they can test in game quickly). It is being done on the `native-core` branch as a RED4ext plugin (`native/core/`), system by system: file IO thread, knowledge collectors and payload, companion tick and actuator, gaze, faces, seating, vehicles. Every system keeps its Lua fallback and a kill switch for A/B FPS checks. Systems that pass an in-game test by Fri 16 Oct ship in 0.2; the rest go in 0.3. Plan: `docs/NATIVE_REWRITE_PLAN.md`. Players will need RED4ext.
  - **Lua wins on `main`, status 2026-10-12** (`docs/PERFORMANCE_NATIVE_PLAN.md`; committed and unit-tested, NOT installed or live). The 2026-10-12 capture measured `onUpdate` at 179 ms/s (3.7 ms of each 20.7 ms frame); the knowledge collectors were 69 ms/s of it, file IO 32, init.lua's own work about 34.
    - Done: the idle face sleeps between blinks (`cf1cdda`); the collectors rest between scans and rescan on a journal change or before a turn (`843a527`, `01c34de`); turns name the knowledge parts by revision instead of carrying about 800 KB (`4b9ff83`, phone `5c4d870`); phone chat makes no per-frame JSON round trip and reads the reset journal every 5 s with no chat open (`cb96750`); the developer mailboxes are read every 4 s with Developer mode off (`471de62`); the 2 s lease heartbeats write one per frame instead of about 25 ms in one frame (`01d93c2`); four native calls per frame fewer (`4dcc8e4`); hook rows in the probe (`49f89b8`) and init.lua segment timers (`9057812`).
    - Next, after the capture below: fix what the `init:` rows show; invitations, relationship, phone style and auto-connect per-frame work; lazy chatter Overrides and `enabled`-first loot hooks; buffered `log()`. The C++ file layer (`31d7ea5`) covers the remaining reads and writes once the plugin is installed.
- **Three conversation options for players** (a fourth comes later):
  1. **Default: the shared Convai key plus local voice.** Convai covers memory, STT and LLM. It has no voice cloning yet; the user is working on that. When a player reaches the daily limit, offer options 2 and 3.
  2. **Fully local.** An on-device LLM, STT, TTS, embedding model and vector store. The aim is a local experience that rivals Convai plus our local voice. Choose the LLM, embedding model and vector store for speed and low VRAM, since they share the GPU with the game.
  3. **Fully free cloud.** Mistral as the LLM (generous free limits). Compare its latency with Groq, which may be faster; both keys are in the keys file. Inworld for voice cloning: about 70 free minutes a month. Test Inworld sparingly, only to confirm it works. STT and the vector store can stay local. If an online embedding model plus vector DB has good free limits for players, name the keys to add to the keys file for testing. The keys file already has Cohere.
  - **Multilingual** for options 2 and 3 would be good, but it's not a hard requirement.

Its cloud voice is Voxtral (same free Mistral key, no monthly cap found, cloned from the OmniVoice long references). Groq is offered as an optional faster text provider (player adds a Groq key). Each line is one check on what is installed. Detail: the doc or log entry named.
- **Holocall (2026-10-14, branch `holocall`; install all committed `mod/` files, the bridge and `r6/scripts/CyberpunkConvai/*.reds`, game restart):** Phone > Chat, select a mod character, press the Holocall action (or Call inside her chat). After the phone closes, the call window at the top left should ring, then connect with her name and portrait. Talk by push-to-talk and by typed text: she answers in her mod voice as someone on the phone, with a bottom subtitle. Tap the phone button to hang up. Also check: combat ends the call; a quest call during a mod call wins and shows normally; no FPS change. The CET log should show `[Holocall] requested`, `state=dialing ... mode=audio hud=ok`, `state=connected ... window=audiocall`, `Holocall conversation: profile=...`, then `state=ended ... reason=hangup`. Optional: turn on Development > Live holocall picture (experimental) and look for `holo ready` or `holo failed ... fallback=audio`. Detail: docs/HOLOCALL.md.

Expect V's line first, in V's voice, then Judy; log `[VVoice] ... result=played` with marks. Check: (a) V's gender picks the right clip for this V (`Turn context:` line shows `male`/`female`; the save is Male/Male, so also test a V whose body and voice differ if one exists, `GetResolvedGenderName` may follow the body); (b) the clip sounds like V (listen to `assets/v-voice/v-male.wav` and `v-female.wav` first, they are not approved); (c) V's volume follows the game's Dialogue volume; (d) Judy's audio starts right after V ends, with her subtitle and face in time, and never over V; (e) the first line after a cold start may be dropped (`reason=slower-than-reply`), the next ones should play; (f) a short reply from a fast model: V is dropped or finishes first, the reply is never late (compare `bridge-latency.json` with the setting off); (g) push-to-talk and F9 group voice never replay V; (h) Mistral and Inworld voices: V plays and no Pocket model starts (`local-voice-state.json`, process list); (i) OmniVoice: V is rendered whole, check the GPU is not blocked (reply voice latency); (j) choosing the Convai · Cloud voice with the setting on shows the one notice, and turning it on beside Convai shows the refusal.

Expect V's line first, in V's voice, then Judy; log `[VVoice] ... result=played` with marks. Check: (a) V's gender picks the right clip for this V (`Turn context:` line shows `male`/`female`; the save is Male/Male, so also test a V whose body and voice differ if one exists, `GetResolvedGenderName` may follow the body); (b) the clip sounds like V (re-picked 2026-10-14 by the automatic picker, branch `v-voice-clips`; listen to the files in `Temp\claude\v-voice-candidates` first, they are not approved); (c) V's volume follows the game's Dialogue volume; (d) Judy's audio starts right after V ends, with her subtitle and face in time, and never over V; (e) the first line after a cold start may be dropped (`reason=slower-than-reply`), the next ones should play; (f) a short reply from a fast model: V is dropped or finishes first, the reply is never late (compare `bridge-latency.json` with the setting off); (g) push-to-talk and F9 group voice never replay V; (h) Mistral and Inworld voices: V plays and no Pocket model starts (`local-voice-state.json`, process list); (i) OmniVoice: V is rendered whole, check the GPU is not blocked (reply voice latency); (j) choosing the Convai · Cloud voice with the setting on shows the one notice, and turning it on beside Convai shows the refusal.

Judy must follow up stairs and stop at the usual distance. If a `PARITY MISMATCH <field>` line appears, that field is the cause of the stairs/stop-short bug (bisect with `actuator.life` / `actuator.sense` / `actuator.state` lines in `native-off.txt`).
- **Native core, the 2026-10-13 late fixes (DLL plus CET reload):** probe.log should show `Game access: GameInstance.GetMountingFacility resolved as ...` (and `GetMappinSystem`, `GetScriptableSystemsContainer`, `GetSceneSystem` when used) with no `no function GameInstance.X found`, then `vehicles=native` instead of `vehicles=lua (mounting keeps answering unavailable)`; `Game access: static NPCPuppet.IsInCombat resolved as IsInCombat;ScriptedPuppet`, `Actuator: combat flag read through ...`, `Actuator: capabilities 0x7f (all)` and `actuator parity proven over N senses (M with the companion walking); combat flag read by the plugin, compared N times`. In a car with Judy: look at her and read `Companion recovery seated-gaze` lines in probe.log; they name the gate that held her look (`No selected seated attention`, `Wait for an actual seat in V's current vehicle`, `Driver watches the road`, `The car is too fast for a seated look`) or show `seated-attend` with a style. If `seated-attend` is logged and her eyes still do not meet V's, the game's vehicle animation is ignoring the look-at: say so with the line. A/B the actuator with perf-probe rows `tickNative.*` and `companions.update` at equal FPS (line `actuator` in `runtime/native-off.txt`).

Ride with a companion, ask her to drive you to a map pin and to a Quest objective (retarget, stop, brake, leave the car), and board an elevator; `probe.log` should show `RTTI: ConvaiNativeVehicles|Chauffeur|Passage registered N natives`, `vehicles/chauffeur/passage=native` and the `matched the Lua 8 times, now native` lines, and no `Native core: <name> MISMATCH` line with the line `<name>` in `runtime/native-verify.txt`. Look for `Vehicles: no member ...`, `no static function ...` or `global function ... not found` warnings: each names a game function the plugin could not resolve (they say what the class has under that name), and `ConvaiNativeChauffeur.Stats()` `sampleFailedSections` should stay 0. A/B each by adding the line `<name>` to `runtime/native-off.txt` mid-session against perf-probe rows `vehicles.inspect`, `vehicles.mounting`, `chauffeur.sample`, `chauffeur.inspectDrive`, `passage.carSpot`. Read `event:onUpdate`, the `init:*` rows, `worldKnowledge.update` and `journalHistory.update` (near zero between scans), `over25ms`/`frameMaxMs` (should drop: heartbeats no longer share a frame), `hook:*` with `hooksWrapped` above 0, and `json.encode` max during a turn. - **Right-now block and stranger gate (2026-10-12; bridge restart plus CET reload):** talk to a spawned companion by text and by voice: `probe.log` has `Turn context: HH:MM(native) District/Area <gender> <lifepath> vehicle=<bool> party=N` (one per real read, at most every 5 s) and bridge stderr `[TurnContext] ... Turn context: N facts, M chars`. Compare the logged clock with the HUD clock (if the source says `seconds` instead of `native`, the GameTime.Hours/Minutes calls failed; the hour should still match), the district with the map, `party` with who stands within 15 m, `vehicle` inside and outside a car, gender and lifepath with V. Ask a companion "what time is it?" and "where are we?": the answer follows the block, in her own words and not announced. Then talk to a pedestrian or vendor: they know the time and place, may remark on V's clothes or a drawn weapon, and know nothing of V's tracked quest, gear list or lifepath (`runtime/last-input.json` still carries the full journal; the gate is on the bridge). Mistral: the block is the first lines of the live context. Weather, health, heat and money: see the next line.
- **Weather, health, heat and money in the right-now block (2026-10-13, branch `dynamic-context-2`; install all committed `mod/` files and the bridge, CET reload plus bridge restart):** every call is an unverified CET name, so read `probe.log` after one text turn to a spawned companion: the `Turn context:` line gains `weather=<kind>(<state text>|<rain>|<getters that answered>) health=<band>(<percent>%|<read>) heat=<0-5>(<getter>) money=<band>(<eddies>)`. Each field that is missing means that call failed: a separate line says `Turn context: <weather|health|heat|money> read failed (<error>)` or `answered nothing` or `not recognised (state=... rain=...)`, once per kind (after three straight errors a read is skipped until the next reload or save change). Check against the game, each in its own situation: (1) weather: stand in rain, clear sky, fog or a toxic-rain district (Pacifica, Badlands dust storm) and compare the kind (clear, cloudy, rain, heavy-rain, toxic-rain, fog, smog, sandstorm); if only `GetRainIntensityType` answers, only rain shows (no clear/cloudy) and the failed state names are in the log, so send them; (2) health: take damage and read `health=hurt(<percent>%)` against the health bar (hurt 30 to 59 percent, badly-hurt below 30); (3) heat: get one star, then three and five, `heat=1`, `3`, `5`; stage 0 shows nothing; (4) money: `money=<band>(<eddies>)` against the inventory number (broke below 250, low below 2,500, ok below 50,000, comfortable below 250,000, rich above). Then ask the companion things like "how are you feeling?" while hurt, "are we in trouble?" while wanted: she answers in her own words and never announces the block. A pedestrian knows the weather and may say V looks hurt, never the heat or money. A Phone Chat contact knows the weather where V is (like the time) and nothing else of the four. Cadence: read only at turn time, cached 5 s, never per frame; confirm `probe.log` shows one `Turn context:` line per turn window and no FPS change. If a getter name is wrong the log says which and a one-line fix goes into the candidate list of `nativeAdapter` in `mod/turn-context.lua`.

Text chat already works in any language.

  - Lip sync stays English-trained (audio-only, so it moves; visemes are English-shaped).

Also optional: decoder v2 retrained with a weaker anchor (5-10) might win back some of A's ECAPA gain if it stays artifact-free (a listening question).

### Dropped by the user
- Mixed voice providers in one group (and their cancellation).
- Voice cloning setting (a clip-count choice, 2026-10-06 request): dropped 2026-10-07; references are measured by duration and chosen per character, with no in-game reference-length or clip setting.

- Jackie constrained-reference variant: the user preferred the overnight best-length set.
- A new Pocket teacher/student run: not needed per the user (the v2 student already meets the plan criteria).
- World NPCs joining the party (2026-10-11): Follow V for pedestrians and other world characters is off; only companions the mod spawns are party members. Leave V's Party stays for them.
- Mod systems acting on game-placed characters (2026-10-11): the mod never touches a named, story or quest character the game placed, and for ordinary world NPCs only the talk path (and an auto-connect warm-up with no in-game effect) acts.
- Lip sync for vendors and ordinary world NPCs (2026-10-10). The only routes are an appearance hop, which flashes the outfit, or the heavier face graph on every vendor at spawn, which costs FPS. Companions and named characters keep full lip sync.
- Integration 2026-10-13 follow-ups: (a) the Inworld cloud voice's tail request is sent only after the head's voice is decided (the Voxtral tail is sent at once and held), so an Inworld two-part reply may start its second part a little later than a Voxtral one; measure it live before changing it. (b) Two tests are timing-flaky on this computer and pass alone: `bridge/local-stt-capture.test.mjs` ("browser capture: AudioWorklet chunks...": it runs the real recogniser, whose words vary: once "In his Mr. Coulter's manner", and sometimes no live partial arrives in time) and `packaging/runtime-bootstrap.test.mjs` ("a stop during imports is retried...": EPERM on a rename in the temp folder, Windows file locking). (c) The game copy still holds `mod/tactic-perception.lua`, which fight-crouch removed from the repo; nothing requires it any more, delete it with the next clean install (needs approval). (d) Everything merged on 2026-10-13 is unit-tested only; the live checks are listed in each branch's captain's-log entry.

## Captain's log

**2026-10-14 (branch `skippy-hologram`, from main 15e2d625; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT heard or seen in the game, game not touched): Skippy remarks that showed a subtitle but could not be heard; his voice from the gun; why the game's hologram text cannot carry our remarks.** Cause of the silent remarks, from the 2026-10-14 logs: the bridge cut remarks the game had already accepted. The reaction lease (`runtime/reaction-lease.json`) said `playing` only on the add-on tick after the game accepted the reply, and its deadline was floored to whole seconds (`os.time()`), so a short remark could look expired the moment it began. Example from probe.log: companion-kill played and was released in the same second, and the bridge logged `Continuous speech owner changed`. Separately, one refused or half-written lease read (Windows sharing, an atomic replace in flight) made `reactionCurrent` return false, and `tick()` aborted a playing turn. During play the lease is rewritten every second (fight-start was cut about 1 s in). Fixes: `canDeliver` writes `playbackStartedAt` at once (`mod/addons.lua`); the deadline adds a second for the rounding; `reactionCurrent` retries EPERM/EBUSY/EACCES/ENOENT and half-written JSON three times, 15 ms apart, and keeps a playing turn through up to 1 s of unreadable lease (`bridge/convai-bridge.mjs`). New `reactionLeaseProblem` (`bridge/battle-context.mjs`) names the reason. New log details: ` voice-stopped=<speech state> (<error>)` on `Remark released` when the voice ended early, and `(lease: <reason>)` on the bridge's `did not reach the player`. Voice position: new speaker option `voiceFrom` (`docs/ADDON_API.md`); Skippy passes his active weapon, so the browser voice takes its pan and distance from the gun (asked once per reply, dropped back to V if the gun is not within 3 m of the camera; the native Omni phrases still play on V). Hologram: the game's text beside the gun is a fixed onscreen string per voice line id (`vo_onscreens`, 19 entries, all mq007), drawn by something no script feeds. `Invisible` lines are rejected by both script subtitle controllers, so our text cannot be routed there and the subtitle fallback stays (detail and sources: `docs/addons/SKIPPY_RESEARCH.md` 1.4.1). Tests: `battle-lease` (retries, the bounded grace, refusal reasons), `addons-lua` (playing at once, the deadline, voice-stopped, voiceFrom, the init seam), `continuous-speech` (gun position, fallback, ordinary replies untouched), `loot-context` (adapter). Live check: Live checks list, "Skippy remarks heard and his voice from the gun".

**2026-10-14 (branch `v-voice-edges`, from main 39962c9f; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT heard or seen in the game, game not touched; bridge restart plus CET reload): V's line gets air at both ends; subtitle safety nets for a companion reply.** (1) `bridge/pcm-edges.mjs`: 160 ms lead-in, 15 ms fade-in, 40 ms fade-out, 320 ms silent tail around every V line (cause and details: `docs/V_VOICE.md` "Air at both ends"); the companion's own Pocket lines have the same tightness but are untouched (face and lip-sync are frame-exact). (2) Companion subtitle that stayed after the reply: the CET log was too old to show it, so the code paths were audited. Found and closed: a timed caption page had no timer of its own and relied only on `speech.lua` to end it (`mod/subtitles.lua` `TIMED_STALL`: 8 s without a driver sync clears it, a menu or a long hitch does not count); `speech.abandon` (world pause with a failed lease write) dropped the speech but left the page; a reply heard to its end whose state file then went stale or was cancelled was re-shown as a full written caption for its reading time (`mod/speech.lua` `continuousHeardAll`). The exact trigger in the live session is not proven. Live checks: type a line with V's voice on (both ends of V's line should sound natural, a short pause before the reply); after several replies including a slow Local AI one, the subtitle goes about 0.6 s after the voice ends.

**2026-10-14 (branch `mature-wordbank`, from main 2f6e180e; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): Mature word palette, cruder with the game's own slang.** One new line in the Mature layer (`PALETTE`, `bridge/mature-content.mjs`): "use words like these freely and often, in your own voice, and any others you like (a palette, not a checklist: vary it, never the same word at the end of every reply, only where it sounds natural)", then 20 plain crude words and 20 Night City slang words that are in the repo's lore data (choom, gonk, preem, nova, delta, flatline, chrome, wetware, meat, eddies, dollhouse, BD, braindance, jack in, plug in, corpo, ripperdoc, doll, joytoy; the full list is in `docs/MATURE_SAMPLES_V4_2026-10-14.md`). The suggestive level gets swears and slang only. HARD LIMITS, adults-only line, guard and closing sentence unchanged; off is byte for byte the old prompt (`--dump --layer off` of this branch and 2f6e180e: identical, 48 requests; the existing comparison tests pass). Cost: layer 491 to 641 tokens (Gemma tokenizer), 488 to 636 (Qwen3.5), about +150, 3% of the prompt. Eval (brief set, n=3, 72 replies per cell, Gemma-MiniFantasy and HauhauCS 4B, round 3 prompt against the palette): crude or explicit word in adult replies 18 to 32 (Gemma) and 18 to 27 (HauhauCS) of 36, in neutral replies 6 to 22 and 15 to 23 of 36; graphic 0 to 9 and 11 to 22 of 72; guard drops 0 and 1 (round 3: 0 and 1; the guard is untouched). A first palette with "choom, gonk, doll" at the head made Gemma end 15 of 72 replies ", choom" (a tic); the shipped one lists the vocatives last and says to vary, 7 of 72. HauhauCS misuses some slang (dollhouse as an insult, "preme" as a verb); no reply is a list of palette words (one flagged by the heuristic, read and fine). New tests in `bridge/mature-content.test.mjs` (palette only in the layer, only with Mature on, never for Mistral, Groq or the cloud fallback). The layer test ceilings moved from 500 to 650 tokens and the turn delta from 680 to 800. Open: user to read the samples and say whether to keep the vocative-last order; the sign-off tic on Gemma is halved, not gone. Not installed: install all committed `mod/` and bridge files, restart the bridge, to see it in the game.

**2026-10-14 (branch `skippy-line-ids`, from main 6f810eb5; unit-tested; NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): Skippy's mute claims only his own lines.** The generic rule "any unnamed `Invisible` line while he is held" is gone. `addons/skippy/line-ids.lua` is an exact allowlist of his 76 string ids (74 distinct mq007 lines in `gun_found`, `skippy_reactions`, `skippy_about_killing`, `skippy_return`, plus 2 cameo lines in `delamain_default`), read from the repo's scene index `scene-lines.jsonl` (live scene paths only; "New magazine detected" and the "Provocation." lines are in it). A line is claimed only if its id (`line.id.hash`, digits only, compared as strings) is listed or its speaker is named Skippy or `LocKey#46444`. If the id cannot be read the name rule alone applies, with one log line (`the id of a game subtitle line could not be read`); the first five id matches are logged (`a game line was recognised by its id`). Unverified live: that the hook's id equals the string id. Install `addons/skippy/{init.lua,line-ids.lua}`, CET reload.

**2026-10-14 (branch `v-voice-default-on`, from main 87438a3a; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT heard in the game, game not touched; needs a bridge restart plus a CET reload): V's voice for typed lines is on by default.** New installs and saved settings that never stored `vVoice` get on (`mod/manager.lua`); only an explicit false stays off. Removed the three Convai rules: the refusal when turning it on beside the Convai cloud voice, the auto-off notice when that voice is chosen, and the load-as-off pairing. Beside the Convai voice (which may be the default companion voice) the setting stays on and is inactive: the bridge plans no clone, so no V line, no renderer warm-up, no voice prepared (`bridge/v-voice.mjs` now checks the plan before the dialogue volume, so no false "volume not known" log either), and the Voices row shows one inline note, "Works with the Pocket, OmniVoice, Voxtral or Inworld voices." (`mod/mcm.lua`, appended to the row description only while the saved voice is Convai; the Characters sidebar Speech line lists V's voice only when it is active). Switching to a cloning voice works at once: the bridge reads the setting file on its poll, and the turn-context cache (5 s) is read again once when the volume becomes wanted (`mod/turn-context.lua`). New `manager.vVoiceActive()` keeps the game-side dialogue-volume read off while the voice cannot clone (`mod/init.lua`), so the default-on costs Convai users nothing per turn. Latency design, the no-local-model-for-cloud-voices rule and push-to-talk never replaying V are untouched. Strings: one description changed, one note added, two notices removed, in `localization/en.json` (regenerated, `--check` passes) and all 15 `mod/locales` (translated by a Haiku helper, not reviewed by a native speaker). Docs: `docs/V_VOICE.md`, `docs/MANAGER_UI.md`, `README.md`. Tests: `bridge/manager.test.mjs` (default, explicit false kept, Convai pairing loads as on, no refusal or notice, switch to Pocket active, row note), `bridge/v-voice.test.mjs` (on unless false, inactive beside Convai), `bridge/turn-context.test.mjs` (cache re-read).
**2026-10-14 (branch `addon-game-lines`, from main 87438a3a; unit-tested; NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched; install all committed `mod/` files, the bridge (restart) and both add-ons, then a CET reload): Skippy remarks that never played, the game's own Skippy and cab lines, and a marker for our lines.** Live report: "new message detected", "provocation" and beeps, no way to tell the game's lines from ours, the AI remarks seemingly not playing. probe.log showed almost every Skippy remark cancelled, not-delivered within the same second, or stale. Causes found: (1) **cancelled** = the add-on's own `vanillaSpoke`: any `Invisible` subtitle line while Skippy was held (the game's bark scene, name key `LocKey#46444`) cancelled whatever was waiting; (2) **not-delivered in the same second** = the bridge's local-AI pace: `LOCAL_REMARK_GAP_S` (20 s) is counted from the start of the previous remark on the local route, including the cancelled and stale ones, and a refusal (`addon-pace`) lost the moment; priority 5 (near death, fight end) was refused too, and so was the Delamain arrival; (3) **stale** = a cold or contended local model with no patience (Skippy's remarks had none, ttl 3 s). Fixes: the bridge no longer refuses priority 5, tells the game the spacing (`gap`) so it waits instead of asking and being refused, and gives the pace back for a remark that never reached the player (`remarkRefunded`, in a try/finally in `runAddonTurn`); every Skippy event now has patience by priority (5: 12 s, 4: 8 s, 3: 6 s, 2: 4 s) with `relevant` = still holding Skippy, and his speaker plays through ordinary scenes; his own subtitle hooks and the cancel are gone. **Replacing the game's lines** (new API `handle:replaceGameLines`, `docs/ADDON_API.md`): per add-on option **Replace the game's own lines** (default on) and **Mark our lines in the subtitles** (default on, adds ` (AI)` after the add-on speaker's name). The subtitle is hidden by one `Override` of `SubtitlesGameController.ShouldDisplayLine` installed on the first rule (fallback: `HideDialogLine` right after); the voice by `AudioSystem.Stop('vo_3d_important', speaker)` for Skippy; for the cab there is no known voice event, so **only its subtitles are hidden and its voice still plays** (the earlier "our welcome right when the game's would have come" is not done: it needs a verified voice stop). Skippy's claim: speaker named Skippy or `LocKey#46444`, or an `Invisible` line with no readable speaker name while he is held; Delamain's: speaker named Delamain while V sits in the cab; no other line is touched; the scene, map and payment are never skipped. Tests: bridge pace (priority bypass, token refund), game-dialogue filter/fallback/stop, addons-lua rules/settings/muted-line/silenced-scene, subtitle marker, Skippy and Delamain rules. Pre-existing failures in this worktree, not from this change: `facial-timeline` and `companion-actions` pinned SDK (node_modules absent here) and two packaging allowlist tests (`bridge/v-voice.mjs` missing from the list, from the V-voice work). **Next live test, probe.log lines:** `Game dialogue: subtitle filter installed`, `[Addons] muted game line: Skippy <id> (subtitle=filtered audio=stop-sent(vo_3d_important) ...)` (does the voice really stop? if not, the stop does not work for scene dialogue), `[Addons] Remark: ...`, `Remark played: ... event=... after Ns`, `Remark released: ... reason=... refused=<code> spoken=<bool>`, `Remark dropped: ... reason=...`, and `muted game line: Delamain <id> ...` on the cab ride; bridge.stderr.log: `remark ... did not reach the player; it does not count towards the local pace`.
**2026-10-14 (branch `v-voice-clips`, from main 87438a3a; measured, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT heard by anyone, game not touched, GPU not used): V's reference clips re-picked by the project's automatic picker.** User report on the Pocket voice: V's line sounds "a bit off, a little low quality" (gender and timing right). Inspection of the first clips: no gross defect (no clipping, peaks -5 to -8 dBFS, clean floor, no radio or phone filter, no music or SFX bleed, 24 kHz, loudness within 1 dB), but they were chosen by ASR match and loudness only; nothing measured speaker likeness, and it was poor: ECAPA to six held-out real V lines 0.537 (male) and 0.429 (female). The extractor also never looked at the scene index (12 percent of V's quest lines are inner-dialog, cyberspace or phone expressions; the first picks happened to be plain Spoken). Fix: V's lines went through the same flow as the companions: `build_v_pool.py` (1200 lines per gender in the picker's pool format with its effect exclusions plus dull, noisy, hot and the combat/expression flags from the scene index) then `search.py` (Pocket student v2 on the CPU, one low-priority worker, cap 18 s) then `compare_v.py`. Result on the same held-out lines: male 0.673 (search pick, 2 clips, 10.65 s) and 0.677 (3 clips, 13.6 s, shipped: calm opening, same score within one SE, higher on the four typed test sentences), female 0.521 (2 clips, 11.55 s; the search pick, shipped). Honest note: PLS is slightly higher for the old male clips (121.5 vs 115.7); the old clips were clean, they lacked likeness. ASR on the renders 0.98 to 1.00. `assets/v-voice/v-voice.json` is updated and committed; the new `v-male.wav` / `v-female.wav` / `v-voice-build.json` are in this worktree's `assets/v-voice/` (git-ignored, copy them into the main checkout with the merge and into the installed `voices/v-voice/`); the manifest and clips must be installed together (the bridge checks the hash). `GAP` in `scripts/clip-search/v-voice/vcommon.py` is now 0.4 s (the project's reference recipe, what the search scored). New scripts: `build_v_pool.py`, `run_search.py`, `compare_v.py`. Listening files (old and new references, rendered sentences, real V lines, README table): `C:\Users\akshi\AppData\Local\Temp\claude\v-voice-candidates\`. Open: the user listens (`ref_old.wav` vs the winner, `test*_old.wav` vs `test*_long.wav` male / `test*_auto.wav` female); if it still sounds low quality the cause is Pocket's rendering, not the reference (artifact measures of the renders are in the normal range of real V lines). `docs/V_VOICE.md`.

**2026-10-14 (branch `delamain-arrival`, from main 0f3abd5e; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched; needs a CET reload of the Delamain add-on): a false "destination reached" in the Delamain cab.** Live ride (probe.log, 20:35 UTC on 13 Oct by the log clock): V sat in the cab with Alex aboard, set a destination on the map, and 3 s after `ride-start` the game's own state went to Arrived (5) with the cab standing still; the game's Delamain also said "destination reached", so the add-on only mirrored the game. Evidence it is the vanilla taxi and not the mod: every main-mod hook on the taxi or AutoDrive is an Observe with read-only bodies (`mod/init.lua` `OnAutodriveToggled` after, `OnStopAutoDriveOnDestinationReachedRequest` before); the only `Override`s are phone, MCM and subtitle UI classes; the only setters near the cab are the companion mount and unmount commands (`mod/companion-vehicle-native.lua`, V's companions, not the cab) and `DisableAutoDriveRequest` in `companion-chauffeur-native.lua` `M.disable`, which refuses a Delamain journey and is only for old reload receipts; the add-on itself never writes a fact; the probe log shows no companion exit or chauffeur line near the event. What we cannot see: why the game said Arrived (the destination distance was not logged, so a point already near the cab, a stale value or a quirk of picking the point right after the greeting are all possible). Fix in `addons/delamain/init.lua`: an Arrived signal counts only after the cab moved at least 25 m (or half the start distance for a destination closer than 50 m) since `ride-start`, measured from V's position read once at the Started event (no polling); a re-plan (Started again) resets the origin; boarding (Ready) again clears the old plan, start time and arrival flag; an ignored arrival keeps the talk phase at "riding" and logs one line `[Addons] delamain-cab: arrival ignored (moved N m since ride-start, needs M m)`. Tests: five new cases in `addons/delamain/delamain.test.mjs` (the live sequence, a small creep, re-planning, boarding again, a zero-distance destination); the whole file, 40 tests, passes. Live check: take a cab, pick a point, and watch probe.log; if the game says "destination reached" before the cab drives, there must be an `arrival ignored` line and no `event=arrival` remark; a normal ride must still end with the arrival remark. If it fires on a ride that did drive, raise nothing: read the "moved" number in the line.
**2026-10-14 (branch `mature-v2`, from main 0f3abd5e; unit-tested; NOT merged, NOT installed, NO model run or downloaded, game not touched): Mature mode pushed harder, and its model choice cut to two (lite and good).** User verdict on the samples: "too tame, check online for better models or push them harder, and reduce the model options to just two, a lite one and a good one." (1) **Prompt, only while the setting is on** (off is byte for byte the old prompt: the 12 brief-set requests compared with `cmp`, plus the old rule texts kept verbatim in `bridge/mature-content.test.mjs`): output rule 6 no longer offers "a question back" and rule 3 gets "Four in an adult moment." (`RULES` in `bridge/mistral-prompt.mjs`); the layer names the words, says a guarded profile is how the character flirts and never a reason to dodge, says V's partner is the most forward, and keeps an adult moment on the two of them (no quest, gear, car or road talk, no stand-ins for sex: the Panam driving drift); a partner's relationship text ("do not narrate actions or kisses; affection is not consent to a new intimate act", warmth "a few words", practical first) is swapped by `matureWorld` for a shorter text that makes the partner lean in; the closing line says what to say. HARD LIMITS and adults-only lines word for word, guard untouched. Cost, tiktoken o200k, 12 cells of the brief set: Mature turn 4,319 to 4,360 tokens (+41, +0.9%), partner turn about -15, off 3,880 unchanged. Not measured on a model (GPU was busy with a live test): whether it is actually more explicit is unknown until the eval runs. Left alone on purpose: the "ordinary" relationship text for a confirmed partner whose romance delivery is Off (it still says no extra flirting; the user chose Off), and the optional follow-up-question setting (its pull is overridden by the closing line but not rewritten). **2026-10-14 (branch `teardown-safety`, from main 7d1379a8; unit-tested; NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): session-teardown review after two crashes during a save load (`Cyberpunk2077.exe+0x53F9D4` on 14 Oct, one second after "Session references released: player detached"; `+0xE6ABAD` on 12 Oct). Neither stack contains the native plugin (it only registers RTTI natives, holds no game object between calls and installs no hook). No single culprit is proven; four paths that made engine calls on objects being destroyed, and several strong handles that pinned them past the unload, were closed.** (1) `companion-locomotion.lua`: the `AIMoveToCommandHandler.UpdateCommand` observer (every walking NPC, every frame) stayed switched on after `companions.abandon` because only `companions.update` refreshed `convaiLocomotion.active`, and update is skipped until the next session is up; with a party it therefore called `GetOwner(context)` and `npc:GetEntityID()` on AI contexts the engine was destroying. It is now false from the start and set false in `companions.abandon` (fast travel too; the next update turns it on again). Both crash sessions had mod companions. (2) Add-on hooks (`addons/delamain`, `addons/skippy`) ran with no session check: the cab hooks read the quest system and `component:GetEntity()` during the unload, and Skippy's weapon-swap and crosshair hooks read the player and weapon (and could set `holding` true again after `sessionEnded`). New add-on API call `handle:live()` (true from the first tick of a session until the main mod releases it; documented in `docs/ADDON_API.md`); both add-ons return at once when it is false. Delamain counts the ignored callbacks by name and logs them at the next session start ("cab callbacks ignored while the previous session was released"), which shows which callbacks the engine sends during a teardown. A cab found at the start of a session is still picked up by `sessionStarted`. (3) `seated-look.lua`: the Workspot exit hooks called `CancelDelay` on the engine's delay system (and the pending id was never cleared) while the player's state machine left Workspot during a load; with the session released they now only forget (`live` = not `worldSuspended`, passed from `init.lua`); entering is not gated so a save loaded while V sits is still widened. (4) Handles held across the unload (a CET handle is a strong reference, and `onUpdate`, which would have cleared them, does not run until the next session): the face writer's cached actor rows (`companions.expressionActors` cache plus the `convaiExpressionFrame` context) held every ready copy's and nearby candidate's NPC; the gesture and seating controllers held V (`self.player`); the MCM page held old settings-screen widgets (`statusOriginal`, `contentHold`) and would have called into them on the next update. All dropped in `abandonSession`/the modules' abandon; `abandonSession` then runs one `collectgarbage` (not on fast travel, never per frame; unproven in game, a one-off hitch at a load) so the engine objects are released while the world still owns them, not by a Lua finalizer in the middle of the unload or the next session's start. Not changed: adoption records keep a hidden world NPC across a fast travel on purpose (needed to show it again); `mod/face-test.lua` (developer tool) has no abandon. Tests: `bridge/companion-behavior.test.mjs`, `bridge/companion-duplicates.test.mjs`, `bridge/companion-expression-playback.test.mjs`, `bridge/seated-look.test.mjs`, `addons/delamain/delamain.test.mjs`, `addons/skippy/skippy.test.mjs`. Reviewed and found sound: the whole `abandonSession` chain (pure Lua, file writes only, no native call), `companions`/`vehicles`/`actuator`/`equipment`/`reload-cleanup` abandon, speech/subtitles/continuous speech, `ConvaiTurnStep` delay callbacks (weak refs, generation checked), Codeware callbacks registered by UI controllers (unregistered in `OnUninitialize`), `ConvaiKnowledgeState` (unregisters in `OnDetach`). Still suspicious, unchanged: the Codeware `Entity/Extract` callback in `Face.reds` (untargeted, runs `GetDynamicEntitySystem().IsTagged` for every entity the engine builds, including during session start and end; a `dynamic.IsReady()` guard was NOT added because the redscript API could not be verified without the compiler); `Session/BeforeEnd` (already registered in `KnowledgeState.reds`) fires before the player detaches, while the Lua mod only learns of a teardown at `PlayerPuppet.OnDetach`, so callbacks arriving before that are not gated; the un-gated UI hooks (`phone-input.lua`, `phone-layout.lua`, `phone-chat-style.lua`, `subtitles.lua` `ChatterLineLogicController.SetLineData`).
**2026-10-14 (branch `holocall`, from main df759e93; unit-tested, redscript compiles in the isolated check; NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): holocalls with mod characters.** User request: call a character from the phone, with the game's call window at the top left, and talk to her that way. Research (decompiled scripts): the game's own call path (`questTriggerCallRequest` -> `PhoneSystem.TriggerCall`) writes the quest fact `phonecall_player_with_<contact>` and the `UI_ComDevice.PhoneCallInformation` blackboard that many systems read, so the mod never uses it. Instead it drives the HUD call window directly (`NewHudPhoneGameController.holoAudioCallElement`; `ShowIncomingContact`, `StartAudiocall`, `StartHolocall` and `ShowEndCallContact` on its logic controller; the game's own ring and connect sounds). The audio window with name and portrait is fully script-drivable. A live picture is a world render (`holocall_camera.ent`, a `RenderToTextureCamera` into `holocall_camera_render_texture.dtex`), so it is an experimental setting that is off by default: a tagged copy is held 40 m above V with the gesture workspot, and the camera faces it; on any failure the call goes on with the portrait. Subtitles use `scnDialogLineType.Holocall`, which is a main dialogue line with no distance check. Entry points: the Chat list's secondary action (now labelled Holocall) and the Call button inside an AI chat. Quest calls always win: a game Incoming or StartCall preempts the mod call before vanilla's handler, which always runs; a mod call never starts while the game has one or calling is disabled; input is never consumed. Talk goes through the same private path as face-to-face talk (warm on dial, voice at V with zero distance, no gaze, no gameplay actions), and the turn context says she is on a voice call or holocall and not with V. The call ends on hang-up (tap the phone button or hold reject), calling her again, combat, a scene, a replacer, a quest call, disabling her conversations, 3 min idle, 30 min, or the HUD going away. Johnny is excluded. Nothing runs per frame: a nil check while idle, and a 0.25 s step during a call. New: `Holocall.reds`, `mod/holocall.lua`, `bridge/holocall.test.mjs` (11 tests), `docs/HOLOCALL.md`. Changed: `PhoneChatTabs.reds`, `PhoneChat.reds`, `init.lua`, `speech.lua`, `continuous-speech.lua`, `subtitles.lua`, `manager.lua`, `mcm.lua`, `notices.lua`, the bridge `protocol`, `turn-context` and `mistral-prompt`, and docs NOTIFICATIONS and PHONE_CHAT. 11 new strings in 15 locales (Haiku helper; extractor `--check` clean). Not verified: everything in game, above all the tap-to-hang-up input, the subtitle type and the whole live picture. Probe lines and the test plan are in docs/HOLOCALL.md.

**2026-10-13 and 14 (branch `skippy-quality`; unit-tested, Mistral evals run; NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): Skippy add-on writing quality, API and builder.** Blind (strict Haiku) score of 30 random lines on the 12 fights, ministral-8b: about 4.5 before, 4.4 to 5.2 and 4.7 after (the two judge runs of the same lines differ by 0.7, as much as the gain); built-in judge 7.7 (stricter rubric, not comparable with 8.2). Dropped repeats 6 to 12 per pass before, 0 after; copied example lines 2 before, 0 after. The target of 7 is NOT reached and is not reachable with this model by prompting (14B scored the same as 8B in the writer's test; Mistral Small is rate limited on the free key). The best lines reach 6 to 7. API (generic, main mod): `cleanReply` keeps `*emphasis*` words and drops only real stage directions (shared with the companions, who are affected the same way: a single starred word like `*perfect*` used to be deleted from their replies too); new remarks are also checked against the persona's and mode's example lines (inside `review`, so the shared second try of `produce` applies); `reaction.maxWords` from the add-on cuts a remark at a sentence end with a small token budget (`capWords`, `remarkTokens`) and collapses a looped word; the eval judge sees the example lines and full facts. Skippy: device-kit joke rotation named for live and stock lines, a comic prop, distance only from 30 m (bridge and engine event text), exact numbers in plain words, no raw keys, opener and overused-joke tracking and a cross-fight joke notebook (written only by `remember`), a no-asterisks line, persona merged into `convai-addon.json` (14 rules cut to the 12 the API keeps; `persona.json` removed). FPS: the subtitle hooks now return on one cached boolean when Skippy is not held (test shows no clock read, no meter entry). Merged `main` (delamain-quality) in; `AddonHost.produce`/`isNearDuplicate` are reused, my example-line check sits inside `review`. Pre-existing failures not mine: `natural-speech.test.mjs` test 37 and `expressive-emotions.test.mjs` test 16 (4 !== 3, already on main). Install (nothing under `mod/` changed): `addons/skippy/{bridge.mjs,engine.lua,init.lua,convai-addon.json,EVENTS.md}`, `bridge/{mistral-prompt,addons,text-provider-host,convai-bridge,addon-eval}.mjs`. Numbers and the best lines: `docs/addons/SKIPPY_SAMPLES.md`. Open: more wit needs a bigger model or hand-written line banks, not more prompt rules (naming a tic in a prompt makes the 8B model use it).
**2026-10-14 (branch `crouch-relaxed`, from main 5279552f; unit-tested; NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not touched): companions did not crouch outside combat because the stance alone shows nothing in the Relaxed state; the crouch now raises the high-level state first, with a head-height read-back.** Live (probe.log 19:29Z): V crouched outside combat, `Companion stance: crouch hls=Relaxed (evelyn-parker)`, no retry line, and Evelyn stayed standing; `stand hls=Relaxed` 23 s later. The stance request was accepted (no error, no `crouch again`, so the stance blackboard read Crouch), yet no pose. Cause, from the decompiled 2.31 scripts (`cyberpunk/NPC/components/npcStateComponent.swift`, `NPCPuppet.swift`; fetched read-only from the public dump already cited in `docs/addons/SKIPPY_RESEARCH.md`): `NPCPuppet.ChangeStanceState` only queues an `NPCStateChangeSignal`; `NPCStatesComponent.UpdateStanceState` then sets the `stanceState` anim feature, the Stance blackboard and the `inCrouch` wrapper weight. The locomotion wrappers that carry the walking poses are keyed by the HIGH-LEVEL state (`GetAnimWrapperNameBasedOnHighLevelState`: Alerted `alertedLocomotion`, Combat `combatLocomotion`, Stealth `stealthLocomotion`, Relaxed none). So in Relaxed nothing consumes the crouch (this is the likely cause; it is read from code, not yet seen in game, and the new head reading will decide it). Vanilla followers crouch only when their own AI is in Stealth. Ways the game itself reaches Stealth, all read in the dump: `NPCPuppet.ChangeHighLevelState(npc, Stealth)` from `AIFollowerTakedownCommandHandler` and from the move command handlers when `alwaysUseStealth` is set (AIMoveTo/AIMoveOnSpline/AIMoveRotateTo, NOT AIFollowTargetCommand, which has no stealth or crouch flag: fields are target, desiredDistance, tolerance, stopWhenDestinationReached, movementType, lookAtTarget, matchSpeed, teleport), plus the `StealthState` AI task. `AIFollowerRole.OnHighLevelStateEnter(Stealth)` barks only with a hostile threat; the Stealth case of `OnHighLevelStateEnter` in the state component does nothing (no senses preset change, no registration). Alerted by contrast calls `TryRegisterToPrevention` and `RefreshCachedReactionPresetData`, and the installed `ApproachVehicleDecorator` refuses boarding while Alerted, so it is the fallback, not the first choice. Companions never enter Alerted on their own (`AlertedCondition` returns false for player companions). Fix (`mod/companion-stance.lua`): crouch = `NPCPuppet.ChangeHighLevelState(npc, Stealth)` first (only from Relaxed; a state the game chose is never changed), the stance call 0.15 s later (two state-change signals in one frame may overwrite each other), then the Head slot height above her feet is read once after the stance call (same `GetSlotTransform('Head')` read `init.lua` already uses) and compared with her standing height read before the crouch: down 0.2 m or more = confirmed; not down within 2 s = the next method (Alerted), then none (Relaxed restored, the old stance-only behaviour). Stand: stance Stand first, then Relaxed 0.15 s later, only while the state still reads the level we raised (never over Combat, Fear, Unconscious); both read back, re-issued every 0.6 s up to three times, head compared with the standing height; `stand not kept by the game` if still wrong. If the AI resets the raised state to Relaxed while crouched, it is raised again up to three times, then `keeps resetting`. A stand because of a blocker (vehicle, lift, tactic, ...) restores Relaxed too, and V crouching again before the restore keeps ownership of the raise. No per-frame work: one 10 Hz tick, one blackboard observer, bounded timers (0.15 s gap, 0.6 s read-back, 0.5 s re-check while crouched, head read until confirmed only). Known side effects while she is crouched in Stealth: gestures stay off (they already do), and the holster handoff waits for Relaxed (`NPC is not relaxed autonomous AI`). **Log lines for the next live test (install `mod/companion-stance.lua`, CET reload; all start `Companion stance:`):** V crouches: `crouch issued via stealth (hls Relaxed -> Stealth, then stance Crouch) stance=Crouch hls=Stealth`, then within about 1.5 s either `crouch confirmed via stealth: head down 0.4x m stance=Crouch hls=Stealth` (works), or `crouch NOT visible via stealth: head down only 0.0x m ...; trying alerted` followed by `crouch confirmed via alerted: ...` (Alerted works, Stealth does not; make `methods = {'alerted'}` the default), or `crouch NOT visible via alerted ...; no method left, hls back to Relaxed` (neither works: the pose needs something else, e.g. a direct `stealthLocomotion` wrapper weight, and the head numbers tell how far off), or `crouch confirmed via stealth by the blackboards only (no head reading)` (the Head slot read failed: then judge by eye). Other lines: `crouch: hls fell back to Relaxed, raised again (stealth)`, `crouch: the game keeps resetting hls to Relaxed after stealth`, `crouch not kept by the game`. V stands: `stand issued via stance, then hls Stealth -> Relaxed stance=Stand hls=Stealth`, then `stand confirmed stance=Stand hls=Relaxed, head back (0.0x m below standing)`; bad cases `stand: blackboards read Stand but the head is still down ...`, `stand again`, `hls Relaxed again`, `stand not kept by the game`. If the stand line shows `hls=Stealth` after confirmation, gestures will say `not relaxed`: report it. Also watch that she draws no weapon and nobody nearby reacts. Tests: `bridge/companion-stance.test.mjs` (35 tests; 14 new: order and gap of the two calls, stand restore, Alerted escalation, no-method fallback, game-chosen states untouched, combat takeover, AI reset retries, ignored restore, crouch-again ownership, blocker, no-head-reading, stance-only adapter, bounded reads, adapter wiring).
**2026-10-14 (full test suite made green again, branch `fix-suite-3`; tests plus one line of bridge code; nothing to install except the bridge line, no game contact).** After the 2026-10-13 merges the full run (31 files in parallel, the game open, other agents working) had 18 failures; every failing file passed on its own, so these were load and wall-clock problems, plus one real shutdown noise. (1) `bridge/local-llm-server.test.mjs`, `local-context.test.mjs`, `local-embed.test.mjs` (15 tests): they start the fake llama-server as a real node process with a 5 s start allowance, which a loaded computer misses (`did not start (timeout)`); the allowance is now 60 s in their harnesses (the tests that expect a timeout keep their own short value). (2) `bridge/bridge-startup.test.mjs` (real bridge keeps one player ID): a REAL bug, harmless but noisy: a settings poll still running while the bridge shut down was logged as `Settings poll: Voice service is closed`; `bridge/convai-bridge.mjs` now stays quiet about errors from the settings poll once `stopped` is set (needs a bridge restart, nothing else changes). (3) `bridge/group-lookahead-bridge.test.mjs`: cleanup hit EBUSY on the temp folder (`group-lookahead-fixture.mjs` now retries the removal), the three-at-once tests put six real bridges beside the suite (now two at a time), and the 'generation' bound compared a wall-clock time with a fixed 2.7 s; it now compares with the serial run's first speaker measured under the same load (+1.2 s). (4) `bridge/local-stt.test.mjs` and `local-stt-capture.test.mjs` (the real Moonshine worker): the worker was started with 30 s to load and at below-normal priority, so a busy computer starved it (`took too long to start`, or no live words ever); the tests now give it 120 s / 60 s and normal priority (production is unchanged). (5) `bridge/manager.test.mjs` 'with the settings menu closed the queue flush...' (about 1 run in 40 failed alone): the once-a-second work follows the real `os.time`, so a run that crossed a wall-clock second read the player extra times; the test now holds `os.time` still. (6) `bridge/voxtral-voice.test.mjs` 'several group speakers go out one after another' (seen once in the re-run): it measured the spacing at the local server, where arrival jitter showed a 98 ms gap for a 120 ms spacing; it now measures when the scheduler releases each request. Not regressions of the code: in a fresh git worktree the gitignored folders (`node_modules`, `vendor`, `runtime`, `dist`) are missing, which fails `facial-timeline`, `companion-actions` and `runtime-bootstrap` and skips the real STT tests; run the suite from a checkout that has them (this session linked them into the worktree and removed the links afterwards). Full suite three times in a row: 4905 tests, 4893 pass, 0 fail, 12 skipped (the skips are tests whose game files or tools are absent here).
**2026-10-14 (branch `preparing-notice`, from main 5279552f; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game; needs a bridge restart plus a CET reload for the Lua and locale files): the phone/manager "preparing" error is now a held notice that names what blocks the companion.** Live test: Evelyn was spawned from the phone right after a save load and got a "could not prepare" notice while the models were still loading; she spawned fine once they had (probe.log: "Phone preparation requested for alex", then for evelyn-parker twice). Trace of preparation, request to ready or error: `mod/init.lua processPhoneAction` -> `queue.invite` -> `mod/invitation-readiness.lua` publishes `runtime/companion-invitations.json` -> `bridge/companion-invitations.mjs` (lane per profile: memory service `prepareCharacter`, then voice model `prepareVoice`, then the connection `warmInvitation`; at most 8 lanes at once, the rest queue) -> receipt `companion-invitations-state.json` -> `readiness.status` -> `manager-party.lua`. Every reason it can wait: the conversation service has not answered yet (starting, or busy loading models; no fresh receipt), its turn in the queue (more than 8), the memory service, the voice model loading, her connection opening (also a connection waiting for a free place), and in gameplay the game placing her (shared frame budget, adopted-character gate). Every reason it can fail: no save loaded, unknown profile, the service silent for too long (was 15 s from the request, so a service busy or still starting after a save load failed her; now a minute, counted from its last fresh receipt), the voice not ready, the memory step failing, the connection failing, no free connection, the player memory changing under it, plus internal identity errors. The daily limit does not apply: it counts conversation turns, never preparation. Local AI warming does not block a spawn either (the text model is only needed when she speaks). Change: the bridge receipt now carries `stage` (memory-state rows) and `errorKind` (error rows), descriptive only; the wait is a line of the same held loading notice as "Loading the OmniVoice voice…" (keys `prep1` to `prep3` in `mod/mcm.lua`, lines from `waiting()` in `mod/manager-party.lua`): "Preparing Evelyn: waiting for the voice model to load…", also "…the conversation service to connect…", "…the memory service…", "…the conversation connection to open…", "…the game to place the character…", and "Preparing 2 more companions…" past three. It shows after 1.5 s (a quick spawn shows nothing), changes in place as the step changes (new `revisions` table in `convaiNotices.loading`, event-driven on the existing 0.25 s tick, nothing per frame), lists every companion waiting (a second request adds a line, two copies of one character share one), stays until she has spawned, and is dropped on a failure, whose own notice now says what failed and what to do ("The voice model did not load. Check the voice in Settings or choose another one, then invite again.", the memory service / connection variants, and "The conversation service did not answer. Wait a moment, then invite again."). The old five-second "Preparing connection to {name}…" line for invitations is gone (the string stays for starting a conversation); `autoConnect.connected` (only it used it) is removed. Showing the notice never delays the spawn. Ten new strings in 15 locales (`node scripts/Extract-UiCatalog.mjs` run and `--check` clean). Tests: invitation-notices (waiting lines), invitation-readiness (wait named, a minute of silence, failures by kind), companion-invitations (stage, errorKind), notices (`revisions`), manager (held notice end to end, shared with the voice load, menu), world-lifecycle and the old preparing-line tests updated. Docs: NOTIFICATIONS.md, MANAGER_UI.md, COMPANION_INVITATIONS.md.
**2026-10-14 (branch `delamain-timing`, from main 5279552f; unit-tested in fengari and node, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT seen in the game, game not launched, no live Convai turns; needs a bridge restart plus a CET reload): the Delamain cab's welcome, ride and goodbye lines no longer collide with the game's own cab lines, no longer get cut off, and no longer get lost.** Live report from the user's second cab ride: the first message never played (too close to the intro), his replies were cut off abruptly, the welcome and ending did not play (they probably coincide with the game's own message), and the driving looked messy (he hit cops and a fight started). Causes: (1) cut-off replies: a bridge bug, not timing. `continuousSpeech.onGenerated` in `bridge/convai-bridge.mjs` threw "Conversation receipt owner changed" for every add-on turn, because an add-on speaker has no knowledge receipt; the reply was cut when the first chunk landed (seen in bridge.stderr.log). Fixed: an add-on turn with no receipt skips the knowledge calls and only has to be the current turn (a turn that lost ownership is still rejected). (2) welcome and ending lost: the game's `delamain_taxi.scene` plays its greeting and farewell as quest-scene subtitle lines, and the host only knew scene flags from the 0.5 s observer sample; a flight that met a scene was released and never asked for again (the old retry only covered queue drops). (3) first message lost right after the intro: the same path, plus a pending request with no reply held the queue (36 s seen). Fixes in `mod/addons.lua`: a remark can have `patience` (seconds) and a `relevant()` check; while a game scene, a scene tier or a game subtitle line is up it is held, not dropped (its ttl does not run), and sent after the scene clears plus 1.2 s of quiet if still relevant, with a fresh clock; a patient remark taken back in flight (a scene hit before it was heard) is asked for again, twice at most; a request that gets no reply within ttl plus 2 s is withdrawn ('stale') so it cannot block later remarks, and a patient one is asked again; no game line playing means no wait at all. New `M.gameLine(seconds, kind, measured)` is fed by `mod/game-dialogue.lua` `noteLine` (Regular, OwnerlessRegular, Holocall, SceneComment and Radio lines, once each, with their own duration or an estimate of 2.5 words/s, clamped to 1.5 to 10 s; the mod's own replies, crowd chatter and TV never count), through `onGameLine` in `mod/init.lua`, only while an add-on is live (no cost otherwise, no per-frame work; the host's 0.1 s tick already runs only while an add-on is live). A speaker can set `playThrough`: a line already playing finishes over an ordinary scene (dialogue, choices) instead of being cut; a call, Johnny (replacer), a tier 4+ cinematic or the player's own turn still stops it. Why play through rather than hold the game's subtitle off ours: the cab's greeting and farewell are a quest scene we cannot delay or reorder, so the workable options are to wait for it before we start (done) and not to be cut by it once we have started (done). Delamain add-on (`addons/delamain/init.lua`): a `WAIT` table gives each event its patience and the ride states it stays relevant in (boarded 30 s / READY, ride-start 20 s / STARTED, district-passed 12 s / STARTED, arrival 25 s / ARRIVED or FINISHED, ride-interrupted 20 s / CANCELLED); the arrival and goodbye stay relevant briefly after the ride ends while V is still in the cab; the old RETRY machinery is removed; a destination chosen while the greeting still waits turns into one ride-start line that greets (the greeting is cancelled; one line, not two). Docs: `docs/ADDON_API.md` (Patience section, `playThrough`, `requeued`, new reasons), `addons/delamain/EVENTS.md`. Tests: `bridge/addons-lua.test.mjs` (patience, relevant and expiry, requeue twice at most, stale withdrawal, game lines, playThrough), `addons/delamain/delamain.test.mjs` (welcome taken back and said again, game line holds the greeting, plays through, arrival after the farewell, no arrival to an empty cab, destination chosen during a held greeting), `bridge/game-dialogue.test.mjs` (noteLine), `bridge/voice-routing.test.mjs` (add-on turn without receipt); the harness `bridge/addon-lua-harness.mjs` now names the failing test line. Full suite: only environment failures in this worktree (no `node_modules` here: facial-timeline, the companion-actions SDK adapter test, the runtime-bootstrap playwright test) plus the known timing-flaky voxtral spacing test, which passes alone. The unknown only the game can settle: which scene signal the cab ride raises (dialogue, tier, or only a subtitle line); the logs name it. **Driving verdict: nothing in the mod or the add-on writes to the cab's driving.** The Delamain add-on is voice only (it reads ride state from the `delamain_taxi_state` fact and the observer; it never sets vehicle or driver AI, speed or route). The main mod's chauffeur modules (`companion-chauffeur-native.lua`, `companion-chauffeur.lua`) explicitly refuse the Delamain cab (`delamainCab`) and treat a foreign AutoDrive journey as someone else's; the companion vehicle code only seats companions (taxi seat order is back-left, front-right, back-right, front-left last, so the driver seat is used only with four or more companions); seated gaze writes look-at only; the work done while the cab moves is read-only sampling. The cop collision is most likely the game's own autodrive (the vanilla Delamain does hit traffic and police); it cannot be ruled out from code alone, so a live A/B is the answer. Cleanest live A/B: the same short ride, same start and destination, twice: once with the mod disabled in CET (or add-on and companions off, nobody in the party), once as in the report; also read probe.log for `Native core: vehicles=native|lua`, `journey-start`, `seat-reserved`, `mount-requested`, `taxi-arrived`, and check that no companion took `seat_front_left`. **Log lines to look for in the next ride:** `[Addons] Remark held: ... reason=scene|scene-tier|game-line signals=... patience=Ns` (what held it, and the signal the cab scene raises), `[Addons] Remark: ... waited=N.Ns` (it went after the wait), `Remark released: ... reason=scene signals=... (taken back; it is asked for again)`, `Remark plays through a game scene: ... signals=...`, `Remark dropped: ... reason=expired|irrelevant`, `Game line: <kind> for about X s (its own duration|estimated); patient remarks wait for it`; in bridge.stderr.log there must be no `Bridge request failed: Conversation receipt owner changed` for Delamain turns.
**2026-10-14 (branch `v-voice`, from main 5279552f; unit-tested, NOT merged, NOT installed, NOT heard in the game, game not touched; needs a bridge restart plus a CET reload for the Lua and locale files): new setting "V's voice for typed lines" (Voices, off by default) speaks what the player types in V's own voice, cloned from clips cut from the game's V lines.** User request: a setting so that, only for typed input, V's voice is generated so it sounds like V said the line. Design and detail: `docs/V_VOICE.md`. (1) *Setting*: `vVoice` in `mod/manager.lua` (default Off, snapshot field, bool validation, `M.vVoice()`), a row after Follow-up questions in `mod/mcm.lua` (also in the Speech line of the Characters sidebar), the bridge reads it from `runtime/settings.json` in `pollSettings`. (2) *Voices*: the setting uses the voice the player already chose. Pocket and OmniVoice clone V locally; Mistral (Voxtral) and Inworld clone in the cloud and load no local voice model. The Convai cloud voice cannot clone: turning the setting on beside it is refused with a short message, choosing the Convai voice while it is on keeps the voice and turns V's voice off with one notice, and a saved pairing loads as off. (3) *Clips*: one reference per gender cut from the game's own V lines (male 11.4 s from 3 lines, female 14.5 s from 4 lines; `scripts/clip-search/v-voice/`, Oodle plus vgmstream on this machine, CPU only, Whisper small.en matches every subtitle 0.94 to 1.00). **Nobody has listened to them and they are not approved.** `assets/v-voice/v-voice.json` (hashes, transcripts, sources) is committed; the `.wav` files and `v-voice-build.json` are git-ignored like the add-on clips and `packaging/build-release.mjs` packs them from the local copy into `voices/v-voice/` (a build without them works and logs it). They are merged into the voice configurations at bridge start as `v-male` and `v-female` (`AddonHost.voiceConfigPaths` `extra`, lazy, so nothing is loaded for them; `withoutVariants` keeps them out of every profile list). (4) *Latency*: the typed text goes to the model as before and V's line starts after that request is under way (`runInput`); it plays on a second hidden renderer page (`RealtimeHost.playVoiceLine`) with its own control (no game utterance, so no face, lip sync or subtitle). The companion's audio waits only while V is audible: the existing continuous-speech `hold` buffers it silently (a reply published whole waits in `publishResult`). A V line that is not audible when the reply is ready is dropped; one still waiting for its first audio when the companion's own `ttsSent` mark comes is stopped on a local voice. Timings: V's own `TurnClock` (`voiceStart`, `ttsSent`, `firstPcm`, `audible`, `ended` or `dropped` with a reason), one `[VVoice]` log line and a `vVoice` record in `bridge-latency.json`; the companion's clock gets `vAudible`, `vEnded` or `vDropped`. (5) *Gender and volume*: V's gender is the turn context's `gender` (`GetResolvedGenderName`); the game's Master x Dialogue volume is read once per typed turn while the setting is on (`dialogueVolume` in `mod/turn-context.lua`, cached 5 s with the rest). Nothing runs per frame. (6) *Tests*: `bridge/v-voice.test.mjs` (clips, line text, WAV to packets, gating, the real local renderer host with an offline player, the real continuous speech held by V), plus additions to `manager`, `turn-context` and `addons` tests; `bridge/turn-clock.mjs` gained `on()`. `packaging/runtime-layout.mjs` lists the new module. 15 locales plus `localization/en.json` got the four new strings (translated by a Haiku helper: a native-speaker pass can follow). `packaging/runtime-bootstrap.test.mjs` cannot finish in the worktree (no `node_modules/playwright`); its allowlist comparison passes up to that point.
**2026-10-13 (branch `mature-samples`; code, unit tests and the model runs done; not merged, not installed): the Mature layer is explicit and forward, a log line says when it is applied, and a sampler shows how the uncensored models talk.** Why: in the live test the 2B uncensored model answered as Evelyn with generic, coy replies, one sentence repeated. Findings on the first samples (`scripts/Eval-MatureSamples.mjs`, 4 characters x 8 adult prompts x 2 samples, the real prompt path with persona, quest, gear, relationship block and the real layer): the layer WAS applied (the host now logs `[MatureContent] layer applied (level full, model ...)` once per bridge session, and `layer NOT applied (provider ...)` when a cloud provider answers with the setting on); the cautious wording ("you may refuse, tease or deflect", "unless the facts say you are together you are not V's lover") was not the only cause: the output rules ("stay vague about what the profile does not cover, a question back"), the guarded persona and the quest/gear context outweigh a layer in the system message, and the 2B is simply too weak (incoherent half of the time). Changes (user direction: explicit, direct, forward): `matureLayer` rewritten (plain and graphic about sex, violence, gore and drugs; forward; the relationship facts set the tone and no longer forbid; personality shapes HOW, not whether; consenting adults), a tone line that overrides "stay vague / question back" for an adult moment, and one sentence in the closing line of the user message (`matureClosing`, only with the setting on): measured on the 4B, sexual words per reply 0.49 with the first explicit layer and 0.88 with the closing sentence.
