**2026-10-13 (later): the Voxtral reference is Ogg Vorbis q2, because the 24 kHz MP3 still sounded dull (bridge only; tests pass; NOT yet seen live, needs a bridge restart; each reference re-prepares once).** The user listened to the level-matched files: the 40k MP3 clone (version 3) was still dull, the whole-clip clone fine. Cause: an MP3 encoder low-passes at 10.5 kHz (40k) to 11.3 kHz (64k, whatever `-cutoff` says), so the 10 to 12 kHz band of the answers was 8 to 9 dB under the whole clip's (-33.0 against -24.7 dB). Formats tested on the real API: Ogg Vorbis, Ogg Opus, FLAC and 8-bit WAV are accepted, M4A/AAC is refused (the error text says only mp3 or wav). Ogg Vorbis q2 (37.6 KB, body 50.4 KB) matches the 24 kHz WAV and the whole clip: 10 to 12 kHz within 1.0 dB, 8 to 10 kHz 1.4 to 1.9 dB brighter, bw40 11,971 Hz, mean of 12 calls. No latency cost: median first audio 1,138 ms against 1,194 ms for the 40k MP3 (pooled; interleaved pairs 1,050 against 1,245 ms). Preparer version 4: Ogg Vorbis q2, else MP3 64k, else the 24 kHz WAV; bit-exact (the same bytes from the release builder and a player); a 400 about the audio file for an Ogg reference switches the voice to MP3 references and clears the cache (that one reply falls to Pocket). `packaging/build-release.mjs` now accepts any compressed slot (it needs ffmpeg with libvorbis). Files: `E:\CyberpunkConvai-offload\work\voxtral-ab-2026-10-13\listen-levelmatched\V4-24k-vorbis-q2-s1|s2|s3-lvl.wav` next to `D-original-wav-s1-lvl.wav` (never played). See `docs/CLOUD_VOICE_VOXTRAL.md`, section "The reference format search".

**2026-10-13: the latency queue (`docs/LATENCY_2026-10-13.md` section 4; branch `latency-queue`; unit-tested, measured offline and for Mistral and Local AI; NOT yet seen in the game: bridge restart, no Lua change, no mod file changed).** (4.1) The voice key on a text route voices the reply from its first sentence, as a typed turn does (`bridge/voice-key-stream.mjs`, `prepareReply`/`onReply`, shared `voiceStreamedReply`): with the harness (`scripts/Measure-TurnLatency.mjs --voice-key`) sound starts 462 ms (Mistral + Voxtral), 402 ms (Groq), 1,216 ms (local + Voxtral) and 1,015 ms (local + Pocket) sooner. (4.2) The playback pump polls when the game writes its lease (watcher on `continuous-control.json`, `createWakeSignal`): about 11 ms (Windows timer granularity is the floor). (4.3) The face file is written after the reply, not before the state: first frame to published 10.7 to 5.5 ms idle; state and reply stay in sequence (a test pins that a cancel never admits the reply). (4.4) `speech-state.json` is read from a copy at most 1 s old on the turn path (5.5 ms to 0.01 ms per turn). (4.5) The Mistral and Groq connection is kept 60 s (read to the end after `[DONE]`, a shared Node undici `Agent`, `warmConnection` when the provider is applied and the composer opens): about 36 to 48 ms on every turn more than 4 s after the previous one, measured live. (4.6) The Omni first phrase's WAV is prepared while the face analysis runs and the diagnostic file is written after the reply: 112 to 90 ms idle, 284 to 257 ms with a slow analysis; the shorter first phrase was NOT done (gap risk and prosody cannot be judged without the GPU and a listener); the provisional face (a Lua change) was not done. (4.7) The chat `llama-server` starts with `-fit off`: 7.24 s to 5.65 s median to `/health`. 4.8 (native notification) not done. Live checks: `docs/LATENCY_2026-10-13.md` section 7, items 8 to 13.

**2026-10-13 (live test): loading notice is now ONE held notice, and moving a slider or option no longer flashes the page (unit-tested; NOT yet seen in the game; Lua only, CET reload).** (1) The loading line was re-posted every 3 s, so it popped up, chimed and blinked each time. The game warning line (`WarningMessageGameController`, read from the installed redmod scripts) plays an intro and a jingle for every message and hides it after the message `duration`, and `SetWarningMessage` always asks for 5 s. `mod/notices.lua` now writes the loading notice once to `UI_Notifications.WarningMessage` (a `SimpleScreenMessage`, duration 90 s as a safety net, built with `SimpleScreenMessage.new` / `ToVariant`, the usual CET snippet), refreshes it instantly (`isInstant`, no intro) every 80 s and when the list of loads changes, hides it with `isShown = false` when the load ends without an outcome line (only when it is still the notice on screen), and lets the ready or failure line replace it. A menu opening mid-load posts it again once on return; a game notice over it is waited out (one read a second) and ours returns; if the blackboard write fails it falls back to the old repeated notice. Tests: `bridge/notices.test.mjs`, loading cases in `bridge/manager.test.mjs`. Docs: `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md` (Loading notices). (2) Slider flicker, root cause: every value change makes MCM redraw the whole screen from its own update, which runs after ours in the frame, so the new rows showed in MCM default look for a frame before being styled, were veiled, then shown. The page content is now created hidden (`McmUiBeginContent` override passes `contentReady = false`; no widget is touched in the hook) and shown when styled, with a 1.5 s failsafe that releases a stale hold and a switch-off if the wrapper cannot be found. Still visible on a change: the page is blank for the one to three frames styling takes, and the status line and bottom buttons are placed after styling. Docs: `docs/MANAGER_UI.md`. Tests: `bridge/manager.test.mjs`.

**2026-10-13: latency pass, every hop from the player's last word to the first sound (`docs/LATENCY_2026-10-13.md`; unit-tested, NOT yet seen in the game: needs the mod files and a bridge restart).** The live records showed every Mistral turn waiting 410 to 483 ms in memory recall: the embedding query waited its whole 400 ms timeout for a `llama-server` that never started in the game session (`embedding server did not start: timeout`), so recall gave nothing and cost 0.4 s. Now a turn never waits for the embedder to start (it is started for the next turn, deferred past the request), the server starts with `-fit off` (6.3-7.7 s to 2.6-3.4 s) and stays up 5 minutes: measured 404/408 ms to 2/1 ms on cold turns. The request, microphone and phone mailboxes are read by a directory watcher (`bridge/file-wake.mjs`; detection 38/78 ms mean to 8 ms; polls kept as fallback). The game checks `reply.json` and `phone-chat-output.json` every 30 ms while a reply is due through the native plugin's revision number (`convaiNative.revision`, plugin polls 15/25 ms; simulated 65 to 24 ms per reply; nothing changes without the plugin), and stops decoding an unchanged `reply.json` on every tick. The "thinking" status write no longer holds up the request. Expected: about 470 ms less per typed text-route turn while the embedder is down, about 75 ms when it is up, plus about 70 ms on voice-key and phone turns. Queued with exact proposals for the agents in those files: streaming the voice-key reply (0.17-1.2 s), the OmniVoice first phrase (2.36 s: store beside the analysis, a shorter first phrase, a faster kernel, a provisional face), waking the playback pump on the game's lease, publishing before the face write, a cached speech-state read, Mistral connection reuse, `-fit off` for the chat server. Then the lip-sync convolution runs in a WebAssembly SIMD kernel (`bridge/jali-face-model.mjs`, scalar loop kept as fallback, `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_FACE_WASM=off` forces it): a 4.56 s phrase analysed in 76 ms instead of 622 (96 instead of 726 with text), faces within 2.2e-6, so the OmniVoice first phrase should start about 1.7 s sooner. Live checks: section 7 of the doc.

**2026-10-13: the Local AI prompt is fitted to the server's context, a turn it cannot answer goes to the cloud or says so (unit-tested and run against the fake llama-server; NOT yet seen in the game).** Live record 2026-10-12: the player talked to Judy and heard nothing; `The local AI request (8790 tokens) exceeds the available context size (8192 tokens)`. Root cause: the server ran at a fixed 8,192 tokens while the prompt was bounded only in characters (persona and rules about 4.5k tokens, the rest up to 9k+), and the refusal was a code-less, non-retried, non-falling-back error. Fix: `bridge/prompt-budget.mjs` fits every Local and Groq (8,000 tokens a minute) request to n_ctx minus a reply reserve, estimated with a learned ratio and counted with `/tokenize` near the limit, cutting jobs, journal history, recent events, gear, retrieved memories, older turns, the summary, the tracked quest and the world facts in that order (discrete cuts, so the prefix cache holds) and never the persona, rules, right-now block or the player's words; a refusal that still comes is learned from and retried once. Context per tier: the supervisor asks for 16,384, then 12,288, then 8,192 by free video memory and reads the real n_ctx from `/props`; measured +136 MiB (standard, 3,008 to 3,144 MiB) and +104 MiB (lite, 1,399 to 1,503 MiB) from 8k to 16k, q8_0 KV saves only 75 to 90 MiB and is not used. A failed local turn falls back to Mistral (key saved) or Groq, with the notice `local-turn-fallback`; with no cloud provider the game says `local-failed`. EPERM on `knowledge-status.json`: a reader without the delete share (CET `io.open` in the Lua heartbeat check) blocks the rename; the bridge's retry already outlasts it, the log line is now rate-limited, the plugin needs no change. Detail: `docs/LOCAL_AI.md`. Still to do: a live Local AI turn in the game with a deliberately small context (`CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_LOCAL_CTX=4096`) to see the trimming and the fallback notice.

**2026-10-13: companion actions now work on the Mistral and Local AI text paths (unit-tested and measured against the real Mistral API; NOT yet seen in the game: needs a bridge restart and a CET reload; Gemma measured only in part).** Root cause of Judy ignoring "drive for me": the text path never read `config.companionAction` at all (no action prompt, no parser, no delivery), so only Convai could act, and the replies were pure talk. Convai's actions are drive to the waypoint, attack the marked target, follow, and leave the party; there is no wait, stay, sit or stop action in the game. Design (`bridge/text-actions.mjs`, wired in `TextProviderHost`): a separate 12-token classifier request (one word, temperature 0, runs beside the memory lookup, failure means plain talk) decides, the reply prompt gets a `GAME ACTIONS` note in the volatile block (stable prefix untouched) that says what is asked or what V has to do first, and the host delivers the same message Convai would send after a good reply; stray `[CODE]` tags are stripped before the voice. New `driveUnavailable` code (Lua `gameplay-actions.lua` -> `protocol.mjs` -> `companion-actions.mjs`, text routes only) carries why a ride is impossible. Rejected: tool calls (ministral-8b returns no speech on action turns and picks the wrong tool) and a code at the start of the reply (recall 12% or precision 58%). Eval (`scripts/Eval-MistralPrompt.mjs --actions`, ministral-8b, 6 characters, 27 situations, n=3): before 0 of 196 asked actions fired; after precision 100% (195/195), recall 99% (195/197), 0 false triggers in 288 turns; a Haiku judge scored false promises on impossible requests 19% before and 3% after, and replies that name what is needed 12% before and 26% after (wait and sit still draw a promise 17% and 6% of the time). Gemma 4 E4B through the same classifier (own llama-server, 2 characters, 16 situations, n=2, 64 replies; it takes about 7 s a reply on this GPU): precision 100% (13/13), recall 81% (13/16: one follow and two leaves missed, so a miss means V repeats the request); before is 0 by construction. Open: watch the first live "drive for me" with a car, a waypoint and V on foot; add a local-model eval cell for multi-turn history.

**2026-10-13: Convai sign-in backend built on branch `convai-auth`, nothing deployed, bridge mode OFF by default (unit-tested only; no live Convai, Vercel or Upstash call was made).** Design: `docs/CONVAI_AUTH_BACKEND.md`. Backend: `auth-backend/` (Vercel Node functions: `POST /api/convai/token`, `GET /api/admin/turns`, `/api/healthz`, `/api/readyz`; no dependencies). Every limit is one Redis script call (one Upstash command per token request): per-install and per-address hourly limits, new installs per address per day, a global daily mint cap ("busy"), a server-side turn backstop (an install whose reported UTC-day turns reach 100 gets no new token, "daily limit reached"), kill switch, minimum mod version, blocklist, signed requests (HMAC plus a +-120 s timestamp; obfuscation only, the secret ships in the bridge), fail closed when Redis is down. Two modes behind `CONVAI_AUTH_MODE`: `shared` (default, one token per warm instance) and `per-install` (own token per install, revoked on the cap; needs a live test because Convai's mint limit is unpublished). Bridge: `bridge/convai-credential.mjs` (`TokenSource`: fetches in the background at start, renews 8 to 10 minutes before expiry, never on a turn; install id in `runtime/convai-install.json`), `ConvaiKeys.credentialFor`, `RealtimeHost` `credential` hook, `realtime-client.mjs` passes `authToken`, `player-memory-api.mjs` backend mode (API-AUTH-TOKEN header, no character list/read/update), `scripts/Enable-CharacterMemory.mjs` (developer-side, NOT run). Switch on with `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_AUTH_BACKEND=1`, `..._AUTH_URL`, `..._AUTH_SECRET` (or `runtime/convai-auth.json`); `..._AUTH_FALLBACK=0` removes the embedded-key fallback (default on; logged only in bridge.stderr.log; only used when the backend is unreachable, never when it refuses). A player's own key is untouched. Open: deploy steps are in `auth-backend/README.md` (CLI-driven); step 0 of the design doc (token on memory calls, revoke shape, mint limit) still needs one live check; a release step must write `convai-auth.json` with the signing secret into the package; run `Enable-CharacterMemory.mjs --apply` before players use backend mode.

**2026-10-13: settings flicker fixed and loading notices added (unit-tested; NOT yet seen in the game).** (1) Flicker, three causes: a drawn page was styled in batches over several frames, so default-coloured rows showed in between (the page is now hidden in the same frame it is drawn and shown when styling is done, at most 0.75 s, and always shown on an error or when the page stops being ours); the page was drawn again for states it does not show (voice loading/speaking, model scan timer), so it now carries a per-view signature of only what it shows and draws again only when that changes (the scan follows the number of models found); and the search observer re-armed a 6-frame redraw on every navigation (now only when the text changed). Closed-menu cost is unchanged (the signature is read on the existing 1 Hz tick). (2) Loading notices `load-progress`, `load-ready`, `load-failed` through the notice system: OmniVoice, Pocket and Local AI loads are announced after 1.5 s, shown again every 3 s until the ready line, or a failure line that says what to do; a load shorter than 1.5 s says nothing, a per-character voice preparation is not a load, and a fallback that has its own notice is not announced twice. Signals read: `local-voice-state.json` and (only while Local is the provider) `localAi` in `mistral-state.json`, once a second while idle, never per frame. No signal yet for Voxtral reference prep, the voice browser warm-up or Inworld (documented in `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md`, one `loadStatus` entry and two strings each when they have one). Ten new strings in 15 locales. Tests: manager (veil, redraw, models page, five loading cases), notices (loading board), mcm-fonts (`busy`). The default-provider texts now say Convai free tier (42b8efd, 253fae5).

**2026-10-13: fight on a tagged enemy (Distract removed) and companions crouch with V (branch `fight-crouch`; headless tests pass, NOT installed, NOT proven in game).** Fight: `mod/companion-marking.lua` reads every NPC within 100 m once per request (tag read tries `IsTaggedinFocusMode` then `IsTaggedInFocusMode`, errors logged), takes the closest tagged NPC first (neutral, civilian and crowd allowed; companions, children, vendors, invulnerable and friendly non-police never; named, quest-bound or scene-controlled only when hostile to V and in combat), then the aimed NPC with combat evidence, then the nearest NPC hostile to V; `probe.log` gets `Tactic candidates: N NPCs within 100m, T tagged, E tag reads failed (...), via <reader>; <name> <m>m tag=yes|no|err; ...` then `Tactic target: ...`. `mod/companion-tactics.lua` is fight only (offers reach 100 m), draws the right-hand weapon with `AIEquipCommand` when her hand is empty and reads it back (`Companion tactic: weapon draw requested`, `weapon readback: drawn (<record>)` or `hand still empty, ...`). Distract is gone: `tactic-perception.lua`, the distraction plumbing in `init.lua`, `distraction-context.mjs`, the `distracting` activity, manifests, docs. Crouch: `ObserveAfter('PlayerPuppet','OnLocomotionStateChanged')` stores a boolean; the 10 Hz companion tick calls `NPCPuppet.ChangeStanceState` on managed copies only, only from Stand, only stands from a crouch it set, re-checks every 0.5 s, stands her for combat, vehicles, lifts, workspots and tactics, three retries; lines read `Companion stance: ...`. **To check in game (install all committed `mod/` files, CET reload):** (1) Fight: tag a ganger with the scanner (also try one 60-90 m away and a civilian), ask Judy "go attack him": `Tactic candidates:` lists him with `tag=yes`, `Tactic target: tagged ...`, then `Companion tactic: fight fight-approach`, `weapon draw requested` or `weapon already drawn`, `weapon readback`, `fight fighting`. If he is missing or `tag=no`/`tag=err`, that line says why. Nobody tagged: face a hostile ganger (`aimed`/`nearest`). (2) Crouch: V crouches with Judy following: `Companion stance: crouch (judy-alvarez)`, she crouches; V stands: `Companion stance: stand`; crouch then enter a fight or a car: `stand - combat` / `stand - vehicle`. `Companion stance hook failed` or no line at all means the observer did not fire.

**2026-10-13: character editor for Mistral, Groq and Local AI (branch `character-editor`; built and tested headless, NOT live, NOT merged).** A web page (not an in-game editor, because the menu has no multi-line text entry and an in-game editor would cost per-frame draw work) opened from Settings > Edit characters. The bridge starts a loopback server on demand (random port, per-session token, Host and Origin checks, CSP nonce, 30 minute idle stop) and stops it on Close. Overrides live in `<state>/character-overrides/<key>.json` (changed fields only); the prompt adds "YOUR OWN RULES OF SPEECH" and "WHAT YOU KNOW AND REMEMBER" sections and is byte-identical with no override. Limits keep the prompt tight (bio 9,000, style 1,800, 10 rules, 20 examples, 12 memories, 14,000 in all). Picked-up memories and the summary can be edited or deleted; edited records are pinned. Not for Convai (page, banner and settings row say so). 71 new sentences in 15 languages. Tests: `npm run test:editor`. Merge note: touches `bridge/convai-bridge.mjs`, `package.json` and the packaging manifests, so expect conflicts with parallel branches.

**2026-10-13: v0.2 packaging: native plugin, two Local AI packs, pre-built Mistral references (dry run and clean-install test pass; NOT yet seen in the game from a package).** (1) The Runtime package ships `red4ext/plugins/CyberpunkConvaiNative/CyberpunkConvaiNative.dll` (built from `native/core` by the release build, MSVC; `--native PATH` and `--no-native` for local runs; the RED4ext.SDK and yyjson licences beside it) and `runtime/native-off.txt` with exactly `actuator`, `vehicles`, `chauffeur`, `passage`, `faces`. The plugin polls the file about once a second and a missing file means all on (`native/core/src/core/Switches.*`, `mod/native-core.lua`), so the file is part of the package. RED4ext was already in the README requirements; the uninstall list gained the Native folder. (2) Local AI packs: `localai-standard` (Gemma 4 E4B QAT q4_0, 5.15 GB model, split into 1 GB parts, joined and hash-checked on first start by `assemble-parts.mjs`) and `localai-lite` (Qwen3.5-2B Q4_K_M, 1.28 GB), each with llama.cpp b11560 Vulkan `llama-server` pruned to the server and its DLLs, the granite embedding model and a manifest fragment; every file pinned by URL, revision and SHA-256 in `packaging/release.config.json` and unmodified; the release workflow builds both in a matrix job. Licences: llama.cpp MIT, models Apache-2.0 (Google, Alibaba, IBM), LLVM OpenMP Apache-2.0 with LLVM exceptions; all redistributable with the licence text, which the packs carry (`packaging/licenses/`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`). The README gained the 6/8/12/16 GB table (the voice is kept before the text when short). Finding: the standard tier needs about 6 GB free to start although it measures about 3.1 GB (see Release blockers). (3) Pre-built Mistral references: `voxtralCache` in `packaging/build-release.mjs` runs the bridge's own `VoxtralVoice.build` for every voice and ships the slots in `voices/prepared/` (Runtime: Pocket clips, 256 voices, 10.64 MB; OmniVoice pack: 215 voices whose clip differs, 8.99 MB, 41 identical slots left out; sizes are for reference version 3, 24 kHz 40 kbit/s, rebuilt after `4931270` changed the settings from 16 kHz 32 kbit/s, which was 8.55 and 7.23 MB; the slot name includes the settings, so a build always carries the packaged bridge's own); `prepare-state.mjs` now sets `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_OMNI_REFERENCES` for every install (safe: the Omni worker is only built when the pack is installed, which supplies the other three paths). All 512 slots were checked to be cache hits for the bridge's preparer. Extract Runtime before the OmniVoice pack. (4) Results: `node --test packaging/release.test.mjs` 27 of 27; dry run from `268493c` into a scratch folder (Scripts 1.95 MB, Runtime 1.14 GB, Omni 6.37 GB in 4 parts, standard 5.31 GB in 5 parts, lite 1.43 GB); `test-release-install.mjs --local-ai` 26 of 26 (the packaged llama-server loaded all three models on the CPU; Pocket, lip sync and speech recognition ran from the packaged Python; no development launcher in any package). The two new characters' references and configs were uploaded to the bucket (`final/MANIFEST.json` updated, 782 files verified). `Refresh-ReleaseData.mjs` was not needed and must not be run blindly (it copied the untested gestures archive `36f54d4f` over `6966d508`; reverted). The build and install test only read the repository's `node_modules`; every build writes to its `--out` folder (use a scratch one).

**2026-10-13 (late): four live findings on the native core, fixed in branch `native-core` (DLL `7442124bd33c...`, plus CET reload; NOT yet proven in game).** (1) `vehicles=lua (mounting keeps answering unavailable)`: `GetMountingFacility`, `GetMappinSystem`, `GetScriptableSystemsContainer` (and `GetSceneSystem`, used by `faces`) are NOT global functions. Parsing `r6/cache/final.redscripts` (the format: header, six tables; names, name offsets, definitions of 20 bytes) shows each as a native static function of the script class `GameInstance` (native `ScriptGameInstance`) with one parameter `self`; the only globals that take a `GameInstance` are `GetPlayer`, `GetMainPlayer` and `GetPlayerObject`. The old lookup searched the globals only. `convai::game::GameSystem` now looks in the statics of `ScriptGameInstance` / `GameInstance`, then the globals, then all class functions, and else asks the instance's own system lookup for the native class; the route is logged (`Game access: GameInstance.GetMountingFacility resolved as ... (static function of class ScriptGameInstance)`). (2) `NPCPuppet.IsInCombat not found`: the game registers the static as `IsInCombat;ScriptedPuppet` on `NPCPuppet` itself (the compiler appends parameter types after a `;`; PlayerPuppet's member is `IsInCombat;`), so `GetFunction("IsInCombat")` finds nothing; the earlier claim that it lives on `ScriptedPuppet` was wrong (that is its parameter type). One matcher (`ScriptNameIs`, headless-tested) now serves `game::Method`, `game::StaticFunction`, `FindMember` and `vreflect::StaticFunction`, so the actuator and faces read the combat flag natively and the chauffeur's `P` section and `ProbeJson` get `IsInCombat` too; faces' `GetSceneSystem;GameInstance` global (also not a global) goes through `GameSystem`. The parity gate compares the combat field (new counters, proof needs it compared on every counted sense, `PARITY MISMATCH in combat`, proven line says who reads it). (3) Judy in a car: `seated-look-skip {"reason":"in a vehicle"}` is V's own camera widening (`mod/seated-look.lua`), not her gaze; her gaze has its own gates and none of them was logged, so the cause is not provable offline (the Oct 2 live pass used the same Lua with narrower limits). Changes: a `Companion recovery seated-gaze {...}` probe line when her seated attention changes (selected, gate, style, speed, error), seated limits widened (full 100/150/150, narrow 60/120/150; V is about 90 degrees off a passenger's axis, where the old 70/120 faded the look to about 60 percent), `calculatePositionInParentSpace` set for seated requests (as the game's in-vehicle look-at does), and speed bands (full to 8 m/s, narrow to 20, none above, 2 m/s margins). (4) `companions.update` 6.3 to 10.6 ms/s: from the perf snapshots the jump is Judy appearing (3 ms/s before her, 12.8 in the parity window, 12.6 proven, no drop after the proof at 21:03:28Z), and FPS drives the row (7.4 ms/s at 40-50 FPS, 14.5 below 30); proven native vs plain Lua is not separable from that data (other native systems differed between the sessions; matched-FPS rows showed proven native 1.5-2 ms/s higher, not a controlled comparison). Fixes: the combat read no longer falls back to Lua every tick, the window after the first 40 senses narrows to one sense in four while only walking is missing (a standing companion used to hold it open at twice the cost), and perf rows `tickNative.life/sense/commandState` make the A/B possible. Tests: ctest 64 + plugin_host (new: script names, seated plan), node 431 on the native, gaze, vehicle, chauffeur, passage and companion shims (new: combat proof, sparse window, seated bands, seated-gaze probe line). Detail: `docs/NATIVE_VEHICLES.md`, `docs/NATIVE_ACTUATOR.md`, `docs/COMPANION_GAZE.md`.

**2026-10-13: Judy's twisted legs (screenshot 02:52 IST, 21:22Z): the first diagnosis was wrong and is reverted (388cc77, revert 2849208); cause still unproven, no behaviour changed.** I blamed Round 6b's `judy_own` talk loop (her ambient idle is 37.6 cm in foot position from every arms-crossed clip it plays); the user says the twist predates Oct 11, so that mapping is not the cause. The user's description is a re-face: she turned to V, V moved, V looked at her again, she re-faced. Checked in code: while any workspot holds her no turn or step command is issued (`companions.lua` skips `actuator.apply` while `bodyAnimation.observe` holds; a stance or idle is only entered from `settled`/`attend` with a hold motor and `facingError` <= 35, so no turn is in flight at entry; the device is placed at her yaw; the only teleports are the floor settle, vehicles and recovery; LookAt is head and chest only), so no path rotates her over planted feet. What does happen after the workspot exit: `probe.log` 2026-10-12 21:20:31Z `exit movement` (hip idle `generic_hips`), `movement-trace.ndjson` (trace clock about 975-979, mapped from the end of the session) `settled`, then `attend_step` (a small walking step) 0.6 s later, `attend_settle`, `attend`; i.e. the re-face step starts the moment the exit finishes, while the exit pose blend (0.5-1.4 s) is still running. That is the leading candidate and it is NOT proven; not changed (rare, evidence bar). To catch it: capture the legs at 34 fps through an exit-then-step; if it is the cause, hold the attend step until the blend is over.

**2026-10-13: the free cloud path, three live problems (bridge only; tests pass; NOT yet seen live, needs a bridge restart; the reference re-prepares each character once, about 0.4 s).** (1) One reply, one voice: the first-sentence head and the tail each fell back to Pocket on their own, so a Voxtral spike (5 to 11 s) on the tail made Pocket speak it mid-reply. Now the head decides the engine (`bridge/voice-utterance.mjs`), the tail follows it, is requested as soon as its text is known and held until the head is known to be Voxtral, gets 6 s then 10 s (one ask again), and if it fails after the head played the reply ends after the head and is logged; only a failure before any audio sends a whole reply to Pocket. The 02:42:19 routing line was the previous reply's state read before voicing; it now shows `cloudLast=`. Harness (9 s spike on the tail): old tail in Pocket, new tail in Voxtral at the same moment. (2) Muffled: the clone has only the reference's bandwidth, and the 16 kHz reference (32 kbit/s, versions 1 and 2) capped answers near 7.5 kHz (8 to 12 kHz bands 55 dB down against 22 to 24 dB for the whole original clip); 64 kbit/s or WAV at 16 kHz is just as dull, the sample rate decides. Reference version 3 keeps 24 kHz and 40 kbit/s: +11 KB (about +11 ms, not measurable in the medians), bandwidth 10.5 kHz. Our pipeline is not the cause (only float32 to int16, null SNR 85 dB). **2026-10-13: three live native-core bugs and a docs bug (branch `native-core`; DLL plus CET reload; NOT yet proven in game).** (1) `gaze=lua (class LookAtAddEvent not found)`: `CRTTISystem::GetClass` knows only native names; the game class is `entLookAtAddEvent` (script alias `LookAtAddEvent`). Gaze uses the native name, and `game::Class` falls back to `GetClassByScriptName`. (2) `clearance=lua (the spatial queries system was not found)`: the native name is `gameSpatialQueriesSystem`, and the system exists only while a world is loaded, so the self-test now only waits (Lua answers; retried every 2 s, every 10 s after 15 waits) and fails at once only when the class itself is missing. (3) Actuator (Judy did not follow up stairs, stopped short): every value of the plugin matches the Lua path in the fixtures (maths, wire format, label, argument order), so the live cause is unproven. Delivered: the plugin's reads are compared with the Lua reads for the first 40 senses (10 while walking) and only then used, with a canary every 100th sense; any difference logs `Native core: actuator PARITY MISMATCH <field>: plugin X, Lua Y` and the third switches the actuator off. `runtime/native-verify.txt` keeps Lua in use and compares forever; `actuator.life|sense|state` lines in `native-off.txt` bisect. The per-tick `IsInCombat` warning is now logged once (combat stays a Lua read). (4) The CET console cannot reach `convaiNative` (each CET mod has its own environment): docs and comments now name only the `native-off.txt` / `native-verify.txt` files. Tests: tick-native 14, clearance-native 23, native-core 17; ctest passes.

Every game function name is looked up through RTTI at run time and none has run in the game yet, so the first live session is the proof (the merge onto the all-or-nothing registration and the audit of the names are the next paragraph); detail, wire formats and the A/B in `docs/NATIVE_VEHICLES.md`. Also fixed in the vehicles shim: the empty-answer run is counted per call (a good `mounting` answer no longer hides an `inspect` that is never answered).

**2026-10-13: vehicle group merged onto the current `native-core`; names audited against the game (one DLL now carries every system; NOT live).** Registration: the three classes use `StaticClass(name, system)` and plain `Static()` calls, so `rtti::CommitAll` checks every parameter and return type (`String`, `Bool`, `Int32`, `Double`, `handle:gameObject`, `handle:IScriptable`; no `array:Double`) and trips a system whole when one is missing; the per-class `TypesExist` helpers are gone. Names checked against the RED4ext SDK headers (native `NAME` against script `ALIAS`) and the strings of `r6/cache/final.redscripts`: the enums `gamePSMVehicle`, `gamedataVehicleType`, `gameAutodriveDestinationType`, `gameAutodriveLaneValidityResult` and the struct `gamemountingMountingInfo` are the native names; `NPCPuppet`, `PlayerPuppet`, `AutoDriveSystem`, `AIVehicleCommand` and `AIVehicleDriveToPointAutonomousCommand` have no alias; `DynamicEntitySystem.IsReady/IsManaged/IsTagged` are Codeware natives. Two real bugs found by that audit, both fixed: (1) `CClass::GetFunction(shortName)` answers the first function of that name, statics before members, so overloads such as `IsAttached`/`IsVehicle`/`IsDestroyed`/`IsInCombat` (member and static versions under one short name) can resolve to a function with other parameters; this is also why the live actuator logged `NPCPuppet.IsInCombat is not a one-argument static function` (the nearest `IsInCombat` is a member, the static `IsInCombat(puppet)` is declared on `ScriptedPuppet`). The vehicle group now resolves members by name AND parameter count (`vreflect::FindMember`, nearest class first, statics skipped), statics from the nearest declaring class (`StaticFunction`), refuses an argument list that does not fill the parameters, caches only hits and logs what the class does have on a miss. NOT applied to the actuator (`ActuatorGame.cpp` `CombatFunction` still uses `GetFunction`): the same fix would let the plugin read the combat flag there. (2) The three global functions (`GetMountingFacility`, `GetMappinSystem`, `GetScriptableSystemsContainer`) are tried as `<name>;GameInstance` and then by short name with one `ScriptGameInstance` parameter among all global functions; the resolved name is logged. Shims: passage's self-test wait no longer fails after five waits (same rule as clearance: wait, retry every 2 s then 10 s); all three honour `runtime/native-verify.txt` (`api.verifying`); every doc and comment names only the file switches. Tests: node 21 (vehicles), 18 (passage) and 28 (chauffeur) shim tests (new: native-verify.txt in each, passage waits, a source contract on the vehicle group), ctest 61 + plugin_host.

**2026-10-11: daily cap and abuse protection for the project's shared Convai key (built and unit-tested; NOT live: bridge restart plus CET reload for the Lua and locale files; the dev checkout is not limited, see below).** Turns on the packaged key are limited to 100 per local calendar day (`DAILY_LIMIT`), counted once per turn at the single choke point where turns reach Convai (`RealtimeHost`: typed, spoken, Phone Chat, each group speaker, look-ahead turns and automatic reactions; a microphone turn is checked at its start and counted at its finish; connection opens and warm-ups are not counted). A token bucket (15 at once, 12 a minute) and 250 ms spacing apply on top. The count is kept in two HMAC-signed copies (`daily-usage.json` in the state folder and under `%LOCALAPPDATA%\CyberpunkConvai`), so deleting one does not reset it; a damaged or edited copy is ignored and never blocks the player (count 0, rate limit still on). At the cap the bridge refuses the turn before any connection is opened (`[convai-quota:daily]`, reply code `daily-limit`); the game shows one `daily-limit` notice per session (Convai key recommended, or a free Mistral key, Settings > Development) and Phone Chat words it on its status row; battle and loot reactions stop silently. Settings > Development shows "The shared plan: N of 100 left today" and the sidebar of "Use my own Convai API key" explains both free options. A player's own Convai key and other providers are never counted or limited. The package key is now stored scrambled (`convai-key-v1`, `bridge/key-codec.mjs`; the release build round-trip-checks it) and only a packed key is treated as the shared one, so a plain `runtime/convai-key.txt` (development) and a player's own `convai-key.txt` are unlimited. To test the limit with a dev key: `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_DAILY_LIMIT=3`; `off` or `0` disables it; the packaged launcher strips the variable. Honest limits: the bridge ships as readable source and the key ships inside the package, so editing the code, deobfuscating the key or deleting both counter copies is not stopped; there is no port that accepts turns (file mailbox in the game folder), so no token was added. Tests: `bridge/shared-key-guard.test.mjs`, extended manager, phone-chat, supervisor and packaging tests.

**2026-10-11: Mistral as an alternative text provider (bridge + settings; BRIDGE RESTART plus CET reload; UNVERIFIED in game, prompts checked offline; `docs/MISTRAL.md`).** A player who used up the shared Convai conversations can add a free Mistral key (Settings > Development: Conversation provider = Mistral (free plan), Paste Mistral key, Remove my Mistral key; key in `player-mistral-key.txt` in the state folder, never logged or sent anywhere but api.mistral.ai) and companions answer through Mistral. (An OpenRouter route was built first and removed the same day: its free tier is 50 requests a day.) New `bridge/mistral-{client,key,prompt,memory,host}.mjs`: streaming SSE client with retries and mapped errors (`mistral-key`, `-limit`, `-model` become notices), the system prompt built from the same character data Convai gets (bio, appearance, style, examples, first-person and format rules) plus the exact live context string the bridge sends Convai, a bounded local memory (last 8 exchanges per character and channel, a summary of older ones written by the same model and queued behind the reply, cleared by Forget), and `attachMistral`, which makes the realtime hosts use the route while the provider is active. Replies go through the same `cleanReply` (Natural speech and Expressive emotions), subtitles, Phone Chat, Pocket/Omni, lip sync and group rounds; a Convai cloud voice is replaced by Pocket with a one-time notice; no key falls back to Convai with a one-time notice; the voice key shows "needs Convai" unless the local speech-to-text hook says available. Models, best first: `mistral-small-latest`, `ministral-14b-latest`, `ministral-8b-latest`. MEASURED on the user's free key: Small and Medium answer 429 with a request allowance of 0 (`x-ratelimit-limit-req-minute: 0`) and Large is 403, so the host skips a zero-allowance model for 10 minutes and the Ministral models answer (14b: 30 requests a minute, 8b: 188, 3b: 750), replies in about 1.5 s. Offline prompt test (`scripts/Test-MistralPrompts.mjs`, transcripts in `runtime/mistral-prompt-tests/`, 24 turns per run): in character, short, no tag or AI leaks, but the small Ministral models still invent small details and "same as always" history now and then (the rules and a closing reminder reduce it). Worth re-running on a plan that allows Small. Tests: mistral-{client,prompt,memory,key,host}.test.mjs (SSE parser, error mapping, prompt assembly and caps, memory, provider switching and fallback, key never logged, zero-allowance model fallback, pacing, pipeline handoff with a fake server).

**2026-10-11: Local speech-to-text for the voice key on the Mistral route (bridge + packaging; BRIDGE RESTART; UNVERIFIED in game; `docs/LOCAL_STT.md`).** Moonshine Tiny English int8 (MIT, 27 M parameters) run by sherpa-onnx 1.13.6 (Apache-2.0) on the CPU, in the Pocket Python. Chosen by measurement on 26 clips / 130 s (game actors, LibriSpeech, Windows voices with game names): WER 11.4-11.7%, final decode p50 0.18 s / p95 0.28 s on 2 threads and 0.45 / 0.67 s on 1, against faster-whisper tiny.en 12.0% at 0.82 s, base.en 10.4% at 1.9 s, small.en 7.6% at 8 s, a streaming Zipformer 12.7% (game 28%) and Moonshine Base with no gain for 3x the size. Default is ONE thread (onnxruntime pools spin-wait: 2 threads cost 3x the CPU for 30% less time), below-normal priority, live words from a decode of the newest 8 s every 1.2 s at about 15% duty, and a live decode that already covered the recording is reused as the final text (about 3 ms). No process runs outside a recording; the worker quits after 90 s idle. Audio: `bridge/local-stt-capture.mjs` opens a 16 kHz mono AudioWorklet in the hidden Chrome (served from memory, mic granted per context), 100 ms Int16 chunks to the bridge; the existing `MicrophoneSession` states, notices, 700 ms minimum, spam handling and `microphone-heard.json` words run unchanged, and an empty transcript becomes the existing no-speech notice. Wired as `mistralSpeech` in `convai-bridge.mjs` (`createLocalSpeech`); the Convai route never starts it. Model in the HF bucket `final/stt-model/moonshine-tiny-en-int8/` (124 MB, manifest updated by `scripts/Upload-SttModel.py`), `release.config.json` group, `models/stt` in the package, wheels in `pocket.lock.txt`, notices, closure lists, install test transcribes the Pocket sentence. Tests: `local-stt.test.mjs` (16) and `local-stt-capture.test.mjs` (12, includes Chrome's fake mic through the real worker); bench: `node scripts/Bench-LocalStt.mjs --synth --live`. Live check: Provider Mistral, press the voice key, say a sentence with a name, press again: the words show while speaking and the reply follows; silence gives the no-speech notice.

**2026-10-11: V no longer keeps walking after the talk box: the box waits until no movement key is held (redscript; GAME RESTART, plus CET reload for the Lua half; UNVERIFIED live).** Cause (user confirmed it is the text talk box, not the phone): a movement key released while the Codeware popup owns input never reaches gameplay, so the axis still reads held after the box closes and V walks on until the key is pressed again. No retail API to release input was found (and none is used), so the fix keeps the key-up from being lost: `Composer.reds` `ConvaiMoveWatch` is registered on the player from `OnGameAttached` (removed in `OnDetach`) for exactly two actions, `MoveX` and `MoveY` (`r6/config/inputContexts.xml` MovementActions: A/D and W/S plus the left stick). The game calls it only when an axis value changes, it stores the two values (held = either above 0.15) and consumes nothing, so there is no per-frame work. `init.lua`: the talk hotkeys (F6/F7 and the group one) ask `player:ConvaiMoveHeld()`; if a direction is held, the box is not opened but the request waits (`convaiTalkGate`, serviced from onUpdate only while a request waits) until the axis is released, at most 1.5 s. At the cap the box opens anyway (chosen over a hint or a refusal: a talk request is never dropped, and no new localised text is needed), the stuck reading is forgotten (`ConvaiMoveForget`) so the next press is not delayed, and in that one case a key released inside the box can still be lost (known issue stays in `packaging/PLAYER_README.md` until verified). A second press while waiting only changes private/group; the wait is dropped on menu, pause or world change. `Composer opened:` log line keeps `speed=` and adds `moveAxis=held|free|n/a` (n/a = redscript tracker not installed, the box then opens at once) and `waited=` when it waited; `Talk box waits for the movement keys to be released` is logged when a press is deferred. Tests: `phone-input.test.mjs` (tracker shape and the two named registrations, Lua gate: immediate open, wait then open once, cap with forget, second press, world drop). Live check: hold D, press the talk hotkey, release D: the box opens at once on release (probe.log `moveAxis=free waited=0.xx`); hold D past 1.5 s and the box opens anyway; walk with D, tap the hotkey after releasing, type, close the box, V must stand still. If the tracker shows `moveAxis=held` while standing, a key-up was missed earlier (menu, vehicle): the next press waits 1.5 s once and clears it.

**2026-10-11: a locked state file no longer stalls companion preparation (bridge; restart the bridge).** Report: "Alex could not prepare: Companion preparation is not responding" with `bridge.stderr.log` lines `Preparation poll: EPERM: operation not permitted, rename '...companion-invitations-state.json.<uuid>.tmp'` between 14:40 and 14:48 IST. Cause: `guarded-file.mjs` `atomicFile` (the shared writer of every game-facing state file) retried the rename only 5 times over 80 ms; when the game's Lua reader or an indexer held `companion-invitations-state.json` longer, it threw, `CompanionInvitations.publishSnapshot` passed the error up, and `pollPrefetch` aborted before it ran the rest of the poll, so the receipt aged out and `invitation-readiness.lua` gave up after 15 s. (`memory.prepareCharacter` itself writes nothing, so it is not involved; the two orphan `.tmp` files in the game runtime folder were from 5 and 10 Oct and are deleted.) Fix: `atomicFile` now tries the rename 6 times with a 10/20/40/80/150 ms backoff (about 300 ms), retries EPERM, EACCES and EBUSY, keeps the old file and removes its temp file (with `rm` retries) when it still fails; `publishSnapshot` catches a failed or throwing write, returns false and counts it (`publishFailures`, `lastPublishError` in the invitation diagnostic), so the poll goes on and the next one writes the fresh receipt. Tests: `guarded-file` (EPERM twice then success, always failing keeps the old file and leaves no temp, default backoff timing), `companion-invitations` (poll survives failed and throwing writes, keeps its stage and writes again). Not changed: private registry writes (`player-memory.json`, `knowledge-service`) have their own writers and are not read by the game.

**2026-10-11: armed-clone spawn safety: every spawn logs its record, a clone that cannot be created falls back to the original record, and `runtime/armed-clones-off` switches the clone off (Lua; CET reload).** Report: "Alex does not spawn after the armed clone". Finding: the invite never reached the spawn code. `runtime/mcm.log` 09:10:22Z shows `Preparing connection to Alex... [Alex=memory]` and at 09:11:00Z / 09:11:21Z `Alex could not prepare: Companion preparation is not responding` (`invitation-readiness.lua`: the bridge receipt did not move past the `memory` stage within 15 s). Spawn (`companions.spawn`, where `companion-records.lua` runs) happens only after preparation is `ready`, so neither the armed clone nor `b469c32` ran. The bridge was alive (receipts refreshed every second, ready since 09:09:49Z); the stuck step is the bridge's `memory.prepareCharacter` (a Convai memory call), so look at the bridge/Convai side or retry once the bridge has warmed up; the 08:42 restart worked 3 minutes after starting, this attempt came 33 s after the game adapter started. NOT proven to be timing. Defensive changes anyway: `companion-records.lua` logs `Companion spawn record: ...` (original, `Armed_<name>`, or `armed clone failed (reason)`) to probe.log; `companions.lua` retries `CreateEntity` with the original record when the clone cannot be created; creating `runtime/armed-clones-off` (or `M.armedClones = false`) disables the clone with no code change. Tests: `companion-records` (log lines, fallback, switch, failed write). If Alex spawns but still flees, or fails to appear right after a `Armed_` line, create the switch file and reload CET.

**2026-10-11: Alex ran away when V was attacked: spawned companions whose record has no shooting abilities now get a private armed clone (Lua; CET reload plus RE-INVITE the companion).** Cause (evidence, `runtime/movement-trace.ndjson` 08:54Z): both Alex copies were in native combat (`brain.phase = combat`, our command `release`, nothing of ours active) and sprinted straight away from V at about 5 m/s, then stood at 25-30 m; `Automatic battle reaction` released with `speaker-missing`. In the REDmod tweaks `alex : Quest_NPC_Base` has no `primaryEquipment` and only `CanCrouch`/`CanSprint` (the shooting and hand abilities come from `Gameplay_NPC_Base`), the same defect as Johnny on 29 Sep (`docs/COMPANION_COMBAT.md`). Not causes (checked): reaction preset (`NoReaction`, same as Judy), attitude and squad (`AIFollowerRole.OnRoleSet` sets V's group; she did reach the Combat state), our follow/hold commands (combat releases them), gestures (exit on combat), crowd flags (`attachFollower` rejects crowd copies). Fix: `companion-records.lua` `armed()`: any record (not Johnny, not Kerry) with none of `CanShootWhileMoving`, `CanCloseCombat`, `HasChargeJump` is cloned to `CyberpunkConvai.Armed_<name>` with its own abilities plus Judy's ranged set, archetype, Chao pistol and secondary equipment, readback checked; a failed clone or unreadable record leaves the original in use. The game's no-weapon combat branch is the likely flee trigger, but that link is inferred (Johnny fixed the same way, live "Johnny fights now"), not read from a script, and the effect on Alex is UNVERIFIED live. Other quest-base roster characters (an estimate from the tweak chains: many) get the same loadout; check the probe log for `Armed_` records. Tests: `companion-records` (Alex clone, union of abilities, one reuse, game record untouched, capable/unreadable records left alone, failed write falls back). Live test: dismiss and re-invite Alex, get attacked by gangers with her nearby; she should draw the Chao pistol and fight (she is tagged Invulnerable, so she cannot die).

**2026-10-11 (Alex 2 spawned a little up and floated while talking: relocations and tall-navmesh spawns now go to the Static floor; Lua only; CET reload; needs a fresh invite or a relocation to see it).** Log 08:40-08:54Z (Alex, serial 1 and 2, trace z compared with V's 7.156): there was no new `relocated` line with a floor value; every relocation target in the log is a round navmesh value: `fast-travel` to z 7.3000007 (08:49:37Z, floor 7.156, so 14 cm up) and 9.4000006 (08:19:09Z), `blocked` to 11.4496, `stance` to 10.9515, `lift-arrival` to 8.4000034 and 49.500022 (08:13:56Z: she was still at 49.5000 ten seconds later, 30 cm over the 49.2 floor). Cause: `placement.find` returns the navmesh point plus 10 cm and `relocate` teleported her there with `actuator.teleport` without the floor correction of `6804d73`; an AI-driven copy keeps the height she is put at until she first walks, so a stance played meanwhile floats her and the exit drops her (Round 5g). The fresh-spawn correction also missed the apartment case: the spot is navmesh plus 10 cm, so a navmesh 30 cm over the floor is 40 cm over it and the 5-35 cm window rejected it (she was then lowered by `settleOnFloor` half a second after attach, a visible hop). Fix (`mod/companions.lua`): `floorBelow` takes a window; fresh copies and `relocate` use 5-45 cm for a placement spot (`placeDrop`), and `relocate` puts the target on that Static floor (one ray per relocation, on demand; `lift-board` keeps the spot, a car floor is not Static ground; if the frame budget is spent it retries the same plan twice, then places her as found). Same out-of-view rules, nothing per frame. Not changed, on purpose: the gesture workspot device stays at her entity position (the stance never moves her, entity z is constant through it, Round 5g; a device on the floor under a floating entity would pull her down and the exit would put her back); not covered: `carTeleport` (lift car floor), seating stand-up teleports, and the party-car `recover` in `companion-vehicle-native.lua`, which is still a plain transform teleport at navmesh plus 10 cm (undone by the AI, not touched today). Tests: `bridge/companion-placement.test.mjs` (spot 40 cm over the floor is created on it, 50 cm is not), `bridge/companion-passage.test.mjs` (a floor-change relocation lands on the floor, and keeps the spot when no floor is found). Open: confirm live after a CET reload on a fresh invite and after a fast travel or a floor change (z in the movement trace should be the floor, and a stance should neither lift her nor drop her at the exit).

**2026-10-11: Fight/Distract finally find a target: a marked or aimed crowd-flagged gang member is accepted, the look-at can no longer be blocked by a companion, and the character is told when there is no target (Lua = CET reload; `bridge/` = BRIDGE RESTART).** Live evidence (probe.log 08:50Z): `Tactic target: none; skipped aimed-companion=1 companion=1 crowd=2` (also `crowd=19`, `15`, `1`), `companion-actions.json` stayed `{"action":"none"}`. Breaks found along text intent -> bridge action -> `companion-actions.json` -> Lua target -> combat command: (1) `companion-marking.lua` `verdict` refused every `IsCrowd` NPC, and ambient gangers are crowd-flagged, so the tagged ganger was dropped. Now a crowd flag alone disqualifies only the nearest-NPC guess; a tagged or aimed crowd NPC is judged on the same evidence as anyone else (needs ganger, police, cyberpsycho, machine, armed or, new, already hostile to V; civilians, children, vendors, invulnerable, story-controlled, friendly non-police, protected and our own companions are still refused). (2) The aimed step took only the game's look-at, which returned the companion standing in the line of sight (`aimed-companion=1`); it now falls back to the eligible NPC closest to the crosshair line (12 degree cone, 30 m, one `GetCrosshairData` read per request). (3) With no valid target `T.offer` returned unavailable, so the bridge armed nothing and the model had no hint, free to say "on it". The offer now carries `code = 'no-target'`; `gameplay-actions.lua` capture sets `context.tacticUnavailable`, `protocol.mjs` and `companion-actions.mjs` carry it (strict parse, never with a real tactic offer, private chat only) and the context tells the character to decline in character and ask V to tag or aim at an enemy. (4) The log now says why a mark was refused: `Tactic target: none; skipped ...; tagged <name> rejected: <reason> [<signals>]`. Precedence unchanged: tagged beats the crosshair beats nearest. The combat command (`native.startFight`: hostile attitude both ways, `AddThreat` persistent, `SetThreatPersistence` with `CommandInjectThreat`) matches the game's own `AIInjectCombatThreatCommand` in `vendor/reference-game-scripts` (attitude, AddThreat, persistence) but has NEVER run in game, so it is still unverified live; a crowd target's own reaction (flee) is also unverified. `IsCharacterGanger`/`IsCrowd` are native imports with no script body, so whether a crowd gang member reads as `ganger` is only checkable live (the new rejection log line shows the signals if not). Tests: `companion-tactics` (verdict, crowd tag, civilian/protected/companion, cone, no-target code), `gameplay-actions` (advisory capture), `companion-tactic-actions` (bridge parse, window, text), `protocol`. Live test: tag a ganger in the scanner (or aim at one), say "attack him"; the companion should walk or run to him and fight; `Companion tactic: fight fight-approach` appears in probe.log. With nobody valid marked she should decline and ask you to mark someone.

**2026-10-11: typing no longer swallows key-ups; talk box and phone typing leave speed evidence in probe.log (`PhoneChat.reds` = GAME RESTART; `mod/phone-input.lua`, `mod/phone-chat.lua`, `mod/init.lua` = CET reload).** Bug (live): after typing to an NPC V kept strafing right until D was pressed once. (1) Our phone-typing guards consumed presses AND releases (`PhoneChat.reds` `OnAction`/`OnHandleMenuInput`, `mod/phone-input.lua` overrides on `gameuiInGameMenuGameController`). Now every press is still consumed, but a release is consumed only when it is one of the menu actions that controller acts on (verified in `vendor/reference-game-scripts`: `RegisterInputListenersForPlayer` lists OpenPauseMenu, OpenMapMenu, OpenCraftingMenu, OpenJournalMenu, OpenPerksMenu, OpenInventoryMenu, OpenHubMenu, QuickSave, QuickLoad, FastForward_Hold; the pointer callback is `OnPostOnRelease`, so every event there is a key-up and only back/toggle_* are swallowed; the phone popup keeps cancel, phone_open_journal, up_button, down_button, click). A blanket pass-through of releases was NOT used: the in-game controller opens the pause menu and journal ON RELEASE, so Esc/J would open menus while typing. Honest limit: those two controllers only register menu actions, so they never saw D at all; this part is a guard, and the likelier cause is (2). (2) The talk box (Codeware `GenericMessageNotification.ShowInput`) switches the input context, so gameplay can miss a key-up that happens while it owns input. NOT fixed: no verifiable API. `ReleaseAllInput`/`FakeReleaseButton` exist only in Codeware's dump of the dev-only `FunctionalTestsGameSystemInterface`, unproven in retail, so not called; deferring the open until movement stops cannot work (V pushing into a wall has speed 0 with D held) and reading held keys needs a per-event listener. (3) Evidence: `Composer opened: ... speed=<m/s>` (`mod/init.lua`, one `GetVelocity` read per open) and `Phone chat typing opened: contact=... speed=...` (rising edge inside the existing 10 Hz phone tick, only while a chat is open). A recurrence shows whether V was moving when the box opened. Tests: new `bridge/phone-input.test.mjs` (9, in `npm test`); isolated redscript compile passes. Live check: hold D, open the talk box or phone chat, release D while open, close it; V should stop. If V still drifts after the talk box closes, it is the input-context loss in (2) and probe.log shows the speed at open.

**2026-10-11: the mod never touches game-placed characters (world follow dropped; Lua + docs; CET reload; no bridge change).** Decision from the user for release 0.2. (1) World NPCs can no longer join the party: `companion-adoption.lua` offers nothing (the character gets the existing `unavailable` decline advisory, so a pedestrian says no in character) and `queueFollow` refuses; the adoption code is kept behind `allowWorldFollow`, set only by tests. Leave V's Party stays for spawned companions. (2) A named or story character the game placed gets no Convai at all: `profileIdentity` passes `world = true` and `characterCatalog.resolve` returns no profile for a roster record or name (the real Judy; our spawned copy still resolves through its managed key), never falls back to an archetype or resident for a quest-flagged, `q###/sq###/mq###` or MaxTac record. That one lookup covers the talk keys, voice, group, auto-connect scan and look counting, the distract voice and the game-dialogue speaker lookup. (3) New central predicate `convaiWorldPolicy` (`ours`, `protected`, cached per entity, on demand): the tactic target (`companion-marking.lua`) and the perception/attitude changes near it (`tactic-perception.lua`) skip protected characters; `beginFocus` (look-at, bark muting) refuses anything that is neither ours nor a talk-path profile; the passive gaze response now applies to our companions only (an ordinary NPC is faced toward V when a conversation starts, not when V merely looks at it). Auto-connect is unchanged (a bridge warm-up, no game-side effect). Cars, gestures, idle pools, seating and recovery only iterate our own party entries. Not changed, for the user to confirm: Fight/Distract still target ordinary hostile NPCs (a tactic needs an enemy), and a Side/Minor-quest-flagged vendor can still be talked to (the vendor exception) but nothing else touches it. `packaging/PLAYER_README.md` no longer says pedestrians can follow. Tests: `bridge/world-policy.test.mjs` (new), `character-catalog`, `world-companions`, `companion-tactics`. Live: see the open item "Game-placed characters are untouched".

**2026-10-11: release scheme is now 0.N (0.1, 0.2, ...); today's release is 0.2, tag v0.2; hotfixes are 0.2.1.** The user dropped the 2077 ("keep it simple like 0.1, 0.2"). `v2077.1` (Oct 7, the private repository's first build) stays untouched. `packaging/lib/version.mjs` accepts 0.N, 0.N.M and -rc forms (legacy 2077.N still passes), npmVersion maps 0.2 to 0.2.0; `release.test.mjs`, the workflow description, the packaging usage comments, `docs/PACKAGING.md` and the "What's new in 0.2" heading in `PLAYER_README.md` follow. Dispatch command: `gh workflow run release.yml --repo alystria-ai/cyberpunk-convai --ref main -f version=0.2 -f omni=true -f publish=true`. **2026-10-11: the character now knows what V is wearing and carrying, as background (built and unit-tested; read live once with a read-only probe, NOT yet checked in a real talk turn: **CET reload** for `mod/player-gear.lua` (new), `mod/init.lua`, `mod/group-chat.lua`, `mod/microphone.lua`; **bridge restart** for `bridge/player-gear.mjs`, `protocol.mjs`, `quest-context.mjs`).** Request: "in dynamic context see if you can also feed information about the gear the player has on himself currently ... she doesnt have to reference them unless needed". Every manual in-person turn (text, voice, group) now carries one short line in the dynamic context: visible clothing by slot (head, face, outer torso, inner torso, legs, feet, outfit), the weapon in hand plus the three weapon slots, and arms/eyes/operating-system cyberware, with the guidance "Background only: don't mention V's gear unless it's relevant or V asks." (`docs/PLAYER_GEAR_CONTEXT.md`). Read on demand only: `mod/player-gear.lua` reads `EquipmentSystemPlayerData` when a turn is built, cached 5 s, reset on load/death, no frame hook; every native call is pcall-guarded and a failure just leaves the line out. Clothing uses `GetVisualItemInSlot` (follows an active wardrobe outfit or transmog) with the equipped item as fallback. Verified live with a throwaway read-only probe through the gesture-probe mailbox (`runtime/gear-read.lua`, no talk turn): `GetPlayerData`, `GetItemInEquipSlot`, `GetVisualItemInSlot`, `GetActiveItem` and `GetNumberOfSlots` return real items and names (`Game.GetLocalizedTextByKey(record:DisplayName())` gave "Thermal Mantis Blades", "Kiroshi \"Stalker\" Optics", "Erebus"); `GetActiveWeapon` was nil with the weapon holstered (the hand entry is then simply absent); an active wardrobe outfit was not tested (the wardrobe index read INVALID, no outfit set). Not sent on automatic reactions (barks) or Phone Chat (a contact cannot see V). Capped at 900 characters (cyberware dropped first). No setting added (always on; say so if it should get an MCM toggle like "Continue from game dialogue"). Tests: `bridge/player-gear.test.mjs` (17), wired into `npm test`; the two manifests (`packaging/release-plan.json`, `scripts/development-files.json`) and `packaging/runtime-layout.mjs` list the new files. Live check: ask a companion "what am I wearing?" and expect the right jacket/weapons; ordinary chat must not mention gear.

**2026-10-11: phone Chat first message was slow because everything opened after Enter; it now opens while V types (built offline, NOT yet live: **CET reload** for `mod/phone-chat.lua`, **bridge restart**).** Report: the first phone text message takes long, later ones are fine; would Convai's core text API be faster? Path: the phone uses the realtime (WebRTC) route on its own `phoneRealtime` host with `ttsEnabled: false`, `voice: false`; nothing is spoken, so there is no TTS or audio wait. Measured offline (`runtime/convai-latency-test-user.json`, character alex, one fixed throwaway end user, nothing through the game bridge): `/character/list` 1.6-3.3 s (2.2 MB) plus `/character/get` 1.5 s (the memory check, once per character per bridge run; invited companions had it done at the invitation), browser start 0.6 s, page plus realtime connection 4.9-6.3 s, reply 1.2-1.4 s: a cold first message is about 8.3 s without the memory check and 11-13 s with it; the same page's second message is 1.2 s. `bridge-latency.json` has no phone rows (it records talk turns only); the in-game phone turns of 13:15 IST (Mama Welles) have no timing in any log. The old REST `getResponse` (`bridge/convai-client.mjs`) answered HTTP 429 and has no end-user id (no long-term memory, no context update), so it is not a drop-in and was not pursued. Change: `phone-chat.lua` publishes `chatContact` (the open Chat, known contacts only, at once on open or change, plus the 3 s heartbeat; one small file write, nothing per frame); new `bridge/phone-warm.mjs` (600 ms dwell, one contact at a time, at most 4 opens per visit, 15 s keep-alive for up to 5 minutes, one unused warm page kept) drives `PlayerMemory.prepareCharacter` and `phoneRealtime.warm` with the same route and transport identity as the message (`knowledgeRouter.configFor(profile, 'phone')`; a test proves the same `transportKey` as `prepare`), and the message claims that page (`phoneWarm.claim`). Nothing is sent, no session or memory write happens; skipped for single-session plans, talk turns, recordings, resets, ambient routes, and when memory was just enabled (the message does that refresh); a failure leaves the old behaviour. Also added `bridge/phone-warm.mjs` to `packaging/runtime-layout.mjs` and `packaging/release-plan.json` (the import-closure test requires it). Tests: `bridge/phone-warm.test.mjs` (19), 3 new in `bridge/phone-chat.test.mjs`; full `npm test` otherwise unchanged. Check live: open a Chat and wait 6-10 s before sending; the first reply should take about as long as a later one; probe.log has no new line, bridge stderr prints `[CyberpunkConvai] Phone warm-up: <message>` if a warm-up fails.

Same log, arrival: `lift-car-spot` `in-view` (the hidden rider's spot behind V was in view), so the navmesh plan placed her 4 m out of view (`relocated` plan 2, watched false); 1 s later the presence rule logged `unseen-teleport` `not-aboard` because V was still in the car: that is a placement only when V looks away, never in view, so not a violation, but it can move her a second time while V is about to step out (not changed).

What kept her from riding: nothing of ours pins her; the car does not carry an NPC (2026-10-08 trace: she stayed at z 49.2 while V rose 41 m) and the per-frame `TeleportationFacility` carry is applied late by the engine, so she trailed the car by speed times latency (0.9 m at about 4 m/s). Not verified live: whether releasing her (no hold command) lets the platform carry her; that needs a probe the user has not approved. Change (`mod/companions.lua`, `mod/init.lua` passes `delta` to `carry`): the carry places her at V's offset plus the car's vertical speed times a learned lead (starts 0.12 s, corrected from each passage sample by error / speed, clamped 0 to 0.6 s), only while a ride is active; she is hidden (inside the shut car, as before) when she is more than 0.3 m off after the first 1.2 s on two samples in a row, more than 1.2 m off at any point, or the carry fails. Probe lines: `lift-carry` `held` (`lag`, `lead`) once she holds, `hidden` with `reason` `drift` or `carry-failed` (`drift`, `lead`), and at arrival `rode` with `visible` true/false, the max `drift` after settling, `lead`, `sent`. Tests in `bridge/companion-passage.test.mjs` (visible exact carry and ride summary; an engine that places her two frames late stays within 0.3 m through the learned lead, fails with the lead off; no-op carry and failed facility still hide her). **2026-10-11 (lift ride going down: she is hidden at departure and was never really carried; Lua only; CET reload).** Live: "when I go down in the lift he doesn't come down, the lift phases through him". probe.log 07:41:54-07:42:06Z: `lift-departed` `withV`, `lift-carry` `held`, then 2 s later `hidden` with `lag` -0.9 and `sent` 58, `lift-arrived` (41 m down), `relocated` `how` `car`, `shown`. So the per-frame carry (`M.carry`) never held a rider past about a second (every logged ride since 2026-10-08 ends `hidden`), and the hide only came once the lag (0.9 m after 1 s of descent) was already on screen; the same log shows a second bug: a rider hidden in the sample that starts the ride was shown again by the same sample (`checkRiders` ran before `s.riding` was set: 07:20:23Z `hidden`, `shown`, `departed`, `hidden` a second later). Fix (`mod/companions.lua`, `boardRiders`, `M.update` sample order): a rider in the car is hidden the moment the car departs (shut doors) and no per-frame carry runs for her (less per-frame work than before); if the hide event cannot be queued she is carried as before; she is placed beside V out of view at arrival and shown as before; `s.riding` is set before `checkRiders` so a departure hide stays. Probe line: `lift-carry` `hidden` `reason` `departure`. Tests in `bridge/companion-passage.test.mjs` (hidden at departure, never shown during the ride, no carry, placed in the car at arrival, shown, walks out; the carry fallback when the hide fails). **2026-10-10: a player's own key keeps long-term memory by reusing the account's memory user (built offline, NOT yet live: **bridge restart**).** Cause: after `076d25a`, the first conversation on the free key `...014e` was refused (`LTM speaker limit reached`), the anonymous retry was refused too (`Missing end_user_id. end_user_id is required when the character has Long Term Memory enabled.`, `runtime/bridge.stderr.log`: all the mod's characters have memory on), so the key could not hold a conversation. `POST /user/end-users/list` (read-only) shows the account holds two end users made outside the mod, `spongicus2094@gmail.com:playground-assistant` (last active 2026-09-30) and `convai:spongicus2094@gmail.com` (2026-06-24); the mod's id (`player-memory.json`) is not among them. The mod sends one id on every path (`PlayerMemory` UUID through `PlayerKnowledge` into the realtime, phone and microphone configs); `KnowledgeService`/quest sync/knowledge cloud are not wired into the bridge, and the REST memory API is switched off on a player's key. Now: on a speaker refusal on a player's key `memoryRecovery` (async `recover` hook in `RealtimeHost`) lists the account's users, adopts the most recently active untried one (`ConvaiKeys.adoptUser`), saves it in `player-convai-user.json` tagged with a hash of the key, and `memoryPolicy` then sends that id for everything on that key (`ConvaiKeys.identify`); the shared key keeps the mod's id; the local memory state and guards are unchanged. Old sessions and phone threads start afresh once. A later refusal of the adopted id drops it and tries the next; only with none left does the anonymous fallback and its one notice run. Tests: convai-key (23), convai-quota and realtime-single-session (15); full `bridge/` run 3302 tests, 0 failed. Clear-account script (not run): `node scripts/clear-own-key-end-users.mjs` lists, add `--apply` to delete (the delete body is untested; it stops after the first delete unless a new listing confirms it). Deleting also erases the playground memories of those users. See docs/OWN_CONVAI_KEY.md, "Plan without room for memory".

**2026-10-11 (launch defaults): Expressive emotions, Follow-up questions and Body gestures now default On (Lua + bridge; CET reload and bridge restart).** `loadSettings` (`mod/manager.lua`) defaults `expressiveEmotions`, `followUpInterest` and `bodyGestures` to On when the key is missing; a saved choice is kept. A missing `expressiveEmotions` beside an explicitly saved Convai cloud voice stays Off so the default never moves the voice; a new install ends on Pocket with all three On and no switch or notice on first load; Multilingual on still drops the tag settings with the cloud voice and keeps Expressive on with OmniVoice. `bridge/expressive-emotions.mjs` and `bridge/follow-up.mjs` treat a missing key as On. `mod/mcm.lua` marks the three default On; the two descriptions end "On by default." in `mod/locales/*.lua` (15 languages, last sentence and key changed, translated by hand: a native-speaker pass can follow) and `localization/en.json`. Because Expressive is now On, choosing the Convai cloud voice in Settings ends on Pocket with the notice unless Expressive is switched off first. Tests: `bridge/manager.test.mjs`, `expressive-emotions`, `follow-up`, `emotion-tags` updated. Docs: SETTINGS_REVIEW note, EMOTION_TAGS, FOLLOW_UP_QUESTIONS, COMPANION_GESTURES, MANAGER_UI, README. The user's `runtime/settings.json` was not changed.

**2026-10-11 (cloud voice honoured): choosing the Convai cloud voice now keeps it and turns Natural speech and Expressive emotions off, instead of moving the voice to Pocket (Lua only; CET reload; no bridge change).** The user's decision after the launch defaults made Expressive On for everyone. `manager.applySettings` (new `honourCloudVoice`): when an Apply changes the voice to the cloud voice, both tag settings go off; one notice ("The Convai cloud voice cannot use Natural speech or Expressive emotions, so they were turned off.", 15 locales + `localization/en.json`) only if the player had set one (Natural speech on, or Expressive that an Apply ever carried: new `expressiveEmotionsChosen` flag in `settings.json`); turning only the default off is silent, and `applySettings` returns a 4th value so `mod/mcm.lua` reloads the page (`schemaChanged`) without a notice. Turning a tag setting ON beside an already-saved cloud voice still moves the voice (Pocket, or OmniVoice · Balanced with Multilingual) with the old notice; one Apply that changes the voice to the cloud voice and turns a tag on lets the voice win. `loadSettings`: a saved cloud voice with a tag on keeps the voice and loads with the tags off (Multilingual or not). The settings help texts of the two settings still say "applying it while Convai cloud voice is chosen switches Companion voices to Pocket TTS", which is now true only for turning the setting on; reword with a native-speaker pass. Check live: Settings > Voices, pick Convai · Cloud voice with Expressive at its default (no notice, toggle shows Off), turn Expressive on (voice moves to Pocket, notice), pick the cloud voice again (notice).

The installed `manager-characters.json` (474 bytes, Oct 5) had only Johnny and Smasher, and the installed `conversation-characters.json` (the roster, Oct 5) had 79 rows without `alt-cunningham`, so `manager.configure` never registered her (the Lua was at HEAD; the 2026-10-10 "synced from HEAD" covered Lua only, not the three JSON data files). Needs CET Reload All Mods (the mod reads them at load); the bridge already reads the repo copies.
