**2026-10-11 (voice key: a recording that held no speech waited 60 s and then blamed the microphone; the key is now spam-safe; Lua + bridge; CET reload and bridge restart; no voice build).** Live report: voice input did not work, maybe because the key was spammed. Evidence: the bridge log had `Microphone: ... Convai did not finish the reply` at 12:54:24 (the 60 s reply ceiling in `realtime-client.mjs` `collect`), the Windows microphone consent store showed the bridge's browser held the microphone 12:53:19 to 12:53:24 (device, permission, volume 100 percent and unmuted all fine, default input Realtek array), and `microphone-command.json` held the game's `cancel` written at 12:54:25 by the `error` state. So one 5 s recording was captured, Convai returned no transcript and no reply, the player waited 60 s and saw "Microphone unavailable. Check your Windows input device." Spamming made it likelier: a press while the device was still opening sent `finish` at once and a double tap right after Listening submitted a near-empty recording. Fix: `microphone.lua` `press()` ignores a press while connecting (re-shows the connecting notice), within 0.7 s of Listening (bounce) and while a reply is on its way, finishes otherwise, and an overdue reply (8 s) is dropped by the next press, which begins a fresh recording (`toggleVoice` in `init.lua`, nothing per frame). Bridge: `MicrophoneSession` holds a finish that arrives under 700 ms after the device opened; `collect` fails a voice turn with no transcript, no reply text and no thinking 15 s after the finish as "No speech was heard" (new `no-speech` state, notice `mic-no-speech`, 15 locales translated by a Haiku batch), and the 60 s timeout message now says what had arrived (transcript, text, complete, thinking, audio). Tests: microphone-session 26, microphone-adapter 8, realtime-client 82, plus the knowledge-integration and world-lifecycle hotkey tests now press finish after Listening. Live check: press the voice key, wait for Listening, speak, press again; spam the key during Connecting (ignored) and say nothing (about 15 s, then the no-speech notice).

**2026-10-11 (voice input shows what Convai hears, live; Lua + bridge; CET reload and bridge restart; no voice build).** The user asked to see his own words while he speaks. The SDK gives both: `userTranscriptionChange` carries the segment still being recognised (replaced as it grows) and the finalized segments accumulate in the `user-transcription` chat rows, so the page (`realtime-client.mjs` `heardText`, exported as `convaiRealtime.heard`) joins the final words and the live segment (newest 100 characters, single line). The bridge reads it through `RealtimeHost.voiceHeard` and `MicrophoneSession.heard` on the existing 150 ms microphone poll and writes `runtime/microphone-heard.json` only when the words change; `microphone.lua` reads that file on its existing 0.1 s update while recording or waiting for the reply and shows `You: {text}` as the game's notice line (new notice `mic-heard`, translated in the 15 locales by a Haiku batch), at most every 0.3 s and only for the current request, until the reply starts. The final transcript therefore shows before the reply. Nothing runs per frame. Tests: realtime-client 84, microphone-session 27, microphone-adapter 10, localized-feedback and ui-catalog green. Live check: press the voice key, wait for Listening, speak slowly: the top notice line turns into `You: ...` and grows with the words; press again and the reply follows.

**2026-10-11 (live captions did not show: the voice client bundle was stale; now V's words show as V's line in the cinematic subtitles; Lua + bridge; CET reload, bridge restart; `npm run build:voice` run).** Live test of a52eeb1: the reply came but no `You: ...`, and `runtime/microphone-heard.json` never existed. Cause: `runtime/realtime-client.js` is built by the supervisor once per supervisor start (game launch), not when it restarts the bridge, so the bundle dated 12:39 held neither the live-transcript page code nor the no-speech and timeout-diagnostic code of e404821 (the page `heard` was undefined and the host returned an empty string). Rebuilt with `npm run build:voice`; whenever `bridge/realtime-client.mjs` changes, rebuild before restarting the bridge. The host now logs once `the voice client bundle has no live transcript yet; run npm run build:voice` if the page lacks `heard`, and the bridge logs `reading heard words for <id>` and `first heard words N ms after the device opened` once per recording. Display per the user: `subtitles.showLive('V', text)` shows V's words as one dialogue-HUD line replaced by the same page swap a long reply uses (new line first, old one hidden after it rendered, so no empty frame), only when new words arrive (0.3 s throttle in `microphone.lua`), owning no utterance, replaced by the reply's caption, cleared when the recording is cancelled, expiring on its own otherwise; V has no actor, so it is the bottom dialogue HUD (the overhead path updates in place only for a living speaker). With the game's subtitles off, the words go to the notice line as before. Tests: subtitles 47, microphone-adapter 12, realtime-client 84 and the catalog, notices, hotkey and lifecycle suites. Live check: wait for Listening and speak: `V: ...` grows at the bottom; press again and the reply caption follows.

**2026-10-11 (live words fell back to the notice line: V's subtitle line now has the player as its owner and never assigns a nil speaker; Lua only; CET reload; no bridge change).** Live after b386d8a: the words arrived (`first heard words 3155 ms after the device opened`) but showed in the notice line, so `subtitles.showLive` returned false or raised. The likely cause is the dialogue line's `speaker` field: a reply always has an actor handle, V's line had none and `showPage` assigned nil to the native struct (the fake line accepted that, CET may not). `showPage` now assigns `speaker` only when there is an owner, and V's line is owned by the player (no per-frame actor checks: `M.actor` stays nil), always in the dialogue HUD whatever style replies use. `microphone.onHeard` (init.lua) wraps the call in pcall and logs once per reason in probe.log: `Live words: using the notice line (game subtitles are off | subtitle error: ... | subtitle line refused)`, so if it still falls back the log names the reason. The notice line stays only for genuinely off subtitles or a failing call. Tests: subtitles 47 (a strict fake line rejects a nil speaker), microphone-adapter 12, hotkey and lifecycle suites green.

**2026-10-10 (night): the shut mouth: our speech was only added to the facial idle (fixed in the graph archive and the writer, built offline; archive NOT installed: game restart with the archive and `FaceFeature.reds`; Lua hot).** Mechanism: every face graph ends in one Sermo solver whose input is the whole pose, and that pose always contains a facial idle (the Idle state machine plays a looped `idle__<emotion>__<sex>` clip, 39 such clips in `woman_average_sermo`; a workspot, a scene or a look-at streams its own on top). The solver (reconstruction in `vendor/reference-cp77-blender/.../facial/solver.py`) multiplies each control by `lerp(1, <control>AnimOverrideWeight, lipSyncEnvelope)`, skipped when the envelope is 0, then adds `<control>LipsyncPoseOutput`. We wrote only the pose tracks (envelope 0, overrides 1), so a jaw of .1 to .65 was added to an idle holding `lips_together` at .7 to 1.0, `jaw_mid_close`, `lips_suck_dn` and purse at that moment; the clip loops, so the seal comes and goes (it showed under gestures because the talk workspots named `interested`, which holds the lips doubly; the neutral idle only lowered it). The gesture agent's idea (override = 1 minus each control's own pose) would not have cured it: a control at 0 for the instant (lips together on a vowel) keeps the idle's closure. Fix: one new float input `speech_owner` (`assets/facial/controls.json` `owner`, 49 nodes per graph from `scripts/Build-FacialGraphs.mjs`) adds to `lipSyncEnvelope` and subtracts from the override weight of the 48 jaw, lip and cheek controls, so 0 is the old graph and 1 removes the idle from the mouth; eyes, brows and lids have no override track and stay untouched, so blinks and emotions are unchanged. `mod/facial-playback.lua` writes it eased in over .1 s while a reply is heard, with the release ease, and 0 when blocked or handed back to the idle writer; an install without the field keeps the old behavior. `face.expressions.lastSpeech.ownedFrames` reports it. Build: the first build failed in WolvenKit (a handle referenced before its definition in document order; the builder now defines the shared input in the outermost node, with a test); second build passed the 9-graph binary round trip (59 deltas, 49 ownership nodes, 60 inputs each) and the 9-entry archive check. Tests: `bridge/facial-graphs.test.mjs` (12), `bridge/companion-expression-playback.test.mjs` (a heard reply owns the mouth, hands it back, older feature without the field). Not done: no live sample (the user was in game with a second probing agent; a probe round was asked to stop), so the engine's use of the override stage is unproven and `Subtract` is read as track minus input; the packaging copy of the archive is not updated; I did not correlate failing moments with workspot enter and exit times (the logs only hold `lastSpeech` totals).

**2026-10-10 (night): the gesture twitch is the stance entry; flavour/talk re-entry thrash fixed (Lua only; CET reload).** Live frame captures of Evelyn: every mod workspot entry lurches the whole body (head 15-19 px down, hips 16-30 px, side swing, calm after 0.5 s); it is the same for a 0.5 s and a 1.5 s enter blend, the exits are smooth. Cause is the pose gap between her ambient idle and the stance (27 cm per foot for Evelyn's purse stance, 5-9 cm for the generic woman), plus the controller re-entering: the log showed four talk-stance begins in ten seconds and flavour idles left within 2-5 s. `mod/companion-gestures.lua`: 1 s settled before the talk stance, 4 s no re-entry after a forced exit, hand-on-hip idle waits 45 s after a reply, stance entered while V waits (thinking). Open: slower idle-copy and talk enter blends and a bridge workspot per ambient stance, both need an archive and a live A/B; see `docs/COMPANION_GESTURES.md` Round 5b. The gesture probe fights the shipped controller and the actuator on the same companion (auto-unstick releases the actuator command): Evelyn walked back and forth during the 03:20 IST tests; probe only with her controllers paused.

**2026-10-10: companion progress notices removed in every mode; a player and a developer see only "joined your group" and failures (`mod/manager-party.lua`, `mod/notices.lua`, `mod/init.lua`, locales; tests updated; NOT yet live: CET reload only, no bridge restart).**

**2026-10-10: a free key's first conversation said "plan limit reached"; it was Convai's memory-user limit (fixed offline, NOT yet live: **bridge restart**, **CET reload** for Lua and locales).** Cause, from `runtime/bridge.stderr.log`: `Bridge request failed: ... LTM speaker limit reached` at `ConvaiClient.connect`. The free plan allows ONE long-term-memory speaker (`ltm_speaker_limit` 1 in the plan table) and counts every speaker the account was active with in the last 30 days; the mod sends one end-user id per install on every conversation, so an account that had already used memory had no room for it. My classifier filed every "speaker limit" as a plan limit. Now: `speaker` is its own kind and never "limit used up"; on a player's key the refused turn is repeated once without the end-user id and the key's conversations go on without long-term memory (no memory, and no offered companion actions, which need an identified session); one `key-memory` notice with the first such reply, settings line "Your own key, without memory"; if the anonymous request is refused too (`Missing end_user_id`, depends on each character's memory mode, unverified) the notice says to free a memory user or use another key. Voice turns cannot be repeated: the first one shows the notice, the next runs without memory. Tests: convai-quota, convai-key, realtime-single-session, manager. Unverified until a real free key is used again: that the anonymous request is accepted (the log line `The key's plan has no room for another memory user` and a reply confirm it).

**2026-10-10: players can use their own free Convai API key (built offline, NOT yet live: `npm run build:voice`, **bridge restart**, **CET reload** for the Lua and locale files).** Feasibility: every one of the 308 mod characters is `listing=public`, and Convai's `/connect` serves public or unlisted characters to any valid key, so a key from another account talks to them with no per-player setup and spends that account's allowance (`docs/OWN_CONVAI_KEY.md`). Quota exhaustion arrives as one server message `usage-limit-reached` (`quota_type` interaction or tts) and then the session closes; a made-up key answers 401 `Invalid API key`. Built: Settings > Development has `Use my own Convai API key`, a clipboard Paste button, Remove, and a status line (only the last four characters are shown; the key lives in the bridge's `player-convai-key.txt`, never in settings, logs or reports); the bridge switches key at an idle boundary and closes both hosts and sessions; a free key is one connection at a time, so no warm, look-ahead or prefetch connections, an exclusive pool and one retry on a concurrency refusal; Multilingual twins are off and memory management is unavailable on a player key; one `quota-exhausted` notice per session says a free key is available at convai.com. Tests: `convai-key`, `convai-quota`, `realtime-single-session`, plus additions to realtime-client, party-connections, phone-chat, player-memory-api and manager tests. Not verified (needs a real free key): the free plan's long-term-memory flag and its one-speaker limit. Not testable: a real used-up plan.

**2026-10-10: Evelyn answered "why blue hair?" as "I suspect she enjoys the way it makes people look twice" (fixed offline, not yet live).** Cause, reproduced against real Convai from the turn's real input (`runtime/last-input.json`): the journal-history keyword match pulled in the quest "Every Breath You Take" ("wait for Blue Moon") because of "blue" (and a second quest because of "why"); Evelyn's profile has no hair, so the model read the question as about Judy; and the Follow-up block ("answer first, end on a hook, never invent facts about your life") kept it from saying whom it meant, so it talked about another woman without naming her. Before: with Follow-up and Expressive on, 10 of 10 replies were about someone else in the third person (all three blocks 10 of 20), near-verbatim to the live line. Fix: question, function and colour/body words no longer match a quest (`bridge/journal-history.mjs`); a first-person rule on every turn, including "what V says about how you look is about you" (`bridge/quest-context.mjs`), plus a one-sentence reminder after the voice blocks (`bridge/convai-bridge.mjs`); the Follow-up "facts about your life" sentence now says you say it as "I" and that your looks are not such a fact; "How Evelyn Parker talks:" is now "How you talk:" and the other blocks no longer say "this character". After: 0 of 70 third-person replies on the same question with the journal rows removed; 0 of 20 with the rows still present and the rules only (a residual 2 to 3 of 10 for Natural speech alone). Six characters, 48 to 60 turns per condition: third-person self-reference near zero before and after (the profile texts of 71 characters are third person but did not leak on ordinary questions); Expressive tags 100 % to 100 %, voiced sounds 94 to 98 % to 95 to 100 %, replies ending in a question 57 % to 73 %. Not done: the companion's real appearance is not in the context (the model accepts or denies a look it cannot see), and the third-person profile texts were not rewritten. Probe `scripts/Probe-ThirdPerson.mjs` now uses one fixed test end-user id with memories wiped after each job: a first version made one id per sample, about 2,200 LTM end users named `third-person-probe-*` that the user must clean up. Docs: `docs/FIRST_PERSON_RULE.md`. `npm test` failures that are not from this change: the group-voice tests of `bridge/knowledge-integration.test.mjs` (another agent's seated free-look Lua).

**2026-10-10: her seat no longer ends by itself (built into the gesture archive; NOT installed: game restart for `convai_gestures.archive`).** The 21:19Z bar sit confirmed the cause: she left after 59.7 s (`seat-left-on-own`), the minimum of the vanilla workspot's 5-8 fidgets; the reseat brought her back but visibly stood and sat. The user's "she got off the chair when I looked at her" was that. The vanilla NPC sit workspots (what `convai_sit_*` played) run one random list of 4-9 idle clips and then exit. `scripts/Build-GestureAssets.mjs` now copies the four seat workspots as `convai_seat_*` with every random list at 127 clips (signed byte; 255 failed to convert) and its sequence repeating, neutral facial idle kept; the verify step enforces it (`bridge/gesture-seat-endless.test.mjs`, 5 tests). Archive rebuilt on the gesture round HEAD (`runtime/gestures/package/convai_gestures.archive`, sha256 081105ae...; catalog unchanged; verify: 25 workspots, 50 components, neutral facial idle passes); the packaging copy is not updated. The Lua reseat stays as a fallback. Check after the restart: stay seated 5 minutes with V seated (expect no `seat-left-on-own`), she still fidgets, and stands with V. See `docs/COMPANION_SEATING.md`, "The seat that ended on its own".

**2026-10-10: seat sits sooner, and sits again when the sit workspot ends by itself (built, hot-reloaded with `reload-seating`; CET reload keeps it).** User live result after the logging: she sits on a chair now but takes long, and once got up while V still sat. Slow: the 21:10Z bar sit started 8.7 m out and every walk-up point was refused at the `path` stage with her never moving (`at` identical in all five lines): 5 tries at 2-3 s, 3 s closing stall, 3 s placement wait, 19 s in all. Now a `path` refusal that left her where she stood (under 0.5 m moved) logs `seat-walk-stuck`, skips the other points, closes in for 1.5 s (was 3) and then places her, still only while she is out of V's view (about 7 s). Early stand: not a false `player-stood`. Both cases (20:53:59Z, 21:12:35Z) were `seat-finished` reason `left the seat on its own` with V still seated, 105 s and 95 s after `seat-seated`; every other seat that lasted under 70 s was ended by V. The trace shows her standing and stepping out of the seat. The vanilla NPC sit workspot ends by itself after about 100 s (exits are enabled, the idle sequences do not loop forever). Now `seat-left-on-own` is logged, she stands out cleanly through the normal step-out and, if V is still in the same sit and she had sat 20 s or more, `seat-reseat` makes her take a seat again; a seat that ends within 20 s is not retried. She still visibly stands and sits once per ~100 s; the real cure would be our own sit workspots with an infinite idle (archive rebuild, game restart) or a tested `ResetPlaybackToStart` before the end. Tests +3 in `bridge/companion-seating.test.mjs`. Retest: sit on the counter stool with her in another room (expect her seated within about 8 s), then stay seated 2 minutes.

**2026-10-10 (night): gesture round 5: repeats, body twitch, mouth sealed under a workspot, an idle for every tier-1 (built offline; archive rebuilt, NOT installed; Lua committed only).** Repeats: the memory was already per companion and kept across replies; the cause was the policy (top-heavy reach weights, 2-3 clip emotion pools falling to the 'not the previous clip' level, no look-alike grouping). `mod/gesture-selection.lua` now avoids the last 12 clips, look-alike groups, the previous kind and the last 75 s; simulated 60 companions x 12 replies: same clip within 6 gestures 12-39% to 0%, distinct clips 29 to 36-39; pool per woman_base family 62 to 69 usable (30 to 37 calm), men 59 to 66, big men 61 to 69 (an 'open' class: hand 20-30 cm to the side, played at 0.6 weight). Mouth: a workspot swaps the face graph's idle for the workspot hub's facial idle; our talk workspots inherited `idle__interested__*` + `attentive`, which holds the lips together (mean 0.7 vs 0.32 for neutral) while our speech layer never fades the idle; live A/B showed it shut intermittently. All mod-played workspots now use the plain neutral facial idle (5 vanilla idle workspots copied as `convai_face_*`); even the neutral idle sometimes closes the lips, so the real fix is `AnimOverrideWeight` = 1 - pose in the face graph (lip-sync agent, `scripts/Build-FacialGraphs.mjs`). Idles: every tier-1 has its own (Alt her q116 loop; Lizzy, Mama Welles, Meredith, Regina, Padre, Mr. Hands, Viktor, Pepe new); see `docs/COMPANION_GESTURES.md` Round 5. **2026-10-10: why she did not sit beside V on a chair in the apartment (investigated; cause is the walk refusal, not the classifier; logging added; CET reload or `reload-seating`).** Read every `Seating` line of 2026-10-09 20:00-20:53Z (6 sessions): the apartment couch always classified `couch` (h0 0.44, backrest 0.63, one seat 0.66 m left), the kitchen counter stool `bar` (h0 0.83, counter 1.10 m in front, one seat 1.15 m right); no session ever classified `chair`, `table` or `stool`, and between 20:09 and 20:50 there is no Seating line at all (no sit was detected). **2026-10-10: Pocket replies, snapping and held mouth fixed; the no-mouth reply is not reproduced offline.** On Pocket (Omni had timed out) the user saw the mouth miss at the end of a line, stay half open while the next line loaded, snap, and once not open at all. Replayed offline through the real writer and the real bridge pipeline: the speech-mouth factors (closure/rounding budget, chin and corner ducks) were instantaneous, so a smile or frown pumped at syllable rate (smile corner .35 to 0 in three frames, 14 big steps per reply; frown 27); they now ease with an attack and release (steps 14 to 2 and 27 to 8, largest .24 to .08). A heard cursor that stopped while the stream still reported playing held the last jaw for the whole stall, and a stream pause closed the mouth in one frame; after .20 s of stall and on every pause the mouth now eases shut over .12 s and the idle writer takes over. The last-line misses are the model's own (word onsets and quiet tails, 3.5 to 7 % of voiced frames); the reply with no mouth at all (19:29:24 UTC) is complete in the bridge and the Lua writes an open mouth for it, with no warnings in any log; the only difference is a gesture workspot started in the same second, so `speech-state.json` `lastSpeech` now carries `jawPeak`, `openFrames` and `jawLift` to tell writer from rig next time. Tests added (stall, step size, turn record), 184 pass. Needs a CET reload (or hot reload of `mod/facial-playback.lua`), no bridge restart. Details: `docs/GAME_LIPSYNC_MODEL.md` "Sixth round".

**2026-10-10: a slow OmniVoice start no longer downgrades the session to Pocket.** Cause (from the morning's `bridge.stderr.log`: weights loaded, then "Local voice startup timed out", then `fallback="Omni worker unavailable"` on every reply): `VOICE_WORKER_LIMITS.startupMs` in `bridge/resident-voice.mjs` was one fixed 180 s limit, a start takes about 3 minutes on a CPU-throttled laptop in Windows Silent mode even without the game loading beside it, and a failed worker then stayed out of routing for the whole session (`failedBackends` is cleared only when the player chooses the voice again). Fixes: (1) the startup limit is progress-based: any frame or log line from the worker restarts a 300 s stall window, 900 s is the absolute cap, a worker asked to stop while loading gets 60 s (`resident-voice-worker.py` now sends a `loading` frame after the imports, after the model load and after the CUDA graph, same frame type, no protocol change); (2) `VoiceSelection` keeps the fallback (Pocket) but retries OmniVoice in the background after 45 s, 3 min and 10 min (`omniRetry` and `fallback.temporary` in `local-voice-state.json`): due-checked from the existing 500 ms settings poll (no timer, no loop), only with no reply in flight and voice playback on, in a second worker at below-normal priority (`os.setPriority`, raised to normal once ready) beside Pocket (CPU only, no VRAM); when it is ready the next idle poll commits Omni, adopts that worker (no second load) and releases Pocket, never mid-reply; a fourth failure gives up as before; picking another voice, another reference set, a missing pack or shutdown drops the retry or the loading worker; (3) the player sees one notice when Pocket starts serving, "{requested} is not ready yet. Companions are using {voice} for now." (same `voice-fallback` id and 600 s window; new text in all 15 locales and `localization/en.json`), nothing on the way back, and the permanent "is unavailable" text only if the retries run out; the Settings status line says "not ready yet" for the temporary case; the routing log says "Omni still loading (retrying in the background)". Tests: `bridge/resident-voice.test.mjs` (stall window restarts on output, absolute cap, short close while loading, priority), `bridge/voice-selection.test.mjs` (retry timing and back-off, never during a reply, adoption without a second load, drop on voice change or close, a test with the real `ResidentVoice` and fake child processes), `bridge/manager.test.mjs` (notice wording and silent return), `bridge/voice-routing-log.test.mjs`. Docs: `docs/LOCAL_VOICES.md` ("Startup limit, background retry and switch back"), `docs/NOTIFICATIONS.md`. Not touched: Pocket's own 180 s startup limit; the first OmniVoice start at game launch still runs at normal priority. NOT yet live: **bridge restart** (Python worker, `bridge/` and the engine) plus **CET reload** for the two Lua files (`mod/manager.lua`, `mod/mcm.lua`) and the locale files. In-game check: on Silent power mode, launch the game and talk right away; expect either OmniVoice after a wait, or Pocket with the one "not ready yet" notice and OmniVoice back by itself a few minutes later (watch `[OmniRetry]` lines in `runtime/bridge.stderr.log`).

**2026-10-09 (night): Evelyn's mouth still missed on the last line; scripted talk on 7 characters, chin raise fixed.** The saved 11:58 reply (3 Omni phrases) was replayed through the real `speech.start` path: every phrase has a complete `local-jali` face, the stream and the last phrase run to the end, and the written jaw equals the lift and floor to the frame, so the Lua path is not what loses the opening there. **2026-10-09: Omni text-conditioned mouth was never used on long phrases.** Every Omni phrase over about 1.5 s logged `lipText lost: behind`: the phrase WAV is pushed to the facial worker in 1 s slices at once, so slice N always waited behind slices 1..N-1 and the real-time lag guard (`FaceModelStream.guard`, 250 ms) dropped the text path. `bridge/jali-face-model.mjs` now skips the guard in phrase mode (rows are released at finish and speech starts after the analysis, so nothing real-time is protected); streaming (Pocket, Convai) keeps it. Test in `bridge/jali-face-text.test.mjs` (fails without the change). Measured offline on the three saved phrases: text path now active, jaw differs by only .04 to .07 (the gain is in contact, purse and F/V per `docs/LIPSYNC_IMPROVEMENTS_2026-10-07.md`), analysis time +160 to +340 ms per 4.5 to 5.5 s phrase (about +.2 s before the first phrase is heard; later phrases analyse while the previous one plays). Bridge only, needs a bridge restart; no Lua, no per-frame work. Revert by restoring the one-line guard, or set `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_FACE_TEXT=off`.

**2026-10-09: lip sync floor for low-jaw sounds.** Evelyn "sometimes does not open" ("and fill it with exactly..."): offline from the saved Omni reply and its WAVs, the model's track is open there (jaw .05 to .29) and the phrase hand-off does not seal it; the culprit is f, th, l, w and short i, which the model gives a jaw of only .09 to .11, written at .17 to .20 by the first lift, right at the point where her lips part (measured: sealed .08 to .10, thin line .12 to .14, teeth from .16, clear from .20). `mod/facial-playback.lua` now also floors a speaking jaw (model jaw above about .04, smoothstep to .07): `woman_average` .21, men and big bodies .17, Judy and unknown graphs unchanged, silence untouched. Needs a CET reload. Found, not fixed: the Omni text-conditioned mouth is dropped as 'behind' on every phrase longer than about 1.5 s (the WAV is pushed to the worker in 1 s slices at once, so the real-time lag guard fires); it changes this phrase's jaw by under .03. Details in `docs/GAME_LIPSYNC_MODEL.md`.

**2026-10-09: eyebrow flicker fixed (a neutral frame at every phrase hand-off).** User: "sometimes the eyebrows flicker" on Evelyn. Root cause, found by driving the real `speech.lua` + `facial-playback.lua` on a v1 Omni stream: each phrase end (and the reply end, a stream underrun, a late native start) called `face.stop`/`face.pause` -> `ConvaiFaceReset()`, which writes an all-zero feature and kept the idle writer off that frame, so brows, lids, squints and corners showed one neutral frame and snapped back (anger reply: 3 resets, a .55 brow dip at 32.8/s; real trust-to-joy timelines: 2). `mod/facial-playback.lua` `reset()` now hands a managed copy to its idle writer in the same call (speech fields 0, expression/droop/blink continuous; world speakers, a paused game and no frame context keep the plain reset), and the idle writer no longer sits out `paused`. The suspected intensity steps (anger .54, .30, .47) were a slow wave (.16 sag, .12 climb over 6 s, max 3/s), not a twitch; `settleCarriedFeelings` in `bridge/emotion-selector.mjs` still damps a carried feeling's fall to half (.54, .30, .47 plans .54, .42, .47; rises, new feelings and family changes untouched). After: 0 resets, worst step 2.9/s, brow reversal .076 within 8 s. Tests in `bridge/companion-expression-playback.test.mjs` and `bridge/emotion-selector.test.mjs`; details in `docs/COMPANION_EXPRESSIONS.md` ("October 9 night"). Install: copy `mod/facial-playback.lua` and reload CET; the bridge restart is only for the settle pass. Not verified in game (no probe plays a multi-phrase stream).

**2026-10-09: lip sync on wide-lipped heads (jaw lift per head type).** Evelyn and Johnny "don't open the mouth on some words": the speech weights are tuned on Judy's rig, where jaw .06 parts the lips, and `M.sync` wrote them unchanged to every head. A 23-character in-game sweep showed the 12 `woman_average` heads keep their lips sealed until jaw about .20 (typical speech median .12), men and big bodies part later and less. `mod/facial-playback.lua` now lifts the jaw with `jaw' = .7*(1-(1-jaw/.7)^q)` (identity at 0 and .7), q=2 for `woman_average`, q=1.5 for `man_average`/`man_big`/`man_fat`, read once per reply from `ConvaiFaceStatus`, per-character override `traitTable[profile].jawLift`; Judy and unknown graphs unchanged. Proof: `runtime/face-captures/lipsync-wide-lips-sheet.png` and `lipsync-check/<key>.png` (all 23). Needs a CET reload (no bridge or game restart); the in-game real-path check after the reload is still open. Also hardened the `face-stage` probe (anchor once, 1.3 m for big bodies, abort when V moves, ignore stale requests, quiet invites). Details: `docs/GAME_LIPSYNC_MODEL.md` "October 9: wide-lipped heads".

Measured on real Convai characters (8 characters x 40 lines per block, fresh end-user, Natural speech and Follow-up on): the final block (`EMOTION_TAG_BLOCK` in `bridge/emotion-tags.mjs`, 1,400 chars, now also `EXPRESSIVE_TEXT`) is valid on 320 of 320 replies, opens every reply with a tag, 2.06 tags a reply, calm 32 %, interest 9 %. Against 2,600 blind per-segment labels (`assets/emotion/segments-gold-tags.json`): the tag in force is acceptable on 80 % of segments (60 % before) and the face as played 56.5 % exact / 80.3 % acceptable (48.3 / 72.1 before; picker alone 44-46 / 68-72). Voice quality unchanged to slightly better; after the block is dropped the model writes no tags (0 of 54 turns), so a voice switch does not leak braces. Tried and not kept: wiring `selectEmotion({spans})` (specific-tag spans equal the marks path, trusting {calm} spans is worse), a variant without {interest} (joy takes the surplus, fit drops). New `bridge/emotion-tags.mjs` (grammar, aliases, streaming parser, spans; `natural-speech.mjs` uses it), `scripts/Probe-EmotionTags.mjs`, `scripts/Judge-EmotionTags.mjs`, labelling and `--gold` support in the evaluator. Install: bridge restart only. `docs/EMOTION_TAGS.md`.

**2026-10-09: emotion picker no longer dead on tagged replies.** Root cause of the `calm 0.00` timelines: the character LLM puts `{calm}` on 56 % of its tags and `{interest}` on 24 %, and the selector treated every tag as authoritative (`{calm}` wiped text, Convai and context; `{interest}` became a flat anticipation .60). `bridge/emotion-selector.mjs`: only specific tags name a feeling, Convai's label is an informativeness-weighted reply prior, the player line is a quiet frame, everyday dialogue cues (guarded denial, stern warning, teasing, affection) added, intensity fixed so picks are visible; new `spans` option (`[{start,end,emotion,intensity}]`, tags win, picker fills gaps) is the hook for the tag parser but is not wired into `convai-bridge.mjs` yet. 2867 hand-labelled segments (`assets/emotion/segments-gold.json`, `scripts/Evaluate-EmotionSegments.mjs`): acceptable 60.2 -> 70.8 % with tags + Convai (fresh prompts 66.9 -> 75.6), lines that should emote but showed nothing 47.7 -> 25.7 %, wrong-sign 2.2 -> 7.4 %. Details in `docs/COMPANION_EXPRESSIONS.md`. **Bridge restart needed** to see it.

**2026-10-09: Companions know what they last did with V, and V's open jobs by category**
- User (Judy test): she did not recall their latest shared quest event, and should know the jobs V can take now, with main and side jobs told apart.
- Cause: world facts were an unordered set. Judy had 15 true facts, including the apartment date, with no order. "what did we do last?" also pulled in **Gig: Last Login**, because the journal-history matcher counted the word *last*.
- Signal: the save stores each journal entry's last state-change time (uint32 game seconds plus a uint16 sequence within that second), the same value as `GetEntryTimestamp`. Its TimeSystem node holds the game clock. Checked on six autosaves: Pyramid Song's objectives order as played.
- Lua: `world-knowledge.lua` reads the timestamp only when an entry's state changes (memo per world) and reads the clock once per capture. `journal-history.lua` reads each quest root's `GetQuestType` (cached per hash) and timestamp. Both stay inside the existing throttled cycles; GameTime must read the same both ways or is dropped.
- Bridge: `quest-overview.mjs` builds two sections. The first lists up to 3 shared events, newest first, from reviewed participant, witnessed or private rules; each is timed by its latest journal entry (or by quest completion as the latest possible time) and marked recently or a while ago. The second lists open jobs as Main, Side, Gigs and NCPD from the game's own types (`journal-quest-types.mjs` is the cooked fallback), with the tracked job flagged and only the speaker's own jobs given objectives still to do. Temporal stop words were added to the journal-history matcher.
- Offline (`save_overview.mjs`, AutoSave-8): Judy's events are 1) the apartment date, 27 days ago; 2) Pyramid Song, continuing the relationship; 3) Pyramid Song, the dive, 34 days ago. Her jobs list 2 main and 8 side jobs, the apartment quest with "Meet Judy at your Glen apartment." still to do, and 1 NCPD hustle.
- Text probe: before, "I don't know what you mean" and a deflection; after, "the last time we really spent time together, it was when I came over to your place ... a while ago now", and she named the tracked job and the main jobs. Residual: she adds "in the Glen" from the still-to-do objective.
- Tests: knowledge 543/543, research 41/41 (1 skipped), and 9 new overview tests plus 5 new Lua/parser tests. The full `npm test` run had 3115 passing and 2 failing, neither from this change: a group look-ahead timing flake that passes alone, and `natural-speech-talk.mjs` missing from the bridge allowlist since `49424eb`.
- Follow-up after the install (user: talk about what a quest is, not its name). `research/cyberpunk/quest-premises.json` adds reviewed hook-only premises for 272 of 290 main, side and gig quests (six review lanes plus hand-written ones for the two open endgame jobs; endings and meta quests excluded). Both sections now carry `about` instead of titles, and the title is a fallback only. Probe, three runs: "the last time was when I came over to your place"; "that thing with Nele Springer in Dogtown ... heavy stuff with Reed and Hanako that sounds way more important". No titles, and no Glen slip.

**2026-10-09 (later): Her idle face holds until the reply is heard**
- User: the idle emotion went bland before the lip sync started. Causes (all `mod/facial-playback.lua`): a new input or the microphone retired her reaction and zeroed the pose; the resting smile was idle-only; `beginExpression` (reply handed to speech, before audio) cleared the reaction and applied the reply's cue; `pause` while a stream buffered or an Omni phrase loaded reset the face to zero.
- Fix: thinking and listening keep the idle face (mirrored in `companion-expressions.mjs`); a reply's reset and cue wait for its first heard frame (`startHeard`, `expressionHeard`); until then the idle writer keeps writing; `pause`/`stop` of a never-heard reply reset nothing. Same smoothing at the switch; no added per-frame work.
- Tests: phrase route (cursor held at 0 for 2 s), stream route (opened, timeline arrived, paused while buffering), cancel before audio, a lingering reaction through thinking/listening; both new tests fail on the previous code. Face/speech/gesture suites 412/412.
- Install: `mod/facial-playback.lua` (CET reload). The bridge file `companion-expressions.mjs` changed only as the tested mirror (no runtime effect).

- Repeats: Judy's own arm-crossed `gesture__01/03` (9.3 s) weighed 3x and returned after every 30 s cooldown; nothing stopped two variants of one move back to back; listening nods could repeat. Now: never the previous clip, not in the last 4, not the previous *kind* (`yes`, `what`, `talk`...), 45 s cooldown, recent kinds weigh less, character clips 1.5x.
- Pointing: `neutral` matched `describe_left/right/back`, `point_back`, side head flicks and one-hand-out talk clips, weighted 2.5x as expressive. New `scripts/Measure-GestureShape.py` (FK on the glTF exports, finger bones included) measures side/cross/behind, index pointing, one straight arm, torso/head turn, overhead, reach and a hands-centred share into `assets/gestures/clip-shape.json`; `gestures.json` `conversationFit` rules write `kind`, `fit` and an `exclude` reason per clip into the catalog. Conversation pool after: Judy arms-crossed 123 -> 87 (36 excluded, incl. her gesture 01/03 for a 25-27 deg torso turn), hands-at-sides 124/118/117 -> 66/61/63 (woman/man/big). Centred clips weigh up to 1.65x.

- Evidence (game runtime): probe.log 20:13:46Z `Face request: actor=9004379ULL status=requested:citizen__arasaka_corpo_wa__q004__konpeki_receptionist_04`, reply at 20:13:57Z with `error=false`. `bridge-latency.json` for `1791490429-2-9004379ULL` shows OmniVoice 16, 2.36 s, `nativePlaybackObserved: true` at cursor 0.06 s: speech.lua reached `playing` and Audioware advanced. Same entity ID before and after (focus at 20:14:01Z), so the rebuild did not replace the entity, and Audioware binds the emitter by entity ID, not to a component. Either way the NPC was a quest-owned crowd character (The Heist's `q004` receptionists), the kind that should never be rebuilt.

The reply log line now records the speech state and the speaker's face readiness/status, so the next round shows what happened. **2026-10-09: Blinking through speech, and expressions that read during a reply**
- Blinks: our layer never touched the lids. The game's talking blinks come from its dialogue lipsync animations (`eye_l/r_blinkLipsyncPoseOutput`, about 19 a minute in 800 extracted lines), which our speech replaces; the face graph's only other blink is the look-at controller's on a gaze shift, and she holds eye contact while she talks. All routes (Pocket, Omni, Convai) were affected. `mod/facial-playback.lua` now adds a game-shaped blink every 2-6 s with jitter, sometimes at a phrase start, while speaking, listening or thinking; idle stays native; blocked faces never blink; no blink starts in an Omni phrase's last .35 s. A few comparisons per write, no tables, no new hooks. `face.blinks` in `speech-state.json` counts them.
- Expressions: measured 15 replies (Judy, Panam, Johnny) through the real selector, aligner and Lua writer on model mouth curves from real cloned audio. Collapse: a subtle tag (.45 x reserved .8 = .36) gave a third of a linear pose and held one level over the reply; warm smiled .25 x weight. Now frowns and brows follow w^.6, warm .35, tags .6/1, the text can deepen a tag, reserved/cold manners cap a phrase at .8/.6. Judy with tags: sadness corners .35 -> .47 and inner brows .29 -> .39, fear brows .16 -> .39, joy corners .39 -> .48, warm .09 -> .15; Johnny joy .14 -> .30, below Panam. Smiles stay linear: labelled-set labels unchanged, no new false smile on a negative reply (one soft smile on a calm one).
- Tests: seven new playback tests (blink rhythm on four sources, listening/thinking/idle/blocked, phrase boundary and Omni tail, per-feeling minimum deltas, phrase changes, Johnny vs Panam, grief never smiles); all fail on the previous code. Face, expression, emotion and lip sync suites 348/348 apart from a load-sensitive gesture timing test that passes alone; full `npm test` 3087 passed, 1 failed (`runtime-bootstrap` allowlist: `bridge/natural-speech-talk.mjs` from another session's uncommitted work), 9 skipped.
- Install: `mod/facial-playback.lua` (CET reload), bridge files `emotion-selector.mjs`, `expressive-emotions.mjs`, `companion-expressions.mjs` (bridge restart). No redscript or archive change.

**2026-10-08: Follow-up questions: characters sometimes show interest in V (new setting, off by default)**
- The user wanted conversations to feel two-sided, not as if only V is interested. New setting `followUpInterest` (label "Follow-up questions", Settings > Voices next to Natural speech and Expressive emotions; the old `followUpQuestions` key stays retired and is still dropped on load). Like Natural speech the bridge decides the rate (`bridge/follow-up.mjs`, `FollowUp`: warm .45, neutral .35, dry .20, cold .15, never two turns in a row, pity at least once in 5 turns for warm and neutral); the model decides whether and how a pull fits. It is a paragraph in the context of the reply Convai is already generating: no extra request, timer or turn (tested), and off the context is byte-identical.
- Never offered on reactions and barks, battle turns, group rounds, goodbyes, orders and requests (word lists in 15-odd languages; there is no pre-turn action detector, and a microphone turn carries only the placeholder before speech recognition), or right after a reply that ended in a question. Log: `[FollowUp] profile= setting= offered= reason= ended-with-question=`.
- Measured with real Convai (`scripts/Probe-FollowUp.mjs`, fresh test identity per run, memories cleared): 288 lines per setting over Judy, Panam, Johnny and a Tyger Claws ganger. Replies that end in a question 7% off, 23% on (Judy 13 to 31%, Panam 8 to 26%, Johnny 3 to 14%, ganger 3 to 22%); a pull in the last two sentences 11% off, 27% on; average length 21.8 to 24.6 words, none over 75. A Sonnet reader judged 86 pulls of 288: 79 specific, 83 in voice, 65 flowing out of the answer. Findings: the Oct 5 `OPEN_QUESTION_POLICY` line ("do not tack a question on") had to be lifted for the offered turn; the paragraph works much better before Natural speech's "keep the same length and content" than after it (16 of 26 against 13 of 41 offered turns pulled), so it sits before the voice notes and the reply-language line stays last; "X, or Y?" closers fell from 43% to 21% of pulls with the final wording. Known weak spots in the doc.
- Files: `bridge/follow-up.mjs`, `bridge/follow-up.test.mjs`, `bridge/convai-bridge.mjs`, `mod/manager.lua`, `mod/mcm.lua`, 15 locales, `localization/en.json`, `packaging/` lists, `scripts/Probe-FollowUp.mjs`, `docs/FOLLOW_UP_QUESTIONS.md` (rewritten; the retired toggle's history is at the end). Lua is one saved boolean; nothing runs per frame.

Offer rates tuned per profile (Judy .5, Panam .35, Johnny .42). Off and un-offered turns are unchanged. Japanese/Chinese check: only `[question-ei]` was chosen (7 of 22 offered turns). NOT installed: bridge restart. **2026-10-08: Judy did not walk away when told to go home; weapon stayed drawn after a fight**
- Leave, evidence: `companion-actions.json` held the `companion-leave` action (13:49:48), and probe.log shows `Departure queued`, `Departure walking away: copy 1` (13:49:55), then `Departure sent for copy 1 (out of view)` at 13:50:06. So the running bridge did emit the leave action and the Lua side accepted it; "bridge not restarted" is not the cause. The movement trace shows what happened between: the walk-away issued six moves in three seconds (tickets `depart-1-0` to `depart-1-6`), each refused by the actuator (`navigation` and `destination` rejections, one `detour-search`), then a `-rest` hold. Judy stood 1.3 m from V until V walked off and the out-of-view rule removed her. Cause: the goals were blind points 35 m away at fixed turns (50°, 100°, 160°), and the actuator only accepts walkable ground it can route to.
- Leave, fix: candidates are checked against the navmesh before they are used (walkable ground within 3 m on her floor, 12 m or more from V, a route no more than 4 m longer than the straight line, one floor, within the clearance box limit), at most 2 per update and only while no goal is held; distances 24, 17 and 29 m fanned out at seven bearings, home first; a goal the actuator refuses or that gets her 1.5 m nowhere in 4 s is replaced; no headway for 6 s means removal once V cannot see her for 1 s, at 15 s at the latest. Tests: six new cases in `bridge/world-companions.test.mjs` (four fail on the old code).
- Weapon, evidence: the fight ended 13:47:17; the behaviour trace shows her in `catch_up`/`alongside`/`approach` for two minutes, with the first calm hold at 13:49:13. `idle` for the holster was "V standing still and she is in a settled hold", so it waited for V to stop. Fix: after 3 s of calm with a weapon still drawn the equipment owner asks for a bounded stand-still (8 s, then a 20 s cooldown) and holsters through the same `AIUnequipCommand`; Alerted counts as relaxed after 6 s of calm. Tests: `bridge/companion-equipment.test.mjs` (the integration case fails on the old code with `waiting/Wait until settled`). Unverified in game: how long the engine keeps her Alerted/upper body busy after a fight; the new `holster` field in the movement trace will show it.

**2026-10-08: Sit with you (companions sit beside a seated V)**

Companions always sit when a seat is found; the Sit with you MCM row, its manager default, the Characters sidebar Behavior item and its 15 locale strings and `en.json` entries are gone; an old saved `sitWithYou` value is ignored. The only switch left is internal (an archive without the sit components disables seating).

World sit spots are `worldAISpotNode`s, not entities: no Lua query, so the native seats themselves are not used.

- Gesture probe: `run <name>` executes `runtime/<name>.lua` (research probes without a CET reload), `probe.watchers`, `seating`, `reload-seating`. A seat watcher (`runtime/seat-watch.lua`) is installed in the running game to log V's workspot path, scene state and a lateral surface profile the next time V sits.

- Seat watcher, V's apartment couch (5 s): `GetWorkspotInstance` nil, scene tier 4/3/4, PSM `IsInWorkspot` 0/1/0, V's root fixed at the seat front on the floor, cushion 0.44-0.45 m with its front edge 0-0.2 m back, ~1.1 m of seat to the left, floor to the right. The first build would have called it `unsupported`. Fixed: furniture classification for unnamed workspots, PSM settle/stand-up, couch samples 0.30 m back with 0.07 m tolerance, refused or never-taken seat walks slide within 1.8 m. Judy's `obstructed` phase came from the follow behaviour (motion sweep blocked by Static geometry 1.27 m up since before the sit), not from seating. 18 seating tests.

**2026-10-08: A resident on Pocket spoke with a still mouth**
- Evidence: `runtime/speech-state.json` face `error` = "facial-playback.lua:669: Speaker has no prepared owned facial layer" for request `1791445761-7-10041299ULL` (`resident-male`); probe.log "Focus ownership: actor=10041299ULL managed=false", "crowd=true". The bridge side was fine: `continuous-face.json` carried a `local-jali` timeline with the right request, entity and generation.
- Cause: the face layer was companion-only at both ends. Lua `owns()` required `convaiCompanions.owns(actor)`, and natively `ConvaiFaceService` swapped in the ConvaiFace graph only for dynamic entities tagged `CyberpunkConvaiCompanion` (Ready/Apply also required the tag). A crowd NPC's `face_rig` runs the vanilla `*_sermo` graph with no ConvaiFace input, so no Lua change alone could move it. The earlier "lips moved" resident turns (05:42-06:19 UTC, Convai cloud voice) went through the same gate; what moved then was most likely the vanilla look-at reaction or greeting line, which was suppressed from 06:13 (vanilla-reaction hooks).
- Fix (revised the same day for FPS; the first version, `cca9ec7`, prepared every world NPC at `Entity/Initialize` and never shipped): on demand only. `init.lua` calls `NPCPuppet.ConvaiFaceRequest()` for the talk target when a conversation opens. `ConvaiFaceService.RequestWorldFace` checks the running face without writing it, queues the NPC's current appearance name and calls `ScheduleAppearanceChange` with that same name. The existing `Entity/Extract` callback (one size check per build while nothing is pending) prepares that appearance-only rebuild. `ConvaiFaceReady` adopts a world NPC whose running `face_rig` carries our graph, and a dynamic entity still needs the companion tag. At most two world NPCs keep the layer. Lua `speaker()` gates speech playback and `owns()` (companions) gates the idle pass. Isolated redscript compile passes. Unproven in game: whether a same-name appearance change rebuilds `face_rig`, and whether the rebuild is visible.
- Tests: `continuous-speech.test.mjs` (ambient Pocket via `continuous-face.json`, idle pass, unprepared NPC), `facial-playback.test.mjs` (ambient Omni), `facial-graphs.test.mjs` (native contract); the two Lua tests fail on the old `facial-playback.lua`.

**2026-10-08: Judy left behind the building door and the lift after fast travel**
- Evidence (probe.log, movement trace):
  - Fast travel worked. Judy was re-acquired about 9 m from V and caught up.
  - V passed the automatic door. From 07:28:30Z her route was rejected (`navigation`) 25 m back while V waited in the lift car. She counted as stranded for 62 s with zero relocations and no log line: placement searched only around V, and V's car has no walkable navmesh.
  - After the 41 m ride she was teleported only after 12 s (the watched-copy timeout).
  - On the way down she was teleported 2 s after V stepped out.
- Live Codeware reflection:
  - The PlayerStateMachine `IsPlayerInsideElevator` and `IsPlayerInsideMovingElevator` Bools work.
  - `Door.whoOpened` is V for doors V walked through.
  - `DoorControllerPS:ActionToggleOpen` queued with `QueuePSDeviceEvent` opens the door; it auto-closes later.
  - `ActionDoorOpeningToken()` returns nil.
- Built `mod/companion-passage.lua` and the recovery rules in `companions.lua`:
  - open a closed, unlocked door V opened, then replan;
  - no relocation during a ride; rejoin at arrival; pull into a stopped car;
  - a 3 m floor-gap rule;
  - placement fallbacks from walkable ground near V's car and V's trail;
  - blocked-strand threshold 30 m -> 10 m;
  - fast-travel arrival window;
  - one probe.log line per decision.
- `companion-vehicles.lua` no longer keeps a copy owned forever after three failed fast-travel placements. That had disarmed all on-foot recovery.
- Tests: `bridge/companion-passage.test.mjs` (12 cases); motion and vehicles tests updated.

**2026-10-08: context emotion selector, neutral rest, Convai's emotion for local voices**
- Cause of Judy's smile on a sad Evelyn question: the Lua resting smile (Judy about .22) plus the text plan's resting Trust (.67 for warm characters) under every sentence; pleasant words (love, family, glad, thanks, beautiful, laughed, V) scored as smiles, so grief told through fond memories smiled harder; only the reply was read, never the question; regret and empathy phrases scored almost nothing. Two bugs: a segment starting with "V" crashed the planner (fallback = Trust scale-2 smile) and apostrophe phrases never matched.
- Convai: REST `getResponse` and the SSE stream API have no emotion; State of Mind is a Playground view (the SDK's `stateOfMind` is an input). The realtime `bot-emotion` (llm provider, one per turn, scale 1-3) DOES come on text-only turns: 43/43 live turns, 291-620 ms after `bot-llm-stopped`, after `reply()` returned (why October 5 saw none). On 43 real replies it is acceptable 90.7 % (blind labels); nrclex streams per chunk but reads grief as neutral.
- Built `bridge/emotion-selector.mjs` (player message + reply + previous mood + manner + Convai label + tags; frames, idioms, sarcasm, gates), Lua rest 0, late-emotion holder in `realtime-client.mjs` + `RealtimeHost.lateEmotion`, bridge wiring (no added latency), receipt v3, `emotionSelector: "legacy"` fallback. Labelled sets in `assets/emotion/` (dev 130, holdout 80, blind 90, real 43) and `scripts/Evaluate-EmotionSelector.mjs`.
- Results, first look: false smiles on negative replies 45/59, 25/36, 30/41, 13/16 before; 0/59, 2/36, 5/41, 0/16 after. Acceptable 44.6/45.0/38.9/58.1 % before; 93.8 (tuned)/80.0/74.4/72.1 % after; real with Convai's label 88.4 %. About 0.15 ms per reply. A local RoBERTa model was not adopted (download, 10-40 ms per sentence, no context). Not installed yet.

**2026-10-08: Cinematic subtitle vanished while Judy was still speaking**
- Cause: HUD pages were cut at 160 characters regardless of sentences and changed at that page's character share of a duration estimate, not at the audio. On a live Pocket Judy reply (179 chars, 13.1 s, pauses of 1.2/0.4/0.45/1.4 s) page one ended mid-sentence at "on your" and changed at 10.65 s (12.1 s on a slower stream), after that sentence (7.85-10.3 s) was over; replies whose early speech runs slower than its character share (long pauses up front) change early instead (the reported symptom). Also: captions removed at the last sample; Omni showed nothing between phrases; native `HideDialogLineByData` removes every ownerless HUD line (frequency in game unknown; now counted in `status().nativeRemovals`).
- Fix: `bridge/caption-cues.mjs` aligns sentence cues to the pauses of the continuous mouth timeline (errs late; unknown boundaries stay null while streaming) and publishes them in `continuous-speech.json`; `subtitles.lua` pages whole sentences and advances on the heard cursor; 0.6 s end hold / 3 s Omni phrase hold that never blocks the next speaker; native removal re-shows the page. Details: `docs/OVERHEAD_SUBTITLES.md`, October 8.
- Tests: 8 new in `bridge/subtitles.test.mjs`, including the real cue builder driving `speech.lua` + `subtitles.lua` through a slow stream and a stall (the old Lua fails it).
- To check in game: a long Judy reply (3+ sentences, over 160 characters) on Cinematic: each page appears as its first sentence starts and stays until the sentence ends; no sentence split across pages unless one sentence alone is longer than a page; the last page lingers briefly after the voice. Same on Above the speaker. Omni: no blank gap between phrases. Group chat: the next speaker's caption replaces the previous at once. `subtitles-state` diagnostic: `cueTimed` true during Pocket/Convai speech, `nativeRemovals`/`restores` counts.

**2026-10-08: Ordinary NPC first-reply latency**
- Report: a generic resident took ~14 s to first audio. `bridge-latency.json`: `memoryAdmissionMs` 6710, `connectionMs` 3909 cold, `firstAudioMs` 3132 (Convai voice).
- Cause 1: `admitPlayerConversation` ran `PlayerMemory.beginConversation` for ambient profiles too; the first turn per profile per bridge run called `enableMemory` (`/character/list` is 2.2 MB, ~2.6 s; `/character/get` ~1 s; update + readback when off). It also turned `resident-male`'s Convai memory on (now true; `resident-female` and `npc-*` still false). Fix: `providerMemory: 'session-only'` on ambient routes (`player-knowledge.mjs`) and `PlayerMemory.beginSessionConversation` (local Forget target only, no provider call).
- Cause 2: an ambient composer hint had no entity, so nothing warmed, and opening the composer again even closed the page the previous turn used (the second turn to the same resident reconnected, 3.7 s). Fix: the talk keys pass the entity (`conversation-prewarm.json` `entity`), Auto-connect publishes `entities` for warmed and partnered characters, and `PartyConnections` warms exact ambient routes (composer, nearby, partner) within the existing caps; the addressed body wins its profile's slot.
- Metrics: `submissionStages.memory` {mode, beginMs, refreshMs} and `connectionWarm` {state, role, samePage, startedMsAgo, warmMs}.
- Tests: player-memory (session path, vm admission), player-knowledge, party-connections (2), nearby-warm, group-prefetch, auto-connect (Lua publish + init.lua wiring). `npm test`: 2940 pass, 1 fail not ours (packaging allowlist lacks another agent's new `bridge/dialogue-level.mjs`).

**2026-10-08: Mod voices quieter than game dialogue**
- Measured (BS.1770): extracted game VO files median -23.6 LUFS; Omni slots -21.5 to -24.1, Convai -22.7 to -23.2, Pocket -24.3 to -26.4. Sources match; Wwise plays its files louder than our unity playback (browser centre pan -3 dB per ear, Audioware Kira emitter about -2.3 dB). Windows per-app volumes both 1.0.
- Audioware 1.9.9 (latest) has no append-PCM API; runtime WAVs play whole (on-demand). Moving Pocket/Convai to phrase files would add ~0.6-0.9 s first-word latency and 19-27 ms handoff gaps, and Omni on Audioware was just as quiet, so kept both renderers and matched the level instead. Details and the calibration tool: `docs/DIALOGUE_LEVEL.md`.

**2026-10-08: World companions (Follow V / Leave V's Party)**
- Mechanism: a managed copy stands in for the world character (same record, current appearance and orientation, placed at its spot), and the original is hidden with the runtime visibility event. Chosen over giving the live NPC a follower role because community and crowd NPCs stream out with their spawner, every companion code path proves `DynamicEntitySystem` ownership, and quest actors must keep their AI. Nothing persists: a load restores everything.
- Convai actions only, no phrase matching: `Follow V` for safe non-party world characters, `Leave V's Party` for any party member (summoned or adopted), and the first speaker of a group round gets per-member leave actions plus "everyone". Same token, TTL and lease validation as driving and tactics. A refused follow carries only a reason code, and the model declines in character.
- Timing: the copy appears after the accepting reply has been heard, and a leaving companion goes after its goodbye (group: after the round). Generic people join model-only and get their profile when attached. Named characters cannot be duplicated, and the party limit is 8. The partner role ends through `promote()`.
- Tests: `bridge/world-companions.test.mjs` (19) and two realtime-client tests. Full `npm test`: only the other agents' in-progress failures (archetype `buildArchetypes`, at the time of the run, and the multilingual twin map).

**2026-10-08: Multilingual voice rework; auto-connect prominence and conversation partner**
- Multilingual (user follow-up): turning it on switches Companion voices in the same staged Apply with a one-time notice: OmniVoice · Balanced (16 steps, short reference) with the Omni pack, an Omni tier kept, a cloud voice chosen in the same Apply kept; without the pack Convai · Cloud voice and Natural speech / Expressive emotions turned off (Multilingual wins). While on: Pocket is refused with a clear message (chosen over turning Multilingual off: no feature disappears as a side effect, the draft stays pending); turning a tag setting on without the pack is refused with the reason; with the pack a tag setting plus the cloud voice moves to Omni, never Pocket. Off keeps the voice. A saved Multilingual + Pocket file reads as the cloud voice.

The per-turn plan stays as the safety net: no tag instructions on a cloud-voice turn, the reply language to Omni, no non-English reply read by Pocket.

Prominence tie-breaker (tier-1 named, other named, generic) before distance within a priority; looking still wins. Conversation partner role (user revision of 'adopt into the slots'): outside the 2-slot cap, kept while together, released on 15 m for 20 s / 3 min idle / despawn / scene, 30 s grace for the previous partner, 2 at most, reuses the conversation's connection (same identity, verified by test), `promote(key)` hook for the party agent. Ambient residents cannot be warmed without their entity, so they are not kept.

**2026-10-08: Continue from game dialogue**
- New setting Settings > Conversations > Continue from game dialogue (On by default). `mod/game-dialogue.lua` observes `BaseSubtitlesGameController.ShowDialogLines` (plus the `OnShowDialogLine` blackboard event as a fallback), `PreventionSystem.OnPlayerChoice` and `InteractionUIBase.OnDialogsData`, keeps at most 40 lines / 180 s (10 ambient), and attaches a snapshot to F6, F7, F8 and Phone Chat sends (not automatic reactions). Excluded: TV, radio outside calls, narrator, the mod's own replies, braindance, Johnny flashbacks, combat barks. Cleared on load, death and detach.
- `bridge/game-dialogue.mjs` scopes per turn: episodes split by gaps, calls and new speakers; an episode is used only when the addressed character (or a group member) spoke in it, was the choice-hub character, or stood beside V as a companion; phone calls only for the caller; barks only from the addressed actor; 14 lines / 1,600 characters. Rendered after the journal block, labelled, "continue naturally, do not repeat", journal facts take priority.
- Memory decision: not written to memory (per-turn context only; the game story is already verified knowledge, and a misattributed or other-save line must not become permanent). The natural exchange still enters provider memory as any conversation does.
- Tests: `bridge/game-dialogue.test.mjs` (bridge scoping and text; Lua adapter with mocked native calls; group snapshot sharing; send-path wiring). Doc and in-game check list: `docs/GAME_DIALOGUE_CONTEXT.md`. Manifests list the new files. Not yet seen in game.

**2026-10-08: release decisions**
- Release: GitHub Actions builds everything (manual or tag trigger). Players install nothing (bundled Node, browser, Python, models, prebuilt client). Split like the Dawnwalker mod: a Scripts package (text only, for Nexus) and a Runtime package plus an optional Omni pack (GitHub). Player files only, no dev files. Models come from the HF `final/` folder at build time using an HF token secret. The Convai key comes from a build secret. The user sets the secrets themselves. Publishing goes through a public releases-only route (to be approved). Agent working.
- Lip-sync game-word lexicon (espeak-generated): keep it for the release (user).
- Multilingual twins: give them varied Azure multilingual voices matched to each character (agent working). The setting stays off by default; with it on, the cloud voice using Azure voices is fine.


**2026-10-07 (later): lip-sync research finished, Candidate A installed, text stage built, report / devslot / HF refreshed**
- **Candidate A installed** (user-approved): `face-model.{json,bin}` in `runtime/lipsync-model/` are now `candidate-aux-e96-20261007` (trained from scratch on all non-held-out lines, 5,521 lines, with a training-only phone head, 96 epochs, about 14 min on the 4080; same network, size and CPU cost; NumPy source `candidate-aux-e96-20261007.npz`). Backup of the previous model: `E:\CyberpunkConvai-offload\backups\lipsync-face-model-20261007-162230`. Held-out speakers (1,024 lines, 31 speakers), paired 95% CIs excluding zero (verified from `final_vs_e96.json`): aperture r 0.823 -> 0.843 (+0.020 [+0.014, +0.025]), width 0.499 -> 0.535, contact r 0.750 -> 0.794, purse 0.638 -> 0.704 (+0.066), F/V recall 0.709 -> 0.746, rounding recall 0.671 -> 0.714, closure precision 0.394 -> 0.448; closure recall unchanged (inside noise). On cloned voices it improves by about as much, but closes slightly less often (recall -0.02, precision +0.04). JS parity 5e-6.
- **Text stage built** (`f3f2b7c`, `bridge/jali-face-text.mjs`, mirrors `scripts/lipsync/lab_textpipe.py`): the model's own phone head plus a Viterbi over the reply's phones, then a small stage 2 (two 64-channel nets, stream and phrase). Active after the next bridge restart; fallback `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_FACE_TEXT=off` (or delete `face-text.*`); it also falls back by itself (no text, tags, unknown words, low confidence, worker behind real time, wrong face-model). Gains over Candidate A (verified from `text_final.json`): streaming (Pocket, Convai) contact r +0.012, purse +0.035, F/V +0.022, rounding recall +0.037, width +0.023; whole phrase (Omni) contact r +0.063, purse +0.078, F/V +0.077, rounding recall +0.065, width +0.056. Cost: CPU +16% to +24% (about 0.30-0.38 s per audio second in game, at the 0.35 budget). Streaming has one cost: closure recall -0.007 (fewer, more precise closures).
- **Findings (docs/LIPSYNC_IMPROVEMENTS_2026-10-07.md, numbers re-checked against the eval JSONs):** per-character fine-tunes give no reliable gain (per-game models are the default; per-character stays optional), cloned-voice training no gain, distillation from an 8x teacher no gain, acceleration loss and 260 ms lookahead no gain. The "ee width" weakness was mostly an artifact (the game's width came from expression controls, not the 15 runtime outputs). The previous model's held-out scores were slightly optimistic (17 of 31 held-out speakers were in an ancestor model's training). Data decision: lip sync keeps shouted and processed lines (24% of lines; clean-only training was worse even on clean lines); voice excludes them.
- **Report, devslot, HF:** `Downloads/Cyberpunk Lipsync Report.html` updated (backup `E:\CyberpunkConvai-offload\backups\Cyberpunk Lipsync Report.before-research-20261007.html`); `Downloads/devslot handoff 2026-10-07 lipsync service/` updated (README, proposal section 0 with the six service recommendations, technical notes section 10, the `lab_*` scripts, `jali-face-text.mjs` and the updated JS; no weights, corpus, audio, game text or game-word list; backup `E:\CyberpunkConvai-offload\backups\devslot handoff 2026-10-07 lipsync service.before-research-20261007`). HF bucket `final/lipsync-model/` now holds Candidate A, its NumPy source, `face-text.*`, `face-lexicon.txt.gz` and the notice, with the previous model (and its `final.npz`/`final.log`) under `previous-20261005/`; `final/MANIFEST.json` and `final/README.md` updated and checked by re-listing and re-downloading.

**2026-10-07: Convai early audio-player open (latency item A)**
- Where the time went (real Edge, real `realtime-client`, player, worklet, host pump and `ConvaiContinuousSpeech`; fake Convai SDK emitting 24 kHz audio at 2.87x with 100 ms chunks; simulated game finding the reply within 0 to 100 ms like `mod/init.lua`'s 0.1 s poll, then refreshing control every 40 ms; harness is scratch, not committed): the player was created at the first audio packet, and a fresh AudioContext+worklet only starts rendering about 120 to 230 ms after `open()` returns (about 20 ms of `open()` on a warm page, about 190 ms on a page's first context). Control to sound was 120 to 230 ms right after open, but about 30 ms when the context had been running for 300 ms or more. The 400 ms start-up buffer fills in about 105 ms at 2.87x, so it is not the bottleneck.
- Why the old note was too pessimistic: the bridge pump already sends a quiet control every 40 ms while the request is in flight, but a player opened early still must not depend on that (a bridge stall of 2 s would fail a turn that today is harmless), so the early player is not leased at all.
- Design: `ContinuousPcmPlayer.open({standby: true})` opens the device at turn start (`attachContinuous`, so text turns and the microphone finish) in the expected 24 kHz mono. A standby core renders silence, ignores controls and `hold`, rejects audio, and has no lease; it is bounded only by a 70 s ceiling (`PCM_LIMITS.standbyMs`, above the 60 s reply ceiling). `arm()` ends standby and starts the ordinary 2 s lease at that moment (it admits nothing; the game's allowing control still does). The client keeps the standby player separate from `live.player`, so a failed or unusable standby (open error, device change, a source format other than 24 kHz mono) just falls back to opening a player at the first audio as before. Cancel (`close`), a failed text send, a failed microphone finish and the 60 s no-audio timeout all stop it. Game ownership, the lease after the first audio and the Lua side are unchanged.
- Measured (3 interleaved before/after runs, think time 2.3 s, medians of per-run medians, first packet to first rendered samples): warm page 302 to 141 ms, first turn in a page 274 to 135 ms (to device-clock position above zero: 329 to 162 and 306 to 158). Control to sound fell from 120-230 ms to 35-65 ms. A 6 s think time also plays. Numbers move with machine load; the direction held in every run.
- Tests: core standby and arm (`continuous-pcm-core.test.mjs`), player standby/arm/ceiling (`continuous-pcm-player.test.mjs`), a real-Edge standby that outlives 2.6 s then plays and still expires 2 s after arming (`continuous-pcm-browser.test.mjs`), and client tests for promotion, format mismatch, standby open failure, cancel before audio, 60 s no-audio timeout, failed send and microphone finish (`realtime-client.test.mjs`). `npm run test:streaming`: 391 pass.
- To use it: `npm run build:voice` (rebuilds `runtime/realtime-client.js`) and restart the bridge; the player, core and worklet modules are served from `bridge/` on start. No CET reload, no game restart. Check in game that a first reply after a long pause starts sooner and nothing speaks twice.

**2026-10-08: Natural speech tuned against the real characters**
- The user heard no difference on Pocket or Omni 32. Built `scripts/Probe-NaturalSpeech.mjs` (same context assembly as the bridge, real Convai, throwaway identity, 20 varied prompts, one session per character) and measured about 400 requests: the block does reach the model and Omni over-used it (Judy 85%, Johnny 60%, half as the first word), while the Pocket wording suppressed even natural pauses (5% against 15% with no instructions); the last Apply in the settings file left one Omni turn.
- Fix: the bridge offers the devices on a share of turns by trait (warm .36, neutral .30, dry .22, cold .08; never right after a device; one decision per request) for both engines, with rewritten texts (at most one, where the feeling lands, not a habit to open with). Result in 8 to 12 replies per cell: expressive characters 25 to 33%, Johnny 8 to 17%, archetypes 0 to 13%, no consecutive repeats. New `[NaturalSpeech]` log lines per reply. Full table in `docs/NATURAL_SPEECH_TEST_2026-10-06.md`.

**2026-10-07: Expressive emotions setting**
- Second setting from the user, off by default. `bridge/expressive-emotions.mjs` holds the tag set (mapped onto the eight emotion families, calm = resting face; subtle .45, `:strong` .8), the per-turn context text and the setting; `bridge/natural-speech.mjs` parses both tag kinds (brackets = voice sounds, braces = feelings) so the display text, Omni's voice text and the marks (`emotionMarks`, character offsets in the clean text) come from one pass. `analyseReply`/`createPlanner` take `marks`: each mark is a forced segment boundary and its feeling holds to the next mark; text before the first mark keeps the text reading, no marks keeps the old plan exactly.
- Timing: no new aligner. The plan's segments now begin at the marks and `expression-timeline.mjs` places them in the audio as it already did (Omni phrase edges exact, then pauses in the mouth timeline, then syllable-proportional estimate), so the face changes when the words are spoken. Mid-sentence marks without a pause are an estimate (about 0.2 s), sentence and phrase starts are anchored to the real silence. Cloud voice switch, locales and tests as for Natural speech (`bridge/expressive-emotions.test.mjs`, three Lua tests, two real-`synthesizeReply` tests).

**2026-10-07: Natural speech setting**
- Built the setting the user decided on: off by default, in Voices. `bridge/natural-speech.mjs` holds the per-engine context text, the tag handling and the small per-conversation memory of the last reply's devices; `convai-bridge.mjs` appends the block last in `conversationContext` for spoken turns only (not phone chat, not battle/loot reactions, not the Convai cloud voice). The model chooses; the bridge never inserts a device.
- Display text never carries a tag: `RealtimeHost` takes a `transformReply` (conversation and phone hosts) that keeps the tagged original as `speechText`, and everything downstream (subtitles, `reply.json`, stream manifest, memory activity, look-ahead source, phone chat) reads the clean `text`. Omni synthesis re-attaches at most two supported tags per reply, phrase by phrase (`speechPhrases`, `SpeechStream` `speech`). Safety nets: `VoiceSelection.synthesizeStream` (Pocket) strips every tag, `synthesize` (Omni) keeps only the seven English tags, and `mod/subtitles.lua` `split` and the Convai continuous snapshot text strip any that slipped through.
- Staged Apply: `manager.applySettings` judges the settings as they will be after the batch, so natural speech on + cloud voice (in either order, or both in one Apply) ends on Pocket, with the notice "Natural speech needs a local voice, so Companion voices was switched to {voice}." on the MCM status line and once as a gameplay popup; a saved on + cloud pairing is repaired at load. Nine locales and `localization/en.json` updated. Tests: `bridge/natural-speech.test.mjs` (16) and three in `bridge/manager.test.mjs`.

A very short single clip sounds bad. The duration-based redo (`search.py`, greedy beam, Omni caps 10/18 s, Pocket up to 40 s; v3 page builder and collector, `8869dd2`) ran for all 29 voices overnight (see that entry); the earlier search had tied caps to clip counts and Adam's Pocket kept improving up to 5 clips.

- Voice research phase 1 and 2, condensed (write-ups `docs/VOICE_LIKENESS_2026-10-05.md`, `docs/REFERENCE_DURATION_SEARCH_2026-10-07.md`; audio in `E:\CyberpunkConvai-offload\work\likeness-phase1`, matrix page `page/index.html`, `page/summary-table.md`): Pocket's likeness ceiling was its Mimi codec (a round trip of real lines loses 7.1 dB above 8 kHz, ECAPA 0.53, against OmniVoice generating from scratch at 3.7 dB, 0.62), which the decoder work partly addressed. A perceptual score (PLS: full-band MFCC Frechet distance + same-line distance + error above 8 kHz; base 60, Omni 90) matches the user's ear; ECAPA is the guard; WavLM-SV is saturated; UTMOS/DNSMOS are unusable. Omni 16 steps matches 32 on every metric at half the GPU time; no newer Pocket release exists. Multi-clip references are the biggest cheap win (Omni 32 with three <=18 s clips PLS 103 vs single 90, matching real lines at 104; Pocket student three-clip 83, up from 69); loudness-matched sets are worse (92-97) and the automatic selector ties "most typical" (99.2), so the picker and later the search replaced them. Multi-clip latency (`scripts/Measure-MultirefLatency.mjs`): Pocket has no per-turn cost (one-time prepare 0.5 -> 1.4 s per voice); Omni first audio at 16 steps goes 1.17 -> 1.62 s with three clips, and a <=10 s cap keeps it within about 7%; three-clip phrases overflow the CUDA graph's 640-token ceiling (eager fallback in 30 of 72). OmniVoice LoRA (four attempts, 3,000 steps): no gain with a single reference (PLS 89.8 vs 90.0) and worse with three (97.8 vs 103.4); likely cause is the upstream recipe training on same-utterance prompts; no longer run. Kaggle Mimi-decoder runs died silently twice; the decoder trained locally. The deletable leftovers (merged LoRA models, checkpoints, about 8 GB) were deleted 2026-10-06.
- OmniVoice precision test (2026-10-06): no lower-precision variant beats FP16 with the CUDA graph (BF16 same speed at best; INT8 and FP8 12%-2x slower); quality unchanged within noise except INT8-on-FP16, which is broken. Only gain: 0.3-0.5 GB less VRAM. Decision: keep FP16 (`scripts/Measure-OmniPrecision.mjs`, `scripts/Score-OmniPrecision.py`, WAVs in `runtime/effect-examples/omni-precision/`).
- Found-during-test items that are done: invite notices merged (`M.newBoard`, see the entries below), the Settings global Apply, the Esc/Back/status-line fix (`9e5f286`), and the old install-and-test checklist's voice-choice step (replaced by the six-entry menu checks in Open items). The old four-voice order check is obsolete.
