- 2026-10-07 (combat flags): the game itself separates combat from conversation, so the flag is source-first. Findings: every line is played by a scene (`.scene`); all 7,580 scenes were extracted (`E:/CyberpunkConvai-offload/work/combat-flags/scene-lines.jsonl`, 208,827 dialog events, 82,084 string ids, 100% of the dataset rows join to their own scene). Voiceset scenes (`vset_<name>.scene`, the gameplay barks) carry per-line bark triggers (`battlecry_*`, `danger`, `grenade`, `fear_*`, `smasher_taunt` ...) and `Vo_Context_Combat`, but that context is a blanket stamp on the whole voiceset (greetings, phone, stealth too), so the trigger name is the real cue. Quest scenes keep combat dialogue as `Vo_Context_Quest`; the only line-level cues are entry-point names (`*_bossfight_in`, `*_low_health_in`) and scene names (`q110_08c_bossfight`, `q108_18b_combat`). Adam: `vset_smasher` and `q113_13_adam_smasher` (boss fight) flag, `q116_03b_after_bossfight`, the q112/sq031 phone calls and q101 memories do not. Signals and coverage: game metadata is available for 99.5% of clips (strong 517, moderate 1,648, weak 2,016, social barks 480, plain dialogue 20,524, no scene 125); strong evidence alone flags 517 clips; moderate or weak evidence confirmed by acoustics 523; acoustics alone 539 (shouted lines that sit in ordinary scenes, e.g. Claire's car chase, Takemura's "V, you must hurry!"); 143 more are phone-processed `.holocall` variants that inherit the plain recording's verdict. Acoustic model: logistic regression on within-voice robust z-scores (loudness, short-term p90, pitch median/p90/range, alpha ratio, spectral tilt, brightness, HNR, rate) trained on the game's own labels (strong source vs ordinary quest dialogue), cross-validated by voice, AUC about 0.78, threshold at 2% of ordinary dialogue; ECAPA distance from the voice's conversational centroid is a small fixed-weight extra; transcript cues add a little; duration is only a tie-break. Mr. Hands and Songbird are two-performer voices (ECAPA 2-means centroid cosine 0.02 and 0.50) and get one baseline per performer; without that 16% of Mr. Hands was falsely flagged. Duration is not needed as a prior (flagged share: under 2.5 s 14%, 2.5-4 s 9%, 4-6.5 s 4%, over 6.5 s 2%); no new length minimum was added (references 1.5-9 s, targets 2.5-6.5 s and 5 words, Adam's picks last 1.9-4.8 s). Of the current autobest sets only Hanako's Pocket best and short sets contain a flagged clip. Tests: `python -B scripts/combat_flags/test_source_signal.py`, picker and clip-search suites pass. Listening examples: `runtime/effect-examples/combat-check/` (7 flagged, 7 normal; Adam, Johnny, Panam).

**2026-10-06 (expressions): why local voices had no visible face, and the fix**
- Root cause: the wiring was fine (rest pose written about 24,000 times; Omni timelines carry `jali-audio-v1` plus `modelExpressions`), but (1) the text cue needed two lexicon hits, so Judy's reply produced no receipt, (2) the resting smile was .025 and idle-only, and (3) the caps were calibrated against a misread game number. The game's lipsync holds a constant per-line mood offset (smile .10 + brows raise .10 in 39% of 6938 lines, frown .20 + brows lower .324 in 20%); our caps and scaling were at or below it.
- Fix: strengths .5/.8/1, caps up/down .20, brow raise .25, lower .32, speech keeps 85%, rest smile .06 in idle and speech, model brow gain .65, and `text-emotion.mjs` now always returns a cue (lexicon, tone, then character warmth; cold characters flat). New per-turn receipts: `runtime/expression-receipt.json` and `speech-state.json` face.expressions.lastSpeech (peak pose). Details in `docs/COMPANION_EXPRESSIONS.md`. Install: `mod/facial-playback.lua` (CET reload) and a bridge restart. Not seen in game yet; if too strong or weak only the numbers change.

Renders appear as Your picks columns in the row. Pocket renders on the CPU any time; OmniVoice renders wait while another process holds most of the GPU (nothing GPU-side was started while building it).

Live configs are never written. Docs: `docs/LOCAL_VOICES.md`. Waiting on the user's listening; then apply and decide the live switch.

Throughput: staging ~19 min, latent preparation ~30 min, 1.8–2.1 s/update, ~44 min per 1,000 updates including evaluation and a 5 GB checkpoint upload. It **overfit**: held-out best at update 1,000 raw (0.3305), worse every checkpoint after; speech WER rose from 1.2% to 5.6%. It was stopped at 6,000 and update 1,000 selected. Session fixes: a 30-minute end-of-session save reserve, the 10-minute 5 GB local saves removed, and a resume launcher (`Pocket-FullATeacher.py resume`). Then the student run above, launched with held-out stopping.

Held-out results against the game's curves: lip opening r 0.41/0.51 → 0.82, closures 12%/38% → 55%, F/V 0% → 71%, rounding 29%/43% → 68%, lag 67 ms → 0. It streams identically to whole-utterance output, poses lag 218 ms, and it costs 0.25–0.32 s CPU per second of audio under load. A bigger model was rejected (diminishing returns, too much CPU). Expressions: Convai's id-less emotion is now admitted by channel order (it had never been accepted before); local voices get a conservative cue from the reply text; light brow motion follows the voice; ranges follow the game. Weakness: "ee" width is under-articulated. Not tested in game.

## Standing facts

- Never let a test, harness or script play sound on the user's machine (a hidden voice-page browser caused a headphone hum on 2026-10-12). `RealtimeHost` mutes itself under `node --test`. Any other script that starts it must set `CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_MUTE_BROWSER=1` or pass `muteBrowser: true`.

- The mod never touches game-placed characters except the talk path for ordinary NPCs (2026-10-11, `docs/WORLD_COMPANIONS.md`): our spawned companions get the full feature set; ordinary pedestrians, vendors and gangers get only a conversation the player starts; named, story and quest characters get nothing. One predicate: `convaiWorldPolicy` (`mod/init.lua`) over `characterCatalog.protectedReason`.
- Final models are backed up to the private HF bucket `akshitireddy/cyberpunk-pocket-rolling-checkpoints` under `final/` (2026-10-07; see `final/README.md` there). Code is backed up to the private GitHub repo `alystria-ai/cyberpunk-convai`.

**Decisions that stand**
- Voice condition A. No per-character models; one shared model plus a reference per voice, chosen per character by duration (no in-game clip or length setting). Omni stays FP16 (precision test).
- Preserve source audio: no added EQ, pitch, denoise or crossfades.
- Convai speech is one continuous stream; Pocket gets the full reply text once; Omni is phrase-scheduled.
- Shortcuts are the player's own CET bindings. Settings use MCM's own staged Apply (Auto Apply is refused for this provider so drafts stay pending). Esc from the manager returns to gameplay; with unapplied changes the game's OnBack is held and MCM asks first.
- No automatic companion respawn on save load, death, restart or CET reload; fast travel is the exception.
- Lip sync (2026-10-07): per-game model, no per-character models (no reliable gain); trains on ALL lines (shouted and processed lines keep valid curves; voice cloning excludes them); the dialogue text sharpens the mouth when known, with automatic fallback to the audio-only model (`CYBERPUNK_CONVAI_FACE_TEXT=off`).
- Follow-ups are free (no forced questions). Group: the addressed character speaks first; duplicate copies don't multiply speakers.

**Working with the user**
- Ask with a multiple-choice question whenever the user needs to act or decide.
- For tests: launch the game myself (PowerShell `Start-Process` on `bin\x64\Cyberpunk2077.exe`), then give exact steps and observation options, then read the trace.
- Lua changes need CET → Reload All Mods; redscript or plugin changes need a game restart.
- Install by copying only the intended files (from git when other agents have uncommitted work), and compare afterwards.
- Subagents: Sonnet by default, Opus only for genuinely open-ended work. Tell them the same rule for their own helpers.
- Commits: local, focused, no AI attribution, never `git add .`.
